# IMPL-01_scaffold_configs_schemas_prompts

# SPEC 01 — Project Scaffold, Configs, Schemas, Prompts

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: none. This spec creates the repo skeleton and all static configuration that later modules read.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §0a (schema contract), §0b (LoB), §6 (modality instructions), §7 (dataset format, schema registry, prompt template), §9/§9a (LoRA targets), §11 (full hyperparameter spec), §11a (sweeps), §13a/§13b (quantization), §19 (repo structure).

## Goal

Scaffold the `insurance-extraction-finetuning` repo and create every config file, JSON schema, and prompt template that the rest of the system depends on.

## Deliverables

### 1. Repo root
- `README.md` — project overview, setup steps, how to run each pipeline stage (stub with section headings + the module map from master §5). State prominently that this repo implements the **L3 VLM layer** of the Fideon pipeline and that its output schema is owned by **Fideon SPEC_00** (master §1.1).
- `pyproject.toml` — Python 3.11+. **The only place versions are declared**, in dependency groups: `[data]`, `[ocr]` (MinerU 3.4, pipeline backend), `[train]`, `[serve]`, `[quantize]`, `[eval]`, `[track]`, `[dev]`. See *Current implementation* below for the pinned versions and why each group is separate.
- `requirements.txt` and `requirements-{ocr,train,serve,quantize}.txt` — one per machine, each only `-e .[groups]`: they choose groups, never versions. Installed on a pod by `scripts/setup_pod.sh <role>`, constrained by `requirements-{ocr,train,serve,quantize}.lock`.
- `.env.example` — every required env var with placeholder: `AZURE_STORAGE_CONNECTION_STRING`, `AZURE_BLOB_CONTAINER`, `AZURE_RAW_CONTAINER` (separately-permissioned raw container: raw documents, intake batches, exports — master §4, §8), `RUNPOD_API_KEY`, `RUNPOD_ENDPOINT_ID`, `RUNPOD_VOLUME_ID`, `RUNPOD_VOLUME_MOUNT` (default `/runpod-volume`, the staging volume — master §12a), `HF_TOKEN`, `HF_MODEL_REVISION`, `HF_HOME`, `HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER`, `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` / `WANDB_API_KEY` (optional), `DEFAULT_TENANT_ID` (the default tenant), `ALLOW_EXTERNAL_PREANNOTATION` (default `false`) and `EXTERNAL_PREANNOTATION_ENDPOINT`, `FIDEON_BASE_MODEL_DIR` (overrides the local base-model directory), `FIDEON_ALLOW_OFF_POD` (train on a non-RunPod GPU host), `FIDEON_NO_DETACH` (run one command in the foreground on the pod), `FIDEON_INTAKE_DIR`, `FIDEON_WORKSPACE`, and MinerU's `MINERU_TOOLS_CONFIG_JSON` (its `mineru.json`), `MINERU_DEVICE_MODE` (cuda) and `MINERU_MODEL_SOURCE` (local: the pinned weights). Comment each. `tests/test_repo_contracts.py` fails when the code reads a variable the template does not list, or the template lists one nothing reads (library-owned and runtime-set names excepted).
- `.gitignore` — Python caches, `.env`, model weights (`*.safetensors`, `*.gguf`, `*.bin`, `*.ckpt`), `*.pdf` (except under `tests/fixtures/`), `testing/{results,metrics,ocr_cache,test_data}/`, `calibration/calibration_store/`, `pilot/reports/`, the client data folders under `data/` (deliveries, bundles, CGL source data, audit reports, exclusion and override CSVs), `configs/field_types.proposed.yaml`, and `Documentation/`.

### 2. `configs/`
- **`base_model.yaml`** — `model_id: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct`, `revision: <pin>`, **`local_dir: /workspace/models`** (where the pod keeps the weights; every loader reads them from there), quantization block (**`load_in_4bit: false` by default — the base is held in bf16**; the `bnb_4bit_*` keys stay populated so flipping the flag on a VRAM-constrained pod needs no other edit, and are read only when it is true, per arch §9.2), `attn_implementation: flash_attention_2`, resolution cap (`max_image_long_side_px: 1792`, valid range 1536–2048 per arch §11), `train_vit: false`, `max_seq_len` (comment: **set from the 95th-percentile token count measured on the real corpus**, not guessed; the per-task caps that training and serving actually use are in `configs/shared/sequence.yaml`).
- **`configs/shared/vision.yaml`** (per-task `max_pixels`/`min_pixels`, whole 32×32 visual tokens) and **`configs/shared/sequence.yaml`** (per-task `max_seq_len` and reserved `max_output_tokens`: `policy_schedule` 12,288, `lossrun_rows` 8,192, `policy_declarations` 4,096 …) — one file each, read by dataset build, training and serving.
- **`configs/training/unified.yaml`** (was `foundation.yaml` under v1's two-level design) — the full parameter set from arch §11, at the values the code reads now:
  - LoRA: rank 64, alpha 128, dropout 0.10 (0.05 at ≥200 documents per type), `bias: none`, the seven decoder projections of master §2; `include_vision_projector: false` (mergers not targeted); `use_rslora: false` (on for the rank sweep only).
  - Optimization: LR `1e-4`, cosine schedule, `warmup_steps: 10` (steps, not a ratio, so pilot volume still warms up), 3 epochs (document passes), `optim: adamw_torch`, β₁ 0.9, β₂ 0.999, ε 1e-8, weight decay 0.0, max grad norm 1.0.
  - Batch/memory: per-device train batch 1, grad accumulation 8, **effective batch 8** at pilot volume (kept when training data-parallel on several GPUs: accumulation shrinks), gradient checkpointing on, bf16, `dataset_num_proc: 8`; `use_logits_to_keep`, `padding_free`, and `length_grouped_sampling: false` (with one row per device and `padding_free` there is no padding to save, and on it made ms-swift encode every row before step 1).
  - Vision: `train_vit: false` (the §3 gate default), resolution cap inherited from `base_model.yaml`.
  - Eval/checkpointing: eval and save every 50 steps, `save_total_limit: 4` (the candidates checkpoint selection scores), a resume point every 70 steps (`checkpoint_every_steps`), `load_best_model_at_end: false`; validation loss tracked (`metric_for_best_model: eval_loss`, `greater_is_better: false`) for the progress curve only — `early_stopping_patience` is in the file but not passed, since ms-swift 3 takes none. The validation cut: `evaluations_per_run: 10`, each in-training loss check on `loss_sample_rows: 160`; checkpoint selection on `validation_sample_rows: 400` in all modes, then `selection_document_finalists: 2` judged on whole validation documents in `selection_document_modes: [ocr_plus_image]` with `selection_guard_margin: 0.02`, and a near-tie (`selection_tie_break_margin: 0.015`) re-scored. What ships is chosen by generated accuracy (IMPL-06 §3), never by loss. Fixed recorded `seed: 42`, `logging_steps: 10`.
  - Corpus view: `line_balance` (`min_documents: 100`, `max_repeat: 3`) and `render_rotation: true` (one render per twin per epoch), applied in line-scoped views.
  - Distributed: `deepspeed_config: null`, `sequence_parallel_size: 1`, `gpus: auto` (data-parallel on every GPU the pod has).
  - Top comment: "hyperparameters are sweep starting points (arch §11/§11a) — tune on the validation set, not fixed truth."
- **`configs/training/personal_lines.yaml`** — `extends: unified` (merged over it by `common.config.training_config`) and adds the `personal_lines` scope's `data_mix`: scope-level and per-line `synthetic_fraction` and `scanned_share`, all `null` (unsteered) until set; a scope may also set its own `modality_mix`. `casualty_fleet` uses `unified` as is.
- **`configs/scopes.yaml`** — what one training run covers: `unified`, `policy`, `lossrun`, `personal_lines` (seven personal lines; not flood) and `casualty_fleet` (`lines: [gl]`, the CGL documents). Each declares `lineage`, `doc_types`, `lines`, `tasks`, `training_config`, `long_doc_types` and `not_applicable_metrics` (checked against what the scope's shape implies, `common.scopes`).
- **`configs/layout_families.yaml`** — the one LOB → layout-family table (`personal_lines`, `casualty_fleet`, `property_pkg`, `exec_specialty`, `benefits_misc`, `no_family`), every canonical line in exactly one family; read by training and serving, and checked against the delivered `lob_schema_map.yaml`.
- **`configs/schema_sections.yaml`** — the policy section groups, page rules and identifying keys the window planner reads in training and serving; a default map (decl, arrays, lineblk, dtd) for self-contained lines and a `common_model` profile (decl, arrays, lineblk; each table's id field and prefix, reference fields, `array_keys`, unit keys, `run_overlap_pages: 1`); `pages.declarations_leading: 5` for every line.
- **`configs/model_view.yaml`** — repo-owned replacements for common-model descriptions written for schema authors, and how coverage codes are offered (`oneof` or `enum`); part of the prompt.
- **`configs/lob_meanings.yaml`** — one role-based sentence per registered line, for the line classifier (no carrier wording).
- **`configs/label_mappings.yaml`** — labels written for one line's schema used under another's (applied when targets are built and labels scored, never to stored labels).
- **`configs/coverage_code_recodes.yaml`** — coverage codes re-coded when bundles are prepared; **`configs/coverage_code_meanings.yaml`** — a template of neutral meanings per coverage code, to be filled in; not yet applied to the model view or the prompt.
- **`configs/canonical schema/`** — the client's schemas, read-only (§3b).
- **Not created under v2.1:** `configs/training/{acord,lossrun,policy}_adapter.yaml`. There is one adapter per scope and no per-type adapter (arch §4.1); a per-type config arrives only with a type that passes the §4.2 graduation gate. The original v1 description, for reference — LoRA rank 16 / alpha 32 / dropout 0.05, LR `7e-5` (range 5e-5–1e-4), epochs 4 (range 3–5), **effective batch 16–32**; each references its `doc_type`, its schema file, and `foundation_version: <set at runtime>`.
  - **One shared `acord` adapter only** (arch §4b Option 1). Per-form adapter configs (`acord25`/`acord125`/`acord140`) are **not created** — with limited data per form, one adapter learning shared "ACORD-ness" generalizes better than several data-starved ones. Add them only when a form has 1000+ examples *and* eval shows the shared adapter underperforming. The per-form **schemas** still exist, because the classifier must select the right schema regardless.
- **`configs/sweeps/`** *(arch §11a — **author the configs now, run them after the IMPL-15 pilot**)* — the bounded 3-phase sweep definitions, as W&B Sweep / MLflow configs. A 9–12 run sweep against 25–30 docs/type mostly measures noise; arch §11a scopes it to "before the first **production** run", not before the pilot:
  - `phase1_lr.yaml` — Foundation `{5e-5, 1e-4, 2e-4}`, per-type `{2e-5, 5e-5, 1e-4}`; 1 epoch per candidate; metric = validation loss; 3 runs per adapter type.
  - `phase2_epochs.yaml` — Foundation `{2, 3, 4}`, per-type `{3, 4, 5}` with early stopping patience 2; metric = validation field-level F1; 3 runs per adapter type.
  - `phase3_rank.yaml` — Foundation rank `{32, 64, 128}`; **secondary, not run by default** — only if F1 plateaus.
  - Comment the total budget: **~9–12 training runs before the first production run**, and that **every sweep run writes a full run manifest** (IMPL-02) so sweeps are first-class registry entries, not untracked side experiments.
- **`configs/deepspeed/zero2.json`** and **`zero3.json`** — ZeRO-2 default, ZeRO-3 for VRAM-constrained multi-GPU.
- **`configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml`** — resolution cap (**must match `base_model.yaml`**, `common.config.assert_resolution_parity`), `max_seq_len: 32768` (at least the largest per-task cap); `generation`: greedy (`temperature: 0.0`), `max_new_tokens: 12288` (every task's full reservation), `logprobs: true` with `logprobs_mode: raw_logprobs`, `structured_outputs: true`, `repeatable: false`, and `validation_batch_rows: 96` (rows per vLLM call when a whole split is generated, longest expected answer first); `serving`: backend, served model tag (resolved from the registry), `enable_lora`, `max_loras`, `max_lora_rank`; `routing`: `classifier_confidence_threshold: 0.70`, `detect_lob: false` with `lob_confidence_threshold: 0.90` and `lob_family_threshold: 0.60`, `release_pins: {}`; **review confidence threshold (`confidence.review_threshold`, default 0.7, arch §5)**. Adapter paths are **not** listed here — they are resolved through the registry and the promoted releases. The long-document page threshold is **not** set here either: it is `common.constants.DEFAULT_LONG_DOC_PAGE_THRESHOLD` (5), the value the corpus build planned its training windows with, so serving cannot drift from training.

### 3. `schemas/` — JSON Schemas (Draft 2020-12)

**These are not invented schemas — they are the Fideon SPEC_00 Pydantic models serialised to JSON Schema** (master §1.1). Generate them from the Fideon models where available; hand-authored versions are a temporary stand-in and must be reconciled before the first real corpus build.

- `acord25.schema.json` — Certificate of Liability: insured, insurer(s), policy numbers, effective/expiration dates, coverage limits (GL, auto, umbrella), certificate holder.
- `acord125.schema.json` — Commercial Application: applicant, business info, premises, coverages requested.
- `acord140.schema.json` — Property section (per arch §4b two-level ACORD classification).
- `lossrun.schema.json` — carrier, policy number, valuation date, and a **`claims` array** (repeating rows: claim number, loss date, status, paid, reserved, total incurred, description). This is the key list-field type and the one where a **missed row** is both easy and expensive (arch §5).
- `policy_doc.schema.json` — the legacy flat policy schema (named insured, policy number, effective/expiration, coverage schedule, premium, endorsements). Kept with its example, but **no policy is extracted into it**: the registry key `policy` resolves to the client's canonical `_fallback.json` (§3b).
- `common_fields.json` — shared definitions `$ref`'d by the flat schemas.

**Every flat schema must additionally include:**
- **`line_of_business`** (arch v2.1 §0b) — a **list** of enum values (`$ref: lob.enum.json#/$defs/line_of_business`, `uniqueItems`, default `[]`), required in every flat schema; an empty list means the document determines no line. The enum lives once, in `schemas/lob.enum.json` (12 values: five commercial lines and seven personal lines), and lines outside it go to the list `line_of_business_other`. Canonical policy schemas carry no `line_of_business`: a policy's line comes with the request (master §1.3).
- **A `description` on every field** — see the semantic-gloss rules below (master §1.4). This is not documentation; it is injected into the prompt and is part of what the model is trained on.
- Explicit `null` unions for optional fields — "absent" must be representable as `null`, never as a missing key or a hallucinated value.
- Date fields as `string` with an `MM/DD/YYYY` format note — the one output date format (`common.normalize.OUTPUT_DATE_LABEL`), applied to every output by `common.canonical.with_output_dates`.
- `required` vs optional marked deliberately.

**Field descriptions — semantic glosses with exclusions (master §1.4)**

The same field appears under many surface labels across documents (*Insured Name*, *Named Insured*, *Applicant*, *Name of Applicant*, …). The model has to map all of them onto one canonical key. Its main aid is the field's `description`, so write these as **definitions with explicit exclusions**, never as label lists:

```json
"insured_name": {
  "type": ["string", "null"],
  "description": "The party purchasing the insurance policy. Not the certificate
                  holder, the producer/agency, or an additional insured."
}
```

The exclusion clause is the part doing the real work — it teaches the **boundary** between confusable entities, which insurance documents are dense with (certificate holder, producer, additional insured, loss payee, carrier). A definition generalises to phrasings nobody listed; a list of aliases does not, and gives the model a lexical prior that makes confusable errors *worse*.

Rules for writing them:
- Define what the field **is**, in the document's own domain terms.
- Add an exclusion clause naming the confusable entities it must not be.
- One or two lines. These ride on every training row and every production request.
- **Never enumerate surface labels here.** Those live in the alias registry below, which never enters the prompt.

### 3a. `schemas/aliases/{doc_type}.aliases.json` — the alias registry

A versioned sidecar per document type, recording the surface forms actually observed and the entities that must not be confused with them:

```json
{
  "insured_name": {
    "aliases": ["Insured Name", "Named Insured", "Applicant", "Name of Applicant",
                "Applicant Name", "Name Insured", "Insured"],
    "confusables": ["Certificate Holder", "Producer", "Agency",
                    "Additional Insured", "Loss Payee", "Mortgagee", "Carrier"]
  },
  "policy_number": {
    "aliases": ["Policy Number", "Policy No.", "Policy #", "Certificate Number"],
    "confusables": ["Quote Number", "Claim Number", "Binder Number"]
  }
}
```

**Three consumers, and one hard prohibition:**

| Consumer | Use |
|---|---|
| IMPL-04 | Annotator rulebook — so three reviewers apply one consistent mapping, and a label naming a confusable is rejected |
| IMPL-05 | Alias coverage counting in the corpus manifest |
| IMPL-08 | Per-alias eval slicing and the confusable-misattribution metric |

**It is never rendered into the prompt and never consulted at inference** (master §1.4 anti-pattern). Adding a newly observed surface form is a registry edit, **not** a schema change — it triggers no corpus rebuild. That asymmetry is deliberate: the registry can grow freely as annotators encounter new phrasings, while the glosses stay stable.

`schemas/aliases/policy.aliases.json` is built from the canonical schemas by `scripts/derive_aliases_from_canonical.py`, one entry per canonical field, reading each common-model line through its bundle. The SPEC_21 schemas also carry the labels carriers print; those do not reach a rendered prompt either, beyond what a field's own description says (a test checks every common-model prompt, whole and per window). Instead `scripts/check_alias_coverage.py` counts, per common-model line, how many training documents print each recorded label and lists those none does, and evaluation splits field accuracy into labels training showed and labels it never did (`evaluation/metrics/unseen_labels.py`, reported, not gated).

Each schema must be loadable by `jsonschema` and validate a correct example. Include one valid example JSON per schema under `schemas/examples/{type}.example.json`, each exercising a populated `line_of_business` and at least one `null` field.

**Schema versioning:** each schema declares a version and the registry exposes it (`common.schemas.schema_version`): a top-level `version` in `schemas/` (e.g. `lossrun` 1.1.0), `fideon:source.version` in a self-contained canonical file (e.g. `property` 1.5.0), and for a common-model line the bundle version `<overlay>+common.<common model>` (e.g. `homeowners` 1.0.0+common.1.1.0, `gl` 3.0.0+common.1.1.0). Master §7 — a schema change forces a corpus rebuild and a new training cycle.

### 3b. `configs/canonical schema/` — the client's policy schemas (read-only)

Every policy is extracted into the client's canonical JSON for its line. These files are the client's: never edited here; what this repository adds lives in separate files (`configs/model_view.yaml`, `configs/schema_sections.yaml`, `configs/label_mappings.yaml`).

- **`LOB Schema/*.json`** — 34 files. 31 lines are registered as `policy:<line>` (`common.schemas._sources`), plus `_fallback.json` as the key `policy` (a policy with no single line, or a package). `_common.json` and `classic_auto.json` are not registered (classic auto is personal auto). With the three ACORD forms and the Loss Run, the registry holds **36 schema keys**. Request line names reach a file through `LOB_SCHEMA_ALIASES` (`general_liability`/`cgl` → `gl`, `workers_comp` → `wc`, `commercial_auto` → `auto`) and `common.lob.MERGED_LINES`.
- **The Fideon SPEC_21 common model** — `common schema/common_model.json` (1.1.0). Nine line files are thin overlays that `$ref` it block by block: the seven personal lines, `gl` and `_fallback`. The registry **bundles** each overlay at load (`common.schemas._bundle`): references into the common model are made local and the definitions they reach copied in, so the validator, the slicer, the prompt and the every-key fill read one self-contained schema. A bundle is refused when it references any other file, was written against another common-model version, or names coverage codes its `<line>.coverage_codes.yaml` does not; an overlay line in no layout family is refused; a family only partly on the common model (today `casualty_fleet`: `gl` moved, `auto`/`wc`/`umbrella` not) logs a warning, and training refuses a scope whose corpus would hold both shapes (`common.scopes.assert_one_output_shape`). `common.schemas.is_common_model` is the one switch every difference keys off.
- **The model view** (`common/model_view.py`) is what the prompt carries and the decoder enforces for a common-model line, while validation keeps judging the whole bundle: every printed value `raw`/`parsed`/`page_ref` (`raw` never null); no field the pipeline fills (document type, modality, page count, file name, `is_package`, a coverage's id); no full-text tier or provenance; each code field held to the line's coverage codes with their meanings; keywords xgrammar refuses dropped; author-facing descriptions replaced from `configs/model_view.yaml`.
- Each common-model line (the personal lines and `gl`) also ships `<line>.coverage_codes.yaml` (a prompt input), `<line>.crosswalk.yaml` and `<line>.fields.md`; `common schema/lob_schema_map.yaml` maps the 48 L1 codes to schemas and families.

### 4. `prompts/`
- **`system_prompt_template.jinja`** — rendered with `doc_type`, `acord_form`, `modality_mode`, `schema_json`, the output outline, the window's required keys, and two switches decided by the schema registry: `canonical` (a policy's `{raw, parsed, page_ref}` envelopes, absent fields omitted) and `common_model` (a SPEC_21 line). It includes `prompts/doc_types/{acord,lossrun,policy}.jinja`, each type's own structure and failure modes. Must:
  - State the doc type and inject the **exact schema from the registry** — never hand-typed per example (arch §7). For a policy window it is that window's slice of the schema (`sections`); on a common-model line, the model view.
  - Give extraction rules: valid JSON only, no markdown fences, absent fields → `null` (omitted in the canonical shape), the output date format rendered from `OUTPUT_DATE_LABEL` (`MM/DD/YYYY`), how to handle repeating table rows. On a common-model line also: each value written once, its `page_ref` listing every page that prints it; identifiers, references, codes and types as plain strings; rows only from the pages given; `page_ref` the only source reference; and, for the window holding the additional fields, which top-level sections other windows read.
  - Include the **evidence block for the modality**, explicitly declared rather than silently omitted (arch §6):
    - `ocr_plus_image`: OCR text and page images of one document; the OCR text is the primary source for dense text, identifiers and amounts, the images are the authority on layout, and where the two disagree on a character the image wins.
    - `noisy_ocr_image`: identical to `ocr_plus_image` (`common.constants.PROMPT_MODE_ALIASES`) — **the noise lives in the data, not the instruction**.
    - `image_only`: page images and no OCR text; read the values from the pixels, and output null rather than a partly guessed identifier or amount.
  - Be versioned by `common.prompts.PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` (now **7.2.0**), a constant recorded in the corpus manifest (IMPL-05), not text in the prompt.
- **`doc_type_classifier_prompt.jinja`** — zero-shot prompt asking the model to name the doc type (`acord`/`policy`/`lossrun`) and, if acord, the form number — the **two-level classification** of arch §4b — and, when lines are passed, a policy's line of business from the registered lines, each with its meaning from `configs/lob_meanings.yaml`. Output a small fixed JSON: `{"doc_type": "...", "acord_form": "...", "lob": "..." | null, "confidence": 0.0}` (without `lob` when no lines are passed). A `line_only` variant asks the line alone (the family adapter's stage). Line detection ships off (`routing.detect_lob: false`).

**Non-negotiable:** `common.prompts.render_system_prompt(...)` is the single renderer used by dataset build (IMPL-05), evaluation (IMPL-08), serving (IMPL-11), and testing (IMPL-12); `render_classifier_prompt(...)` renders the classifier. Training-time and inference-time prompts must render identically (arch §7).

- The template renders each field's **`description` alongside its type**, so the semantic glosses (§3) reach the model. No structural change is needed — descriptions ride along with the already-injected schema.
- The template **must not** reference the alias registry (master §1.4).
- **`common.prompts.prompt_hash()`** is a SHA-256 over `prompt_input_files()`: every template (`prompts/**/*.jinja`), every schema in `schemas/` and `configs/canonical schema/LOB Schema/`, the common model, the coverage-code files, `configs/model_view.yaml`, `configs/schema_sections.yaml`, `configs/lob_meanings.yaml` and `configs/layout_families.yaml`. Serving refuses at cold start a release packaged with a different hash.
- `testing/prompts/*.prompt.txt` (45 files) are reference renderings, regenerated by `python -m testing.render_prompts` and checked in CI with `--check`; they include every window prompt a common-model line sends. The self-contained lines', ACORD and Loss Run prompts are frozen as fingerprints in `tests/fixtures/old_style_prompt_fingerprints.json`.

### 5. `common/` (shared library — create this package)
- `common/config.py` — YAML plus environment, with the cross-file invariants enforced: `load_yaml(path)`, `env(name)`, one loader per file (`base_model_config`, `serving_config`, `training_config(name)` which merges an `extends:` chain, `scopes_config`, `layout_families_config`, `lob_to_layout_family`), the shared budgets (`pixel_budget`, `seq_cap_for_task`, `answer_cap`), and the checks (`assert_resolution_parity`, `assert_task_budgets_are_coherent`, `assert_effective_batch`, `assert_model_revision_pinned`, `validate_all`).
- `common/schemas.py` — the schema registry: `schema_key(doc_type, acord_form, lob, sections)` selects a schema (a policy by line, the fallback otherwise), loads and bundles canonical overlays, validates (`validate`, `iter_validation_errors`), slices a schema per window, lists required fields, exposes `schema_version`, `is_canonical`, `is_common_model`, `with_page_bounds` (a window's `page_ref` held to its own pages).
- `common/prompts.py` — `render_system_prompt(doc_type, modality_mode, acord_form, lob, sections)`, `render_classifier_prompt(lines, line_only)`, `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION`, `prompt_input_files()` / `prompt_hash()`.
- `common/ids.py` — `source_id` helpers: build/parse `{doc_type}_{index}`, validate format.
- `common/lob.py` *(new)* — the LOB enum, validation, the list form (`normalize_lob`), merged lines (`MERGED_LINES`, `merge_line`: classic auto → personal auto, watercraft → ocean marine), and a per-value coverage counter used by IMPL-05 and IMPL-08.
- `common/canonical.py` — the client's canonical `FieldValue` JSON and the shapes around it: the training target (`training_target`, `to_model_target`), the system fields the pipeline fills (`with_system_fields`), the every-key fill (`with_all_keys`), output dates (`with_output_dates`).
- `common/model_view.py` — a common-model line's schema as the model sees it (§3b), with `configs/model_view.yaml`.
- `common/structural_ids.py` — structural ids on a common-model document: unit keys, references resolved to them, renumbering in printed order, and `comparable_view` (what scoring compares instead of ids).
- `common/grounding.py` — whether a value is printed, and on which pages: one matcher for the label rules, the audit, the OCR-agreement feature and the hallucination metric.
- `common/schema_sections.py` — which canonical sections one extraction call asks for (`configs/schema_sections.yaml`), and `run_overlap` (pages two windows of a split run share).
- `common/scopes.py` — training scopes (`load_scopes`, `structural_not_applicable`, `assert_one_output_shape`); `common/tasks.py` — the `Task` enum and `CORPUS_TASKS` (the tasks the corpus builds rows for: `extract` and the three policy window tasks).
- `common/label_mapping.py` — `configs/label_mappings.yaml` applied when targets are built and labels scored.
- `common/gpu.py` — model compute never falls back to the CPU; `common/run_ids.py` — the one run-id shape.
- `common/logging_setup.py` — the run's logging: `setup()` installs a `ConsoleHandler` (a `StreamHandler` subclass that ms-swift's and modelscope's logger setup leave alone), and `reassert()` restores our levels, handlers and loggers before every stage.
- `common/normalize.py` *(moved here from IMPL-08)* — per-field-type normalizers so comparison reflects correctness rather than formatting: dates (`01/01/2026` == `2026-01-01`), currency (`$1,200.00` == `1200.0`), entity names (`Acme Mfg LLC` ≈ `ACME MANUFACTURING LLC`), policy/claim number separators. **Built here rather than in IMPL-08 because three specs need it and the earliest is IMPL-04**: alias derivation (IMPL-04), the promotion gate (IMPL-08), and the testing routine (IMPL-12). Pure and dependency-free. One definition of "matches" shared by all three — divergence silently changes what "correct" means between them.
- `common/aliases.py` *(new)* — loads `schemas/aliases/{doc_type}.aliases.json`; `aliases_for(doc_type, field)`, `confusables_for(doc_type, field)`, `canonical_for(doc_type, surface_label)` (reverse lookup, **for labeling and eval only**), `is_confusable(doc_type, field, label)`. Mirrors the `common/lob.py` pattern. Its module docstring must state that no serving or inference path may import it.
- `common/constants.py` — `ACTIVE_DOC_TYPES = ["acord", "policy", "lossrun"]`, **`UNCLASSIFIED = "unclassified"`** (the holding bucket for documents whose type is not yet known — it lives here, not in `data_pipeline`, because `common/ids.py` and `artifact_registry/paths.py` both have to honour it and both sit below `data_pipeline`), the **`doc_type` → Fideon SPEC_00 canonical model mapping table (master §1.2)** (`DOC_TYPE_TO_CANONICAL`), ACORD form list, modality modes + mix ratios (`MODALITY_MIX`, `PROMPT_MODE_ALIASES`), resolution cap default, split-ratio table by volume (`SPLIT_RATIOS_BY_VOLUME`), `DEFAULT_LONG_DOC_PAGE_THRESHOLD = 5`, confidence review threshold.

## Constraints
- No business logic yet — scaffold + static assets + tiny shared helpers.
- Everything imports cleanly (`python -c "import common"`).
- **The doc_type ↔ canonical-model mapping exists in exactly one place** (`common/constants.py`).
- The LOB enum exists in exactly one place (`schemas/lob.enum.json`, surfaced by `common/lob.py`).
- Follow all global conventions in master §7.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Repo tree matches master §5 (empty dirs get a `.gitkeep`).
- [ ] `pip install -e .[dev]` succeeds (or documents which heavy deps are RunPod-only).
- [ ] All schemas load and validate their example JSON via `jsonschema`.
- [ ] **Every flat schema requires `line_of_business` as a list of enum values** (an empty list valid, meaning undetermined), and rejects an out-of-enum value.
- [ ] `common.prompts.render_system_prompt(...)` produces a correct system prompt for each of the 3 modality modes, and `noisy_ocr_image` renders **identically** to `ocr_plus_image`.
- [ ] Every common-model overlay loads as one self-contained bundle; a bundle referencing another file, or written against another common-model version, is refused (`tests/test_schema_resolver.py`).
- [ ] The self-contained lines', ACORD and Loss Run prompts render exactly as frozen (`tests/test_old_style_prompts_frozen.py`), and `python -m testing.render_prompts --check` passes.
- [ ] **Every field in every schema has a non-empty `description`**, and each description for a confusable-prone field carries an exclusion clause.
- [ ] The rendered prompt **contains** the field descriptions and **contains no alias strings**.
- [ ] `schemas/aliases/{doc_type}.aliases.json` loads for every active doc type; no string appears in both `aliases` and `confusables` for the same field.
- [ ] `common.aliases.canonical_for("policy", "Applicant")` returns `insured_name`; `is_confusable("policy", "insured_name", "Certificate Holder")` is true.
- [ ] `common.constants` resolves `lossrun → loss_run/LossRunDocument` and the other two active types.
- [ ] `configs/training/unified.yaml` contains every parameter in arch §11's full specification tables (not just the summary table).
- [ ] `configs/sweeps/` defines the 3 phases with the documented candidate sets and budgets (authored, not executed this cycle).
- [ ] `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml` resolution cap equals `configs/base_model.yaml` — assert this in a test.
- [ ] `.env.example` lists every env var used anywhere in the design.
- [ ] `ruff` and `mypy` pass on `common/`.

---

## Current implementation (commit f5668a4, 2026-10-13)

**Dependencies** (`pyproject.toml`; resolved for Linux with `uv` — the resolver found three failures that
would have broken the pods, fixed below):

| Group | Holds | Why separate |
|---|---|---|
| `data` | azure-storage-blob, pypdf, pillow `>=11,<12`, PyMuPDF `>=1.24,<1.25` | Blob and page rendering, everywhere |
| `ocr` | `mineru[pipeline]>=3.4.5,<3.5`, `six>=1.16`, torch `>=2.8,<2.9`, torchvision `>=0.23,<0.24` | MinerU 3.4's pipeline backend (layout, PP-OCRv6 OCR, tables; no VLM); the code imports `mineru` and records its version on every page. `six` because MinerU 3.4.5's OCR imports it without declaring it |
| `train` | torch `>=2.8,<2.9`, transformers `>=4.57,<4.58`, peft, bitsandbytes, accelerate, deepspeed, ms-swift `>=3.9,<4`, qwen-vl-utils | torch pinned to the minor vLLM 0.11 pins: the training pod also runs vLLM |
| `serve` | vLLM `==0.11.0`, transformers `>=4.57,<4.58` | exact, and the same on both pods: calibration is fitted on the training pod's vLLM |
| `quantize` | `llmcompressor>=0.8` (its PyPI name), torch `>=2.8,<2.9` | needs datasets `>=4` (ms-swift 3 needs `<4`) and a transformers range vLLM 0.11 excludes: its own environment |
| `eval`, `track`, `dev` | numpy/scikit-learn/openpyxl, mlflow, pytest/pytest-cov/ruff/mypy/packaging/pyarrow | |

flash-attn is in no group: it must be built against the installed torch with `--no-build-isolation`,
which `scripts/setup_pod.sh train` does as a second step. `tests/test_dependencies.py` fails when the code
imports a package no group installs (MinerU was exactly that), or a requirements file names an unknown group.

**Pod roles** (`scripts/setup_pod.sh ocr|train|serve|quantize|dev`): installs in order, builds flash-attn,
installs tmux, checks every expected package and that torch sees CUDA. On the pod it runs itself in tmux.

**Two environments, not one.** MinerU's dependency set (its own runtimes for layout, OCR and tables) is kept
apart from the training stack, so `ocr` and `train` are separate environments: `scripts/pod_bootstrap.sh`
builds `/workspace/venv` (train, which includes serve) and `/workspace/venv-ocr` (ocr) on the volume and
records the requirements and lock each was built from, so a re-run reinstalls only what changed. MinerU's
pipeline weights are a separate, pinned download (`scripts/download_mineru_models.sh`: PDF-Extract-Kit-1.0 at a
fixed revision into `HF_HOME`, and `mineru.json` written to point at them); OCR refuses to run without that
config, off the GPU, with a remote model source, or with a MinerU 1.x (`magic-pdf.json`) config
(`data_pipeline.ocr.mineru_config`).

**Lock files** (`requirements-{train,ocr,serve,quantize}.lock`, from `scripts/lock_requirements.sh` with uv, for
Linux x86-64 / Python 3.11): `setup_pod.sh` installs each role with `-c <lock>`, so every package is pinned.
Resolving them found what ranges alone hid: the serving environment took **transformers 5.x** (vLLM 0.11.0 sets
no upper bound) and the OCR and quantize environments took the newest torch, built for a CUDA newer than the
pod's. Now `transformers>=4.57,<4.58` in train and serve, `torch>=2.8,<2.9` in every environment, `pillow<12`
and `PyMuPDF<1.25` everywhere (one renderer and one resizer for the OCR environment's training pages and for
serving), and the serve lock is resolved inside the train lock so serving runs exactly what calibration saw.
`flash-attn<3`. The OCR role installs `libgl1`/`libglib2.0-0` (OpenCV). The release bundle's `lockfile_hash` is
the train lock's. `tests/test_dependency_locks.py` fails on a stale lock or drift between environments, and
keeps `six` in the OCR lock. Locked today: ms-swift 3.12.6, transformers 4.57.6, torch 2.8.0, vLLM 0.11.0
(train and serve); mineru 3.4.5 with the same torch, transformers 4.57.6, pillow 11.3.0 and PyMuPDF 1.24.14
(ocr); llmcompressor 0.9 (quantize).

**Base model revision** pinned to `0c351dd01ed87e9c1b53cbc748cba10e6187ff3b` (Hub commit of 2025-10-15, four
safetensors shards, 17.5 GB); the bootstrap downloads exactly that into `local_dir`.

**`.env.example`**: the Azure connection string is quoted — the file is sourced by bash on the pod, which
would cut an unquoted value at its first `;`. `HF_HOME` is documented (on the pod,
`/workspace/.cache/huggingface`); `MINERU_TOOLS_CONFIG_JSON` names MinerU 3.x's `mineru.json` and may be an
absolute path (the bootstrap sets `/workspace/mineru.json`, replacing a MinerU 1.x `magic-pdf.json`);
`MINERU_DEVICE_MODE=cuda` and `MINERU_MODEL_SOURCE=local` are set by the bootstrap and applied before MinerU
is imported.

**Vision budget** (`configs/shared/vision.yaml`): budgets are whole 32×32 visual tokens — `max_pixels`
`2483200` (was `2483712`, a Qwen2.5 28×28 figure that made `MAX_PIXELS` and `IMAGE_MAX_TOKEN_NUM` name
different budgets). `common.config.pixel_budget()` is the one source for the trainer's env and the vLLM
engine's `mm_processor_kwargs`, and refuses a non-multiple.

**Section map** (`configs/schema_sections.yaml`): the policy section groups (decl, arrays, lineblk, dtd),
their page rules and identifying keys, read by the window planner in training and serving. Common-model lines
read the `common_model` profile instead (decl: the policy-level objects, `lob_parts`, premium, billing; arrays:
every unit table and everything that refers to a unit, plus `additional_fields`; lineblk: the remainder, today
the forms list), with each table's id field and prefix, the reference fields, identifying keys, unit keys and
`run_overlap_pages: 1`. `pages.declarations_leading: 5` makes the declarations group read the first five
pages (plus a declarations page found later).

**Schema 2 configs** (all prompt or target inputs, never the client's files): `configs/model_view.yaml`
(description replacements; a test fails once the client's own text matches an entry),
`configs/lob_meanings.yaml` (a test keeps recorded carrier labels out and requires every registered line),
`configs/label_mappings.yaml`, `configs/coverage_code_recodes.yaml` (27 codes re-coded at bundling), and
`configs/coverage_code_meanings.yaml` (the accuracy plan's template of neutral code meanings, to be filled in;
not yet read by the model view).

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line.
- §1: `[ocr]` is MinerU 3.4 (pipeline backend); requirements install through locks; `.env.example` list completed (MinerU 3.x vars, HF vars, intake/workspace, external pre-annotation endpoint); `.gitignore` list matches the file (data folders, `Documentation/`).
- §2: `unified.yaml` values corrected to the file (dropout 0.10, LR 1e-4, `warmup_steps`, `adamw_torch`, weight decay 0.0, effective batch 8, mergers not targeted) and the time-cut keys added (`length_grouped_sampling: false`, `loss_sample_rows: 160`, `selection_document_finalists: 2`, `selection_document_modes: [ocr_plus_image]`, `selection_guard_margin`); added `shared/`, `personal_lines.yaml`, `scopes.yaml` (incl. `casualty_fleet`), `layout_families.yaml`, `schema_sections.yaml`, `model_view.yaml`, `lob_meanings.yaml`, `label_mappings.yaml`, the coverage-code files; `vllm_serving.yaml` described as it is (no adapter map, `validation_batch_rows: 96`, `detect_lob: false`, LoB thresholds, release pins).
- §3/§3a/§3b: `line_of_business` is a list over the 12-value enum, flat schemas only; output dates `MM/DD/YYYY`; `policy_doc.schema.json` is legacy (key `policy` → `_fallback.json`); schema versions as the registry reads them; new §3b on the canonical schemas, the SPEC_21 common model 1.1.0 overlays and bundling, partly migrated families, and the model view; alias derivation and coverage checks for SPEC_21 lines.
- §4: the template's real switches (`canonical`, `common_model`), common-model rules, evidence blocks as rendered, `PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION` 7.2.0, classifier `lob` output, renderer names, prompt hash inputs, reference snapshots and frozen fingerprints.
- §5: `common/` described as built (config loaders, registry functions, `canonical`, `model_view`, `structural_ids`, `grounding`, `schema_sections`, `scopes`/`tasks`, `label_mapping`, `gpu`, `run_ids`, `logging_setup`).
- Current implementation: MinerU 3.4.5 dependencies, pinned weights and config refusals, locked versions, the `common_model` section profile and the Schema 2 config files.

# IMPL-02_blob_io_and_run_registry

# SPEC 02 — Azure Blob I/O + Training Run Registry

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01. This is the ONLY module that talks to the Azure SDK directly. Everything else goes through it.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §8b (tenant isolation per Fideon SPEC_12), §12 (versioning + training run registry), §13b (quantization thresholds recorded), §15 (metrics recorded), §18/§18a (Blob layout, raw-document access control).

## Goal

Provide a clean, typed interface for all Azure Blob reads/writes, and implement the training run registry that records **every** Foundation, per-type, per-tenant, and **sweep** training run.

## Deliverables

### 1. `artifact_registry/paths.py` (build this first)
- The **single place** the Blob layout (master §4) is expressed. Every other function builds paths through it.
- **Reserves the tenant prefix** (arch §8b): `TENANT_SCOPED` — `raw-documents/`, `processed/`, `golden-labels/`, `corpus/`, `releases/`, `exports/` and `golden-eval-set/` — are `{tenant_id}`-prefixed; `SHARED` — `base-models/`, `adapters/`, `merged-models/`, `quantized-models/`, `registry/`, `eval-reports/`, `calibration/` — are never prefixed (the module records that trained weights are tenant data under v2.1 and that moving `adapters/`, `merged-models/` and `quantized-models/` under the tenant is still a pending migration). `tenant_id` **defaults from `DEFAULT_TENANT_ID`** (else `default`) and is validated by `paths._tenant` (lowercase alphanumeric, `-`/`_`, at most 63 characters: it becomes a path segment). Every ingestion, OCR, import and pipeline CLI takes `--tenant`, and separate tenants are in real use: the CGL documents train under a tenant of their own, smoke runs under `smoke` / `smoke-cgl`. Each tenant has its own documents, labels, corpus versions, frozen eval set (`golden-eval-set/{tenant}/`) and releases.
- Distinguishes the adapter lineages: `adapters/foundation/v{n}/` (unified), `adapters/scope/{scope}/v{n}/` (a scoped run) and `adapters/{doc_type}/v{n}/` (a §4.2 graduated per-type adapter). A per-tenant lineage is **not built** until a broker requires one.
- **Also expresses the RunPod staging paths** (master §12a) — `/runpod-volume/staging/{adapters,merged-models,eval-reports,run_manifests}/` — deliberately mirroring the Blob layout so `package` (IMPL-13 command 2) copies rather than translates. Staging paths and Blob paths are built by separate functions that must never be interchanged: one is working storage, the other is the artifact of record.

### 2. `artifact_registry/blob_client.py`
- Thin wrapper around `azure-storage-blob`, initialized from env (`AZURE_STORAGE_CONNECTION_STRING`, `AZURE_BLOB_CONTAINER`, `AZURE_RAW_CONTAINER`). Two backends behind `BlobBackend`: `AzureBackend` (SDK imported lazily) and `InMemoryBackend` (tests; same write-once and container rules). The Azure SDK's own logger is held at WARNING.
- Methods: `read_bytes` / `write_bytes`, `read_text` / `write_text`, `read_json` / `write_json`, `exists`, `list(prefix)`, `delete`, `upload_file`, `download_file`, `upload_dir`, `download_dir`, `holds`.
  - `upload_dir(local_dir, prefix, *, names=None, skip_identical=False)` returns the files written. `names` uploads only those paths relative to the folder (a missing one raises `BlobError`); `skip_identical` leaves any blob that already holds the file's bytes.
  - `holds(local, key)` compares the MD5 recorded with the blob (`BlobBackend.md5`) with the local file's (`file_md5`, read in 8 MB chunks). A blob that records no MD5 does not count, so it is sent again. The properties call is retried like any read.
  - `AzureBackend.write` records `Content-MD5` on every upload (Azure stores one itself only for a single-shot upload, and a multi-gigabyte shard goes up in blocks); `AzureBackend.md5` reads it back from the blob properties, `InMemoryBackend.md5` computes it, the base class returns `None` (nothing is then skipped).
  - `download_dir` matches the prefix at a `/` boundary, so `corpus/default/v1` never pulls `v10`.
- Retries with exponential backoff (`max_retries`, default 3); a rule violation (`ImmutableBlobError`, `AccessDeniedError`) is never retried; clear errors on auth/missing-container.
- **Separate-container support (arch §18a):** keys under `raw-documents/`, `intake/` and `exports/` (`paths.requires_raw_container`) are routed to the raw container automatically, and only a client built with context `ingestion` or `ocr` (`for_ingestion()`, `for_ocr()`) may touch them; any other context gets `AccessDeniedError`, including for the bare prefix.
- **Immutability guard:** writing to (or deleting) an existing `raw-documents/.../original.pdf` raises `ImmutableBlobError`. Raw documents are write-once (arch §18a).

### 3. `artifact_registry/transfer.py`
*(One module, not the `push_to_blob.py` / `pull_from_blob.py` pair originally sketched: a push and its pull must agree on the path, and two files are two places for them to drift.)*
- High-level, path-aware helpers keyed to the Blob layout. Tenant-scoped helpers accept an optional `tenant_id` that defaults from env:
  - `push_adapter(local_dir, kind, version, doc_type=None)` → foundation / per-type path; `pull_adapter(kind, version, local_dir, doc_type=None)`
  - `push_scoped_adapter(local_dir, scope, version)` → `paths.scoped_adapter_dir` (see Current implementation)
  - `push_merged_model(local_dir, version, doc_type=None, scope=None)` / `pull_merged_model(...)`, `push_quantized(local_dir, version, fmt, doc_type=None, scope=None)` / `pull_quantized(...)`
  - `pull_base_model(local_dir)` (from `base-models/`, or from HF at the pinned revision if absent, then cache to Blob)
  - `push_corpus_version(local_dir, version, tenant_id=None)`, `pull_corpus_version(version, local_dir, tenant_id=None)`
  - `push_calibration(version, doc_type, params)` / `pull_calibration(...)` (IMPL-09 store; a missing calibration raises)
  - `push_eval_report(...)` / `pull_eval_report(...)`, `push_golden_eval_set(local_dir, tenant_id=None)` / `pull_golden_eval_set(local_dir, tenant_id=None)` — one frozen set per tenant, under `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/`
- Every push refuses a missing source directory (`TransferError`) rather than creating an empty prefix that would read as a published artifact.
- No standalone pull CLI: the pipeline stages call these helpers directly (training stages its own data through `training/stage_data.py`). `pull_base_model` from the Hub is not wired; the pod reads the base from `/workspace/models` (`configs/base_model.yaml` `local_dir`).

**Reading the index is not optional.** `_index` raises `RegistryQueryError` when the index blob exists but cannot be read. It must never degrade to an empty list: an empty registry and an unreadable one are different facts, and conflating them makes a throttled or 403'd Azure call look like a first-ever run — `latest_promoted` returns `None`, the promotion gate sets `is_first_version` and has nothing to regress against, and a candidate whose accuracy collapsed is promoted. The same swallow silently cleared the arch §12 Foundation cascade block.

### 4. `registry_utils/models.py`
Pydantic model `RunManifest` capturing the arch §12 manifest **plus the v1 additions**:

- `run_id`, `run_type` (`unified` | `foundation` | `per_type_adapter` | `scoped`), `doc_type` (nullable — the §4.2 graduated adapter a run *is*), `scope` (nullable, reads as `unified`) and `doc_types` (what a scoped run covers; required for `scoped`), `tenant_id` (nullable, defaulted), `status` (`training|trained|evaluated|promoted|archived|failed`), `created_at`, `continued_from` (a Foundation run continued from a checkpoint).
  - `unified` / `foundation` runs may name neither a `doc_type` nor a narrower scope; a `scoped` run must name its scope and `doc_types` and no `doc_type`.
  - **`training` exists because the manifest is written *before* ms-swift is launched** — the run_id has to be reserved and the configuration captured even for a run that dies. Without it the pre-launch manifest defaulted to `trained`, so a pod that OOM'd at step 40 left a registry entry asserting a trained adapter over a staging path holding nothing, and `package` published Blob locations for weights that were never written. `training.train.launch_and_record` flips it to `trained` when ms-swift returns and to `failed` when it does not; **`stage_push` publishes only `trained`, `evaluated` or `promoted` runs.**
- **`is_sweep_run`** (bool) + `sweep_id` / `sweep_phase` — reserved fields so sweep runs are first-class registry entries when sweeps are run (arch §11a; deferred past the IMPL-15 pilot).
- `dependencies`:
  - `base_model` (`qwen3-vl-8b-instruct@<hf_revision_pin>`)
  - `foundation_version` (nullable — which Foundation this adapter sits on)
  - `corpus_version`
  - `code_git_commit`
  - **`mineru_version`** and `ocr_device` (arch §8a) — read from the corpus manifest the run trains on (`training/train.py`), so the run records the MinerU that read its documents (the corpus build refuses documents read by more than one version, IMPL-03)
  - **`schema_version`** and **`prompt_template_version`** (arch §7)
- `training_config`: **`technique` (`LoRA` | `QLoRA`) and `base_quantization` (`bf16_frozen_base`, or `<quant_type>_<double|single>_quant_<dtype>_compute` under 4-bit) — both REQUIRED, never defaulted**. They previously carried QLoRA/NF4 defaults on the model, so a bf16 run that did not pass them recorded a technique it never used; base precision is exactly the kind of change that causes a regression, and the manifest is what makes one attributable. Then lora_rank/alpha/dropout, bias, lr, lr_scheduler, warmup_ratio, epochs, **optimizer (`adamw_torch`, also required)**, betas, eps, weight_decay, max_grad_norm, per_device_batch, grad_accum, effective_batch_size, gradient_checkpointing, mixed_precision, target_modules, **`vit_trainable`** with `vit_method` (`frozen` | `lora`; when trainable it must be **LoRA-on-ViT, never full fine-tune** — arch §3), resolution_cap_px, `pixel_budget` (the image resize budget the trainer was actually given), max_seq_len, **`seed`**.
- `data_stats`: train/val/test counts, `modality_mix` and `modality_mix_target`, **`lob_coverage`** (per-LoB-value share, arch §0b), `alias_coverage`, `confusable_example_count`, `tenant_ids` contributing, `split_policy` (carriers held out per line, single-carrier lines, families moved into test, twins dropped by the per-seed cap), `data_mix` (the scope's configured `synthetic_fraction` / `scanned_share` and what each line's train documents came to), `examples_by_line` (split → line → documents, real, synthetic, rows; scoped runs), `deidentified: true|false` and `image_redaction` (recorded; the enforcement rule is **suspended while de-identification is blocked** — see IMPL-00 §8 and IMPL-05).
- `eval_metrics` (all against the frozen golden eval set, arch §15):
  `field_exact_match`, `field_normalized_match`, `field_f1_list_fields`, `list_field_recall`, `schema_validity_rate`, `ece_confidence`, `ocr_arbitration_accuracy`, `image_only_accuracy`, `scanned_accuracy`, `doc_type_classifier_accuracy`, **`lob_detection_accuracy`** (overall, and `lob_accuracy_by_value`), `alias_accuracy` (reported), **`confusable_misattribution_rate`** (gating), `table_f1` and `lossrun_totals_reconciliation_rate` (Loss Runs), `held_out_carrier_match` (reported, not gated), `latency_ms_per_doc`.
- `artifacts`: **`status`** (`staged` | `published`), **`staging_path`** (set while `staged`), `adapter_weights`, `merged_model`, `quantized_model`, `quantized_formats[]`, `eval_report`, `calibration_params`.
  - `finetune` (IMPL-13 command 1) writes the manifest with `status: "staged"` and weights still on the RunPod volume; `package` (command 2) flips it to `"published"` and fills the Blob paths. A manifest is written **either way**, so a reclaimed staging volume never means an untracked training run.
- `promotion`: `gated_against`, `beat_previous_on_all_gates`, `failed_gates`, `promoted_by`, `promoted_at`, `tier` (`production` | `interim`, `None` when not gated or blocked).

### 5. `registry_utils/write_run_manifest.py`
- `write_manifest(manifest: RunManifest)` → writes `registry/foundation/{run_id}/run_manifest.json` (unified and foundation runs share the slot), `registry/adapters/{doc_type}/{run_id}/…` or `registry/scope/{scope}/{run_id}/…` (`paths.run_manifest`) to Blob, rewrites the run's **model card** beside it (`model_card.md`, `registry_utils/model_card.py`, rendered from the manifest alone; a card that fails to write is logged, never fails the run), and updates `registry/registry_index.json` (a flat table: run_id, run_type, doc_type, scope, doc_types, status, artifact_status, corpus/foundation version, commit, created_at, is_sweep_run, key metrics).
- `capture_git_commit()` to fill `code_git_commit`, `is_dirty_worktree()` so callers can warn; `mineru_version` comes from the corpus manifest the training stage reads (IMPL-05), not from a capture here. `mark_promoted` and `mark_published` (below and IMPL-13).
- `data_stats.deidentified` is **recorded but not enforced** while de-identification is blocked (IMPL-00 §8). Reinstate the refusal here — it is the right enforcement point — once the image-redaction question is resolved.
- `log_to_tracker` mirrors params and numeric metrics to MLflow when `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` is set (no-op otherwise; W&B is not wired) — the tracker gives the queryable UI, the Blob manifest is the durable record that travels with the artifacts (arch §12).

### 6. `registry_utils/query_registry.py`
CLI + functions:
- `get(run_id)` → RunManifest.
- `list_runs(run_type=None, doc_type=None, status=None, include_sweeps=False)`.
- `adapters_depending_on(foundation_version)` → adapter run_ids — this makes the arch §12 dependency-upgrade rule **a query, not a manual audit**: when Foundation moves to v3.0, this is the exact list needing re-validation.
- `latest_promoted(kind, doc_type)` → the version currently serving.
- `resolve_model_version(tag)` → given a user tag, return the concrete artifact paths. Used by IMPL-07, IMPL-11, IMPL-12, IMPL-13.
  - **`"base"` is a valid tag** and resolves to the pinned HF base model with **no adapter** — the zero-shot path used by `extract --model base` (IMPL-13), the pilot baseline (IMPL-15), and day-zero pre-annotation (IMPL-04).
  - `"v2"` resolves the merged model, plus a graduated per-type adapter (§4.2) when the routed type has one. Never a stack: one LoRA per request is what vLLM serves.
  - Resolves from Blob for `published` versions and from the staging volume for `staged` ones, so `extract` works on a model that has not been packaged yet.
- `diff_manifests(run_a, run_b)` → field-by-field delta. This is the arch §12 regression-debugging workflow: when a doc type regresses, diff the new manifest against the last-good one and see immediately whether the corpus, foundation version, MinerU version, or a hyperparameter moved.

## Constraints
- Only this package imports `azure-storage-blob`.
- All Blob paths built via `paths.py` — the layout lives in one place and the tenant prefix is not optional.
- `raw-documents/` is write-once; it, `intake/` and `exports/` sit in the raw container and are reachable only from ingestion/OCR clients.
- No PII in logs.
- Every training/eval/quantize job writes a manifest — **no silent runs, sweeps included**.

## Promotion and the index

- **`mark_promoted` requires the affirmative gate result**, not merely the
  absence of a failure. `promotion.beat_previous_on_all_gates` defaults to
  `None` and only the gate ever sets it, so rejecting just `False` let a run that
  was **never evaluated** promote cleanly — and the index could not tell it from
  one that actually passed.
- **A Foundation run owns its adapter and nothing else.** In a per-type build the
  merged and quantized models are produced per doc type, and the `doc_type=None`
  "unified" paths exist only under `--foundation-only`; publishing them anyway
  pointed the manifest at artifacts that were never built.
- **`registry_index.json` is a shared read-modify-write.** Concurrent runs each
  read N rows and write N+1, so the later write drops the earlier run's row — and
  the index is exactly what `adapters_depending_on` and `latest_promoted` read,
  so a lost row makes a run invisible to the queries the registry exists for. The
  write re-reads and re-checks; a store with conditional writes is the real fix
  before jobs run in parallel.
- **Version tags match exactly.** A substring match makes `v1` match `v10`, so
  `resolve_model_version("v1")` can read v10's artifact status and return a
  staging path for a model published in Blob.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] `blob_client` round-trips a file and a directory against a real/emulated container.
- [ ] Overwriting an existing `original.pdf` raises (write-once guard).
- [ ] `RunManifest` validates the arch §12 example (extended with the v1 fields) and rejects malformed input.
- [ ] `write_run_manifest` creates the manifest and updates `registry_index.json`.
- [ ] `adapters_depending_on("foundation-v2.0")` returns the right list from seeded manifests.
- [ ] `resolve_model_version("v2")` returns concrete adapter/merged paths; `resolve_model_version("base")` returns the pinned base model with no adapter.
- [ ] A `staged` manifest resolves to staging-volume paths; a `published` one resolves to Blob paths.
- [ ] `diff_manifests` surfaces a changed `corpus_version` / `mineru_version` / hyperparameter.
- [ ] Unit tests mock the Blob client (no live Azure needed for CI).
- [ ] A published scoped adapter holds `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors` only; a folder missing either is refused.
- [ ] A re-push of the same adapter or merged model writes nothing; a blob that records no MD5 is sent again; `upload_dir(names=...)` refuses a named file that is not there.
- [ ] Each tenant pushes and pulls only its own golden eval set (`golden-eval-set/{tenant}/`).

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

**Paths beyond the original layout** (all in `artifact_registry/paths.py`):

| Path | Holds |
|---|---|
| `adapters/scope/{scope}/v{n}/`, `merged-models/scope/{scope}/v{n}/`, `quantized-models/scope/{scope}/v{n}/vllm/{format}/` | a scoped run's own adapter and models (the unified scope keeps the pre-scope keys: `adapters/foundation/`, `merged-models/unified/`) |
| `eval-reports/v{n}/scope/{scope}/{summary,gate_decision,checkpoint_selection}.json` | a scope's report, verdict and chosen checkpoint (`paths.eval_report`, `gate_decision`, `checkpoint_selection`; not tenant-prefixed) |
| `registry/{foundation\|adapters/{doc_type}\|scope/{scope}}/{run_id}/model_card.md` | the run's model card, beside its `run_manifest.json` (`paths.model_card`) |
| `releases/{tenant}/{release_id}/bundle.json`, `.../calibration/{format}/calibrators.json`, `.../gate/{format}/gate_decision.json`, `releases/{tenant}/release_index.json` | the release bundle — the unit of promotion |
| `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/{source_id}/{golden.json,metadata.json,page_N.png,page_N.md}`, `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/manifest.json` | one tenant's frozen eval set (IMPL-08). A set left at the old root (`paths.legacy_golden_eval_manifest()`, `golden-eval-set/manifest.json`) is refused by `evaluation.freeze_eval_set` with the move to make, never read |
| `intake/{batch}/{document}/` (raw container) | a delivered batch of bundle folders, staged with azcopy before import (`paths.intake_batch_dir`, IMPL-03) |
| `exports/{tenant}/{kind}/{name}` (raw container) | result sets made for download to a laptop, e.g. a model comparison (`paths.export_dir`) |
| staging: `{mount}/staging/train-data/{scope}/v{n}/`, `{mount}/staging/train-images/{tenant}/{corpus}/` | a run's staged JSONL; one page-image cache per corpus version, shared by every run on it |

The staging root is `$RUNPOD_VOLUME_MOUNT/staging` (default `/runpod-volume`; set `/workspace` when the pod
mounts its volume there).

**`transfer.py`** has scope-aware helpers — `push_scoped_adapter(local, scope, version)`,
`push_merged_model(..., scope=)`, `push_quantized(..., scope=)` — which `package` uses to upload the real
adapter, merged model and quantized formats. Each refuses a missing source directory instead of creating
an empty prefix that would read as a published model.

- **A published adapter is its weights and config only.** `push_scoped_adapter` uploads
  `ADAPTER_FILES` = (`adapter_config.json`, `adapter_model.safetensors`) — never a checkpoint's optimizer and
  scheduler state (~1.5 GB of a ~2 GB rank-64 checkpoint) — and refuses a folder missing either file. Both
  `package` and the training stage's `--push-adapters` publish through it.
- **A re-push sends nothing it does not have to.** `push_scoped_adapter` and `push_merged_model` (~18 GB of
  bf16 shards) call `upload_dir(..., skip_identical=True)`: a file whose MD5 matches the one recorded with the
  blob is left in place. Blobs written before MD5s were recorded are sent once more. `push_quantized`,
  `push_adapter`, `push_corpus_version` and `push_golden_eval_set` upload everything.

**Run manifest** records `tenant_id` and the manifest's `quantized_model` points at the first *quantized*
format — bf16 is the merged model, never an export.

**Release bundle** (`registry_utils/models.py::ReleaseBundle`) also records `tier` (`production` at field
match ≥ 0.92, `interim` when it passed every floor below that; `evaluation.gating.release_tier`), and —
recorded, not gated — `latency_p95_ms_by_adapter`, `peak_gpu_memory_mb`, `measured_with`, and `routing` (where
every line of business routes once the release is promoted), plus `scope`, `doc_types` and `lines` (empty
means every type / every line).

**Blob `upload_dir`/`download_dir`** are what staging, localisation and package use; images are fetched
into the per-corpus cache atomically (unique `.part` names, renamed into place).

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Tenant prefix: `golden-eval-set/` is now tenant-scoped (one frozen set per tenant, legacy root set refused); listed the full `TENANT_SCOPED` / `SHARED` sets and `paths._tenant` validation; replaced "single-tenant" with the tenants in real use (CGL, smoke).
- `BlobClient`: documented `holds()`, `upload_dir(names=, skip_identical=)`, `file_md5`, `Content-MD5` recorded on every Azure write, `BlobBackend.md5()`, and the raw-container routing of `intake/` and `exports/`.
- `transfer.py`: `push_scoped_adapter` publishes only `ADAPTER_FILES` and skips identical files; `push_merged_model` skips shards Blob already holds; corrected helper signatures; golden eval set push/pull take `tenant_id`.
- `RunManifest`: added `unified`/`scoped` run types, `scope`, `doc_types`, `ocr_device`, `vit_method`, `pixel_budget`, the new `data_stats` fields (`split_policy`, `data_mix`, `modality_mix_target`, `examples_by_line`), new metrics (`table_f1`, `lossrun_totals_reconciliation_rate`, `held_out_carrier_match`) and `promotion.tier`.
- `write_manifest`: scope manifest path, model card written beside every manifest; removed the non-existent `capture_mineru_version()` (the MinerU pin comes from the corpus manifest); tracker is MLflow only.
- Current implementation: paths table gained model cards, per-tenant eval set, `intake/`, `exports/`; release bundle tier, latency, GPU memory and routing fields.

---

# IMPL-03_ingestion_and_ocr

# SPEC 03 — Ingestion + MinerU OCR (GPU, with Version + Device Pinning)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §7 (page images, multi-page), §8a (**MinerU version pinning**), §11 (resolution cap), §13 steps 1–2, §18a (raw-document storage, dedup, retention, access pattern).

## Goal

Ingest raw insurance PDFs into the immutable, tenant-scoped `raw-documents/` layer, then run MinerU OCR + page-image rendering into `processed/` — recording the exact MinerU version used, because the model is fine-tuned partly on *how MinerU formats its output*.

This is the front of both the training-data pipeline and (reused) the inference pipeline.

## Deliverables

### 1. `data_pipeline/ingestion/pull_raw_pdfs.py`
For each PDF from a local folder or source location:
- Assign/validate a `source_id` (`{doc_type}_{index}`, four digits: `policy_0001`; doc_type either provided or deferred — allow an `--unclassified` bucket resolved later by the classifier). **`build_source_id` and `paths.raw_pdf` must accept it**, not only the guard in `ingest_directory` — accepting it in one place and rejecting it in the others made every document fail one at a time into `failed`, so a broken mode read as bad input. Ids are numbered per tenant and doc type, past every id already in use in `raw-documents/`, `processed/` or `golden-labels/` (`taken_source_ids`), so the PDF importer and the prepared importer never hand out the same id.
- Compute a **SHA-256 checksum** and **dedup** (arch §18a): if the checksum was already ingested, skip and log. Insurance documents — Loss Runs and renewal policies especially — are frequently re-submitted with only minor changes; de-duping prevents redundant labeling effort and corpus bloat.
- Write `raw-documents/{tenant_id}/{doc_type}/{source_id}/original.pdf` — **immutable, exactly as received, never overwritten**. A corrected version of a document is ingested as a **new `source_id`**, so historical training runs remain reproducible against the exact bytes they trained on.
- Write `metadata.json`: ingestion timestamp, `tenant_id`, source system, original filename, checksum, size, page count, a first **digital-vs-scanned flag**, PII flags placeholder (`not_assessed`), retention class. The ingest flag (`inspect_pdf`) reads the extractable text of up to three pages with pypdf — PyMuPDF when pypdf cannot read the file, so only a PDF neither library opens is refused — and calls a document scanned below 50 characters per page. It counts any extractable text, a searchable scan's invisible OCR layer included, so it is a first look only: the flag the scanned eval subset (IMPL-08) and the ViT gate (IMPL-06) read is the OCR stage's `ocr_meta.is_scanned` (§3), which counts visible text only.
- Idempotent and re-runnable. CLI: `--input`, `--doc-type` (an active type or `unclassified`), `--tenant` (optional, defaults from env), `--source-system`, `--purge-plan SOURCE_ID` (prints the downstream cascade a purge must reach, deletes nothing). There is no container flag: the raw container is `AZURE_RAW_CONTAINER`, reached through `for_ingestion()`.
- Uses the tighter-RBAC container for `raw-documents/` (master §8). **This is the only pipeline component that writes this layer** (the labeled-PDF importer stores its PDFs through `ingest_pdf`).


### 2. `data_pipeline/ocr/mineru_version.py` *(new — arch §8a)*
- `get_mineru_version()` → the exact installed MinerU version string (`"3.4.5"`), from the `mineru` package's metadata (else `mineru.version.__version__`, else `"unknown"`, which is recorded rather than raised). A corpus read by MinerU 1.x (`magic-pdf`) records a 1.x version — another distribution.
- `assert_version_matches(corpus_manifest)` → raises when the running MinerU version **or device** differs from what a corpus manifest pins. Device is included because §8a's rule is about the *output distribution*, and GPU and CPU MinerU can format differently — confirm empirically and relax the device check (`DEVICE_AFFECTS_OUTPUT`) if they prove identical.
- **Serving a model trained on MinerU v{n} output against documents processed by MinerU v{n+1} is distribution shift, and is treated as a regression trigger — not a routine dependency bump.** This helper is called by the dataset builder (IMPL-05), the serving pipeline (IMPL-11), and the testing harness (IMPL-12); a mismatch is a loud failure with the remediation stated (reprocess affected documents, increment the corpus version, retrain).

### 3. `data_pipeline/ocr/run_mineru.py`
Batch-process documents from `raw-documents/` (or a passed list of source_ids):
- Run **MinerU 3.4.5's pipeline backend on GPU** to produce per-page markdown. GPU is the **only** execution mode (arch §14): layout detection, table recognition and the OCR models are GPU-bound, and the CPU path falls back to lighter model variants that are both slower and less accurate. `cpu` is refused (`mineru_version.resolve_device`), and the device is **recorded** in `ocr_meta.json`, never assumed.
- Choose how each document is read: from its **text layer** (`parse_method="txt"`) when every page carries visible text and its figures are typed, through **OCR** (`parse_method="ocr"`) when any page lacks a text layer, when a native PDF draws its figures as vector outlines, or — offline — when MinerU's own classification says OCR (`data_pipeline.ocr.modality.reads_by_ocr`, below).
- Render each page to PNG at the **resolution cap from `configs/base_model.yaml`** (`vision.max_image_long_side_px`) — identical in training data prep and production inference. This consistency is mandatory (arch §11): image token count is a direct function of page resolution and is the single biggest cost/latency lever.
- Write `processed/{tenant_id}/{doc_type}/{source_id}/page_{n}.md` and `page_{n}.png`.
- **Preserve page order explicitly** — Interleaved-MRoPE means multi-page ordering is positionally meaningful (arch §3). Page numbering must be stable and 1-based.
- Write per-doc `ocr_meta.json`: `page_count`, **`mineru_version`**, **`ocr_device`** (`cuda`; `cpu` appears only on corpora predating the GPU-only rule), `gpu_name`, **`preprocessing_date`** (ISO 8601, arch §8a), `resolution_cap_px`, `source_checksum`, `failed_pages` (pages OCR read nothing from), **`table_row_counts` per page** (consumed by the list-completeness signal in IMPL-09), `is_scanned` (any page without a visible text layer), `read_by_ocr` (the document went through OCR mode), and `drawn_check` / `ocr_rules` (the version of the reading rules it was read under).
- Idempotent — skip if the meta exists, is not render-only, and the source checksum, MinerU version, device **and** resolution cap all match, and the reading stands under the current reading rules (`OCR_RULES_VERSION`, see Current implementation); a change to any of them forces reprocessing.
- The MinerU invocation sits behind the `OcrEngine` protocol (`MinerUEngine` in production, a stand-in in tests), so it stays swappable.
- CLI: `--doc-type`, `--source-ids ... | --all-unprocessed`, `--tenant`, `--force-reprocess`, `--shard I/N`. `--all-unprocessed` lists documents with no OCR meta, a render-only meta, or a meta from another MinerU version.

### 4. `data_pipeline/ocr/render_only.py` (helper)
- Renders page images without requiring OCR text, at the same resolution cap — the `image_only` mode path (arch §6) for both corpus build and inference. `render_pdf_pages` is also the renderer `MinerUEngine` uses, so OCR'd and image-only documents are the same pixels.
- Writes the same `ocr_meta.json` skeleton (page count, resolution, version, `render_only: true`) minus OCR content, so `image_only` rows are still traceable.

## Constraints
- `raw-documents/` is write-once and tenant-prefixed; assert before writing.
- Resolution cap MUST come from config, never hardcoded, and be logged and recorded in `ocr_meta.json`.
- MinerU version MUST be recorded on every processed document, and a corpus is read by one MinerU version.
- MinerU reads only the pinned model weights, on the GPU.
- No PII in logs (never print OCR text).
- All Blob I/O via IMPL-02.

## GPU only — not a default, a constraint

MinerU runs on **GPU exclusively**. This is not a performance default with an
opt-out; the CPU path is refused.

The reason is correctness, not speed. MinerU's CPU path selects **lighter model
variants**, so the same PDF yields *different markdown*: different table splits,
different cell boundaries, different header handling. The model learns how MinerU
formats its output (arch §8a), so a corpus built across both devices is built
from two distributions — and serving from whichever the pod happened to have is a
third. A speed/cost trade would be a decision; this is a defect.

Consequences, all enforced in code:

- `resolve_device("cpu")` raises. There is no `--device` flag on the OCR CLI.
- With no CUDA device visible, the stage **fails** rather than falling back. A
  silent fallback would finish the job, write markdown from the wrong
  distribution, and report success — invisible until accuracy is unexplainably
  poor.
- MinerU's own device setting is forced too: `MINERU_DEVICE_MODE` is set to
  `cuda` before MinerU is imported, and any other value is refused
  (`mineru_config.assert_on_cuda`) — a GPU being present does not make MinerU
  use it.
- `ocr_device` is still recorded on every document. It is now always `cuda`, and
  that is provenance rather than a variable.
- `assert_gpu_only` refuses to *extend* a corpus that records `cpu` — such a
  corpus predates this rule, stays readable, and cannot take new documents
  without mixing two markdown formats in one training set.
- CI simulates a GPU pod to exercise these paths, because they now refuse to run
  without one.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Ingesting the same PDF twice results in one stored copy (dedup by checksum).
- [ ] Ingesting a corrected document creates a **new `source_id`**; the original is untouched.
- [ ] Digital vs scanned flag is set correctly on sample inputs.
- [ ] A **searchable scan** (page image with invisible OCR text) is a scan: it is read by OCR and recorded `is_scanned`; a blank page does not make a digital PDF a scan.
- [ ] A native PDF whose figures are drawn as outlines goes through OCR; a typed table drawn from thin border rectangles, or a vector logo beside typed text, does not.
- [ ] MinerU output produces `page_*.md` + `page_*.png` at the configured resolution, 1-based and ordered.
- [ ] `ocr_meta.json` records page count, `mineru_version`, **`ocr_device`**, `preprocessing_date`, resolution cap, OCR failures, per-page table row counts, `is_scanned`, `read_by_ocr` and the reading-rules version.
- [ ] The row count discounts **one header per table**, not one per page: a page with two tables otherwise over-reports by one, and the IMPL-09 cross-check then raises a false row-completeness flag — which active learning treats as an unconditional override to full manual review.
- [ ] A **render-only** `ocr_meta.json` (written by `render_only.py` at the same key) does not satisfy the OCR idempotence check. Otherwise a document rendered first is never OCR'd, no markdown is ever produced, and the CLI reports it as processed.
- [ ] `process_batch` reports genuinely skipped documents as skipped rather than counting them as processed.
- [ ] **OCR runs on GPU only.** There is no `--device` flag and no CPU fallback: `resolve_device` refuses `cpu`, and refuses to run at all when no CUDA device is visible.
- [ ] MinerU refuses to run without its config, on a MinerU 1.x config, without pinned pipeline weights on disk, or with `MINERU_MODEL_SOURCE` other than `local`.
- [ ] GPU throughput is measured per document (`benchmark_gpu`), for pod sizing and cost per document. There is no CPU comparison, because there is no CPU path.
- [ ] Re-running OCR on already-processed docs is a no-op; **changing the MinerU version forces reprocessing**, and `--all-unprocessed` lists documents another MinerU read.
- [ ] A text-layer reading stored under older reading rules is read again when the current rules send it through OCR, and recorded as checked when they do not.
- [ ] `--shard I/N` over N processes covers every document exactly once, whatever order each process lists them in.
- [ ] `assert_version_matches` raises on a seeded version mismatch with a remediation message.
- [ ] `render_only` produces images with no OCR dependency.
- [ ] Running `render_only` over an **already-OCR'd** document re-renders its images but **preserves the OCR metadata** — `table_row_counts` survives and the document is not relabelled `render_only`. Overwriting it silently switched off the IMPL-09 detected-row cross-check while the `page_*.md` files still sat there.
- [ ] `find_unprocessed` applies the same render-only test `process_document` does. Counting a render-only `ocr_meta.json` as done filtered the document out of `--all-unprocessed`, so it was never OCR'd and the CLI reported nothing to do.
- [ ] The `_reprocessed` marker is added to the **returned** metadata only, never written to Blob — persisting it made the skip path read it straight back, so `process_batch` reported every skipped document as processed.
- [ ] The serving-side `run_mineru` raises `MinerUError` on a timeout, any engine failure, or fewer than 10 characters read.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

- **MinerU 3.4.5, pipeline backend**: the `ocr` dependency group installs `mineru[pipeline]>=3.4.5,<3.5`
  (plus `six`, which its OCR text system imports, and the training stack's torch 2.8), locked in
  `requirements-ocr.lock` with the same PyMuPDF and pillow as every other environment, so page images are
  drawn identically. Layout, PP-OCRv6 OCR and table models; no vision-language model. The code imports
  `mineru` and records its version in every `ocr_meta.json`.
- **Pinned weights.** Left to itself MinerU downloads the latest weights on first use, so a pod set up next
  month would read pages differently. `scripts/download_mineru_models.sh` downloads the pipeline backend's
  model paths from `opendatalab/PDF-Extract-Kit-1.0` at a fixed revision (`MINERU_MODELS_REVISION`, default
  `ed6b654c018d742e65a17671e379c5e6ecc87ec9`) into `HF_HOME` on the volume, then runs
  `python -m data_pipeline.ocr.mineru_config --write <dir>`, which points `models-dir.pipeline` in MinerU's
  config at them and removes any `model-source` key (MinerU 3.4's config takes only huggingface or
