# IMPL-05_dataset_builder

# SPEC 05 — Dataset Builder (JSONL, Modality, Split)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-04.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §6 (modality dropout), §7 (dataset format, schema registry, prompt template versioning, long documents), §8 (corpus versioning, split strategy), §8a (MinerU pin in the manifest), §8b (tenant isolation per Fideon SPEC_12; **de-identification per Fideon SPEC_11 — BLOCKED, see §1**), §0b (LoB coverage).

## Goal

Compile processed docs + golden labels into versioned, chat-format JSONL training corpora — applying the 3-regime modality split and leakage-safe train/val/test splitting, with a manifest that pins everything the corpus depends on.

## Deliverables

### 1. De-identification — **BLOCKED. Do not implement.**

Arch §8b requires the unified extractor LoRA to train only on Presidio de-identified data (Fideon SPEC_11). **Do not build `data_pipeline/deidentify/` until the question below is answered**, because implementing it as specified would make the fine-tuned model measurably worse.

**Why this is a training bug, not a compliance gap.** Arch §8b specifies de-identification of **text**. It says nothing about the **page images** the vision encoder reads. If the text is de-identified and the image is not:

| Regime | Share of Foundation corpus | What the model is taught |
|---|---|---|
| `image_only` | **30%** | Image shows "John Smith"; target says `PERSON_1`. **The target is not derivable from the input.** These are unlearnable examples — 30% of the corpus becomes pure hallucination pressure. |
| `ocr_plus_image` | **50%** | OCR says `PERSON_1`, image says "John Smith", target says `PERSON_1` → teaches **trust-OCR-over-image**, the exact inverse of what the projector LoRA and the 20% `noisy_ocr_image` regime exist to teach (arch §3, §6). |

**Half-de-identifying is worse than either extreme.** Two coherent resolutions, both acceptable, to be chosen by the compliance owner:

1. **Redact page images consistently with the text** — input and target then agree, and the arbitration signal survives. Costs an image-redaction pipeline (bounding-box detection + overlay) that the architecture does not currently specify.
2. **De-identify nothing; rely on tenancy plus access control** — training data keeps real values and the input/target contract stays coherent. Requires explicit sign-off that PII in `corpus/` carries the same posture as `raw-documents/`.

Until resolved: the corpus manifest records `deidentified: false` and `image_redaction: "unresolved"`, the registry-side enforcement in IMPL-02 stays suspended, and **the limitation is stated to the compliance owner rather than left implicit**. If resolution 1 is chosen this section becomes the Presidio + image-redaction module; if resolution 2, it is deleted.

**Whichever is chosen, one rule holds:** de-identification must be **consistent across the OCR text, the page image, and the target JSON**. Any inconsistency between input and target teaches the model to hallucinate.

### 2. `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/split_groups.py`
- **Split at GROUP level, BEFORE task and modality expansion** (arch v2.1 §8.2). The dataset stage first assigns every document a family `group_id` with `data_pipeline/ingestion/dedup_and_group.py`, within its doc type, from: the source checksum OCR recorded (`ocr_meta.source_checksum` — the dataset build may not read `raw-documents/`), a MinHash over the OCR text, and the declared carrier + `template_id` + insured. Splitting per document would put one renewal of an account in `train` and the next in `test`, and eval would measure template memorisation.
- **Not yet computed:** the page-1 layout perceptual hash. No image-hash dependency is installed, so same-template documents with different text group only through a declared `template_id`.
- Ratio scales with per-type volume (arch §8), configurable, seeded, logged:

| Volume per doc type | Train / Val / Test | Note |
|---|---|---|
| under 200 (pilot, ~25–30/type) | 70 / 18 / 12 | ≈17–21 train / 4–5 val / 3–4 test. **Treat metrics as directional** — the batch's job is proving the pipeline works, not measuring model quality. |
| 200–999 | 75 / 15 / 10 | Val/test now large enough for stable metrics |
| 1000+ | 80 / 10 / 10 | Target state: 10% is ≥100 documents per split |

**Every triple must sum to 1.0**, and `common.constants` asserts it at import. The splitter assigns by hash threshold, so a triple summing to 1.05 gives the test split 10% while `ratios_by_doc_type` — copied verbatim into the corpus manifest — records 15%. Every downstream reader then believes the eval population is half again as large as it is. **Both split edges must also only rise from band to band** (`split_groups.assert_bands_only_grow_train`, at import), so crossing a band moves groups only toward train.

- Output: a deterministic `source_id → split` assignment, saved for reproducibility.
- **A split delivered with the data is used as given** (`assign_delivered_splits`; see *Delivered split* below) instead of being drawn here.
- **Splitting is tenant-scoped** — never build a split spanning tenants.

### 3. Modality expansion — `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/build_jsonl.py`
- **Train: one row per document per epoch** (arch v2.1 §6.1) — for a windowed policy, one row per window per epoch, all in that epoch's mode. `sample_modes` draws each train document's regime for each of the 4 epochs (`EPOCH_FILES`), seeded, against the target mix: the building scope's `modality_mix` when its training config sets one, else `common.constants.MODALITY_MIX` (**50 / 20 / 30**). The target is recorded as `modality_mix_target` in the manifest, and a training run whose scope wants another mix is refused (`training.data_mix.assert_corpus_mix`). It is the only sampling step: v1's `sample_to_target_mix` down-sampler is gone, because running it over epoch rows would drop documents from epochs. Twins over the per-seed cap are dropped before the draw (`cap_twins`), so the mix check measures what trains.
- **Val / test: all 3 variants** per document, so image-only and noisy-OCR accuracy are measured on the full eval population.
- The mix check (`assert_mix_is_close`, 5-point tolerance) is enforced from **900 draws**; below that it warns. At pilot volume (30 train docs × 4 epochs = 120 draws) one regime's share has a ~4.6-point standard deviation, so an enforced check would fail correct corpora.
- `image_only` rows omit the OCR text block entirely and use the image-only system prompt — the explicit "no OCR provided" declaration, not a silently missing field (arch §6).
- `noisy_ocr_image` rows use the **same instruction as `ocr_plus_image`** — the noise lives in the data, not the prompt.

### 4. `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/noisy_ocr_augment.py`
- For `noisy_ocr_image` rows: take the real MinerU OCR and inject **realistic** corruptions — digit↔letter confusions (O/0, l/1, S/5), merged/split table cells, dropped headers, reading-order scrambles — while keeping the **golden JSON as the correct target**. This teaches image-over-OCR arbitration (arch §5, §6).
- Corruptions parameterized and seeded. Mirror MinerU's real failure modes; **random noise teaches the wrong lesson**.
- Tag each corrupted row with the corruption types applied, so IMPL-08's `ocr_arbitration_accuracy` can score specifically on documents where OCR and image genuinely disagree.

### 5. `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/build_jsonl.py`
Orchestrates: for each tenant, split, and doc_type, assemble chat-format rows using the exact data contract (master §9):
- `system` = rendered prompt (schema + modality instruction) via **`common.prompts`** — the same renderer serving and testing use. **Never hand-type the schema per example**; inject it from the registry at build time so a schema change is a one-file edit that regenerates the whole corpus consistently (arch §7).
- `user` = image block(s) (+ OCR text unless `image_only`). **Multi-page documents produce multiple ordered `image` blocks, each followed by that page's own markdown, in one example** (see *Page pairing* below).
- `assistant` = for a flat type (ACORD, Loss Run), the golden JSON via `common.canonical.training_target` (dates written MM/DD/YYYY), including `line_of_business`; for a canonical policy, the window's target (see *Current implementation*).
- Carry `doc_type`, `acord_form`, `lob`, `modality_mode`, `source_id`, `tenant_id`, `split`, `deidentified` on every row, plus `group_id`, `synthetic`, `carrier`, `twin_index`, `render_mode`, `is_scanned` (what a scope's data mix and render rotation read, IMPL-06), `val_half` on validation rows, `epoch` on train rows, and `sections`, `task`, `window_index`, `window_pages` on a policy window row.
- Write `corpus/{tenant_id}/v{n}/train/epoch_{1..4}.jsonl` and `corpus/{tenant_id}/v{n}/{val,test}/{val,test}.jsonl` to Blob — all doc types together, because one adapter trains on every type (§8.1). These are exactly the paths `training/train.py` reads; `tests/test_orchestration.py` asserts that on a real build. Every epoch file holds every train document exactly once (`train_rows_by_epoch` refuses otherwise; a policy's windows may differ between epochs, its documents may not). Rows are written sorted by `(source_id, modality_mode, sections, window_index)` (`write_jsonl`). A line-scoped training run reads a filtered view of these files (`training/corpus_view.py`, IMPL-06), never a corpus of its own.
- Reference images/OCR by Blob URI or a training-time-resolvable path (document the choice; it must resolve identically inside a RunPod pod).

**Required edge cases the corpus must actually contain (arch §7)** — assert coverage and report counts in the manifest:
- Multi-page documents (multiple image blocks in one example)
- Rotated / skewed scans and low-quality faxes
- Handwritten annotations on otherwise digital forms
- Fields legitimately absent — must produce `null`, not a hallucination
- Repeating table rows of variable length (Loss Run claims, ACORD schedule lines)
- **Long multi-page policies**, so the page-selection strategy (IMPL-11 `page_router`) is tested rather than assumed
- **Confusable co-occurrence** *(master §1.4)* — documents where a canonical field and at least one of its registered confusables both appear on the page (a named insured **and** a certificate holder; an insurer **and** a producer), with the golden JSON resolving them to different canonical keys. Without these the model learns the mapping but never the **boundary**, and will happily return the certificate holder as `insured_name` with high confidence. Track the count in the manifest and treat a zero count as a corpus defect, not an acceptable state.

### 6. `data_pipeline/corpus_manifest.py`
Writes `manifest.json` per corpus version — this is what training and eval read to know exactly what a corpus version contains, and what pins it:
- Example counts per `doc_type` × `split` × `modality_mode`; `source_id` lists per split.
- Split seed, ratios, builder git commit; the whole `split_assignment` (counts by line, train-only lines, held-out carriers, twin cap, `delivered`, `frozen_eval_set`).
- `modality_mix` over train rows and `modality_mix_target`, the mix the modes were drawn against.
- **`mineru_version`**, **`ocr_device`**, and per-document `preprocessing_date` (arch §8a). Device is pinned alongside version because the model learns MinerU's output conventions, and GPU and CPU MinerU may not produce identical markdown.
- **`schema_version`** and **`prompt_template_version`** (arch §7) — a change to either forces a corpus rebuild and a new training cycle.
- `tenant_ids` contributing; `deidentified: false` and `image_redaction: "unresolved"` while §1 is blocked.
- **`lob_coverage`** (arch §0b) — computed here, not as a separate module: counts `line_of_business` per doc_type and split, compares against the **≥20%-per-value target**, and writes the actual distribution. Under-coverage emits a **loud warning naming the under-represented values**; it does not block the build, because the remedy is targeted document collection, not a build-time fix.
- **`alias_coverage`** (master §1.4) — same pattern, same module: counts examples per canonical field × observed surface label, read from each label's `field_provenance` (IMPL-04). **Alias coverage matters more than document count** — 95 documents saying *Named Insured* and 5 saying *Applicant* produce a model that is shaky on *Applicant* no matter how large the corpus is. Warn loudly naming variants below a configurable floor (default: fewer than 3 source documents). Non-blocking, for the same reason as LoB.
- **`confusable_example_count`** — how many source documents satisfy the confusable co-occurrence edge case, per canonical field. **Zero is a corpus defect** and warns accordingly.
- Edge-case coverage counts (multi-page, scanned, handwritten, null-field, long-policy).

**Corpus versions are immutable once used for a training run — never edit in place.** New labeled data appends into the next corpus version.

## Constraints
- Zero split leakage — assert no `source_id` appears in more than one split.
- Zero cross-tenant mixing — assert every row in a corpus shares its `tenant_id` prefix. This is the one live tenancy rule, because corpus composition is training data.
- Every row schema-checkable (a flat assistant JSON validates against the doc_type schema, including `line_of_business`; a common-model window target is validated against its window's slice of the model view, and a target that fails raises instead of being taught — `policy_windows._check_writable`).
- Deterministic + seeded; re-running with the same inputs yields byte-identical corpora.
- MinerU version at build time must match the version recorded on the processed documents (IMPL-03 `assert_version_matches`).

## Page pairing and the noise budget

- `SourceDocument.ocr_pages` holds **one markdown string per page**, never a joined blob. The corpus row's user turn is interleaved per page (master §9), so a training row and a serving request — including a page-routed one — have the same structure.
- `noisy_ocr_image` corruption is budgeted **per document, not per page** (`corrupt_ocr_pages`) — and for a windowed policy, **per window**: each window row gets the budget over its own pages, seeded by window, after routing (otherwise one budget over a 200-page policy left almost every noisy window byte-identical to its clean twin). Calling the single-page corrupter once per page multiplies the noise by the page count: a 20-page policy would take up to 40 corruptions instead of 2, and the "nothing changed" fallback would fire on every page. That is not a louder version of the same signal — a corpus where every page is garbage teaches the model to ignore OCR entirely, degrading the 50% `ocr_plus_image` regime that never sees noise.
- Corruption details record **which page** the noise landed on, so IMPL-08's `ocr_arbitration_accuracy` can match a corruption to the field it affected.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] No `source_id` crosses splits (automated assertion).
- [ ] No corpus file contains rows from more than one `tenant_id`.
- [ ] Each train document appears once per epoch file with the drawn modality (the scope's mix, default 50/20/30, across draws); val/test documents carry all three modes.
- [ ] `noisy_ocr_image` rows have corrupted OCR but **correct** golden JSON targets, tagged with corruption types.
- [ ] `image_only` rows contain no OCR text and use the image-only prompt; `noisy_ocr_image` renders the same prompt as `ocr_plus_image`.
- [ ] Corpus rows interleave each page image with that page's own markdown; no two pages' markdown are joined.
- [ ] The noise budget is per document: a 20-page document takes no more corruptions than a 1-page one, and no noisy row is identical to its clean twin.
- [ ] Every flat assistant JSON validates against its schema and contains `line_of_business`; every common-model window target validates against its slice of the model view.
- [ ] A window target cites only the window's own pages; no window holding a value no page prints is built; no coverage row is taught on a multi-window window that shows none of its home pages.
- [ ] `corpus_manifest` warns loudly and names under-represented LoB values below the 20% target.
- [ ] `corpus_manifest` reports `alias_coverage` per canonical field × surface label and warns on variants below the floor.
- [ ] `confusable_example_count` is reported per canonical field, and a zero count warns.
- [ ] `manifest.json` counts match actual JSONL row counts, and record `mineru_version`, `schema_version`, `prompt_template_version`, tenant list, de-id status, and edge-case coverage.
- [ ] Rebuild with the same seed produces byte-identical output.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

A document's `lob` is read through `common.lob.merge_line` (`SourceDocument.__post_init__`): classic auto is
personal auto everywhere.

**Policy windows.** A canonical policy is built as one row per *(section group × page window)* per reading
mode, planned by `policy_windows.plan_windows` — the same function `serving.pipeline` uses, so every served
window is a shape a training row had. The pages come from `policy_windows.routed_pages`, i.e.
`serving.page_router.plan_pages` over the OCR text **that row carries** (clean, corrupted, or none:
`image_only` reads every page); a document of at most `DEFAULT_LONG_DOC_PAGE_THRESHOLD` (5) pages is not
routed, but its declarations page is still detected. The groups are `common.schema_sections.groups_for(lob)`
over `configs/schema_sections.yaml`:

- **self-contained lines:** `decl`, `arrays`, `lineblk` (every section no other group claims), and `dtd`
  where the line carries it;
- **common-model lines** (`common.schemas.is_common_model`: the seven personal-lines overlays, `gl` and
  `_fallback`, common model 1.1.0): the `common_model` profile — `decl` (document, carrier, producer,
  named_insured, policy, lob_parts, premium, billing), `arrays` (every unit table and every table that refers
  to a unit — coverages, deductibles, interested_parties, locations, buildings, vehicles, drivers, watercraft,
  scheduled_items, rating_exposures, rating_modifiers, underlying_insurance — plus `additional_fields`),
  `lineblk` (the remainder). `text_sections` is asked of no group.

A group with no sections on the line is not planned at all.

`decl` (and `dtd`) read the first `pages.declarations_leading` = **5** pages plus the detected declarations
page when it falls later, together in leading windows; `arrays` and `lineblk` read every routed page.
`expand_tasks.plan_policy_windows` packs whole page runs while they fit and splits a longer run evenly; on a
common-model line consecutive windows of a split run share `run_overlap_pages: 1` page
(`common.schema_sections.run_overlap`, `_split_overlapping`), so a row printed across the boundary is whole in
one window. A window's capacity is `expand_tasks.pages_per_extraction_call(..., group)`: the group task's
`max_seq_len` minus its reserved `max_output_tokens` (`policy_declarations` 4,096, `policy_schedule` 12,288 in
`configs/shared/sequence.yaml`) minus the sliced prompt, per page at the task's pixel budget plus OCR.

**Window targets** (`window_target`): the group's sections, narrowed to values printed on the window's pages.

- A label written for another line's schema is first moved into this line's block
  (`common.canonical.schema_label`, `configs/label_mappings.yaml`).
- `page_ref` is narrowed to the window's own pages, in document page numbers, on a copy (the label is cached
  per document and read by every window and mode). A value printed on pages 1 and 30 is cited `[1]` by the
  window showing page 1.
- `text_sections` is never in a target (`EXCLUDED_FROM_TARGETS`). Each `additional_fields` entry is placed by
  its own `page_ref` — on a common-model line, by its own pages and its value's — trimmed the same way, and
  kept in SPEC_21's shape (label, value, section_hint, page_ref).
- A value with no `page_ref` is taught as it is in a group read in one window (`plan.single`). In a group
  read over several windows (`multi_window_sections`) it is given a page by `with_inferred_pages` when the
  clean OCR prints it (at least 4 characters, on word boundaries) on exactly one page, and is otherwise
  reported `unplaced`. Values printed only on pages no window of their group reads are reported `unread`.

On a **common-model** line, additionally:

- a label with two parts, or a part of another line, is refused as a mislabel (`with_own_part`; a part read
  as the line, such as classic auto, is rewritten as the line), and `part` is dropped on a single-part policy;
- ids, codes and types (values no page prints) ride only with a row that keeps a printed value on the
  window's pages, so no window is taught a bare row skeleton. A coverage's `form_refs`
  (`common.model_view.PRINTED_STRING_FIELDS`) ride only on windows whose pages print them
  (`printed_pages_in`, looked up once per document over the clean text); an item the text shows on no page
  stays with its row, since OCR misreads form numbers on scans;
- a reference to a table another group reads is left out (`common.schemas.cross_group_references`), the
  window's ids are renumbered from 1 (`common.structural_ids.renumber_structural_ids`), and references to
  rows the window does not hold are counted as `dangling`;
- **orphan rule:** only a printed value identifies a row (`_printed_identifiers`). A fragment that kept none
  of its row's identifiers is left out (`orphaned`) unless it holds identified nested rows or a value no page
  outside the window cites (`_cited_only_here`): a coverage split at a window boundary is taught, a premium
  printed again on a summary page is not;
- **home rule** (`HOME_FIELDS`): in a group read over several windows, a `coverages` row is taught only on
  windows that show one of its home pages — the pages that print its `coverage_name`, a limit's or
  deductible's `amount` or `percentage`, its `premium` or `coinsurance_percent` (nested rows included). A row
  with a home is left out of every other window (`TargetReport.away`, "away from home" in the build notes); a
  row with none stays wherever its values are cited. Windows of policy wording that print only a caption
  ("Each Occurrence Limit") or "Included" are not taught coverage rows;
- the finished target is written in model form (`to_model_target`), narrowed to the window's slice
  (`within_schema`), put in the grammar's key order, and validated against the window's slice of the model
  view (`_check_writable`): a target the decoder could not write raises `CanonicalLabelError`.

**Label checks before slicing** (`build_jsonl._policy_window_rows`, once per document, on the clean OCR text
whatever the row's mode — the target is the same answer in every mode):

- `label_verification.verified_label`: a value a rule added to a synthetic label (`fideon:filled`) is kept
  where the page prints it, re-paged when exactly one other page prints it, and left out otherwise; with no
  OCR text it is kept and counted unverifiable;
- **unprinted values**: bundle metadata `unprinted_values` (written by `prepare_bundles`' label rules) lists
  label values no page prints; a window whose sections and pages hold one is **left out whole**
  (`_held_unprinted`) — taught, it is a value to invent; deleted, a printed value the search missed would be
  taught as one to skip.

What every check left out — `unread`, `unplaced`, `orphaned`, `away from home`, windows left out, the dangling
count, the verification counts — is kept per document on `BuildResult.window_notes` and logged.

**Expansion failures.** A document that fails to expand is set aside (`set_aside["expansion"]`), except on a
common-model line, where more than `max_expansion_failures` (default 0) such documents fail the build.
`classify_rows` (the family adapter's line question) exists, but `build_corpus(with_classify_rows=True)` is
refused until serving asks that question.

**Blank pages** get one placeholder text in training and serving (`input_builder.EMPTY_PAGE_TEXT`) instead
of an empty block in training and a placeholder at serving.

**Size estimate** (`cap_check.estimate_row`): prompt and OCR text by `estimate_text_tokens` — digits one token
each (Qwen splits numbers into digits), the rest at 3.5 characters per token; pages at the task's
`max_pixels` / 1,024 visual tokens each; the **answer** by `target_tokens` — the base model's own tokenizer
when its files are on the machine (`base_model_dir()` with a `tokenizer.json`, loaded as
`training.length_check` loads it), else the estimate × `TARGET_ESTIMATE_FACTOR` (1.25). A row over its task's
`max_seq_len`, or with an answer over its `max_output_tokens`, sets its **whole document** aside (`budget`);
so does text containing a trainer tag (`trainer_tag`, `common.constants.TRAINER_SPECIAL_TAGS`). The real
tokenizer re-measures every staged row before launch (IMPL-06).

**Split, per line of business** (`split_groups.assign_group_splits`):
- the band (table above) is chosen on the **document type's** volume; placement is by stable hash **within
  each line of business** (`GroupRecord.line`, from the policy's `lob`), so every line lands near the ratio;
- a line with **fewer than 5 documents trains whole** and is listed in `train_only_lines`; a measured line
  with 3+ families always gets at least one val and one test family;
- **any family with a synthetic member stays in train** (the old rule — synthetic only if *every* member
  was — let a mixed family carry generated labels into test);
- **carriers are compared normalised** (`common.normalize.normalize_carrier`: "The Travelers Indemnity
  Company" = "TRAVELERS"; a bundle's own `carrier` first, else the label's), so a held-out carrier is not
  still in train under another spelling;
- **held-out carriers**: once a type has `MIN_CARRIERS_FOR_HELD_OUT` (6) carriers, 2 drawn by hash from the
  smaller half go entirely to test (`held_out_carriers`, `held_out_source_ids`);
- `counts_by_line` and `train_only_lines` are recorded in the manifest's `split_assignment`.

**Twin cap** (`split_groups.cap_twins`, drawn and delivered splits alike): at most `MAX_TWINS_PER_SEED` (20)
synthetic twins of one seed (family) per render mode train, chosen by a seeded hash, before modes are drawn;
recorded as `twin_cap` and `twins_dropped`. Real documents, validation and test are never dropped.

Example — 1,920 personal-lines policies: ≈1,523 train / 206 val / 191 test, each measured line near 80/10/10.

**After the eval set is frozen** (IMPL-08; each tenant has its own, `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/`, and only
the building tenant's counts): the build drops the frozen documents **and every document in their
families**, splits new documents into **train and val only** (train:val kept in proportion, no carrier held
out), records the frozen set's held-out carriers on the split (`frozen_eval_set: true`), and warns when a
carrier the frozen set held out — per doc type or per line — starts training.

**Guards**: render-only and zero-page documents are skipped with a reason; documents read by more than one
MinerU version are refused (`assert_one_mineru_version`); a build with no train rows is refused before
anything is written, and a real (non-dry) build with no val rows — or no test rows while the eval set is not
frozen — is refused too. A real build that sets aside more than 25% of its loaded documents
(`orchestration.preflight.FAIL_REJECTED_SHARE`, measured by `corpus_set_aside`, the figure preflight reads
too) is refused before anything is written. The stage result reports `documents_set_aside` by reason
(`budget`, `trainer_tag`, `expansion`) with the first examples.

**Manifest**: LoB coverage reads each document's line from metadata (canonical policy labels carry none);
the `modality_mix` counts **train rows only** (`modality_mix_basis: "train rows"`), since val/test carry
every mode by design; `modality_mix_target` is the mix the modes were drawn against.

**Line coverage in the manifest**: `lob_coverage` (the ≥20% enum target) counts only labels that carry
`line_of_business` (ACORD, Loss Run); policy lines are counted from metadata in `policy_line_counts`
(canonical schema names). Checking policy lines against the enum raised and failed the whole build.

Only **policies** contribute to `policy_line_counts`; ACORD and Loss Run documents stay in the enum coverage
even when the importer copied their label's `line_of_business` into metadata.

### Delivered split

A delivery split upstream is used as given. `data_pipeline/ingestion/prepare_bundles.py` writes one folder
per document with `metadata.json` (`lob`, `synthetic`, `template_id` = the family the split was drawn by,
`split`, `carrier`, `render_mode`, `unprinted_values`, `held_out_carrier`) from three kinds of delivery:

- fideon_synth's `Train|Val|Test` + `manifest.csv` (`prepare_bundles`);
- a SPEC_21 delivery's `split_manifest.csv` (`prepare_twin_bundles`): each twin becomes a digital and a
  scanned document reading one corrected gold (one document when both renders are the same file), its seed
  is the family, and each seed document is bundled as a real document in its twins' family and split;
- real documents alone, of one line (`prepare_original_bundles`, e.g. the CGL source data with `--lob gl`):
  one family per insured (`_insured_family`), the split drawn by `assign_group_splits` exactly as the build
  would draw it and written into the metadata (`split.csv` lists it for review); a test document of a
  carrier no training document has is marked `held_out_carrier`.

The importer carries `split` into the label metadata and refuses any value other than train/val/test. When
**every** document carries a split (all or none, else the build stops), `pipeline_dag.use_delivered_families`
makes each document's family its source document (`delivered:<template_id>`), `delivered_split_of` refuses a
source in two splits, and `split_groups.assign_delivered_splits` takes the assignment as given (validation
still halved by group; a split with no val, or no test before the eval set is frozen, is refused; after
freezing, no document may be delivered as test). The per-line carrier hold-out
(`held_out_carrier_per_line`: the cheapest carrier whose families can all go to test while the line keeps a
train and a validation family) is **off** for a delivered split (`HOLD_OUT_CARRIER_PER_LINE = False`; pass
`hold_out_carriers=True` to apply it): the delivery already keeps whole seeds out of training. Documents
delivered with `held_out_carrier` that sit in test are added to `held_out_source_ids`
(`delivered_held_out_ids`), so evaluation reports them apart. Families this pipeline detects by text
similarity that cross the delivered split — several source documents of one carrier share a printed form —
are reported with a count of val/test documents on a form seen in training, not merged.
`assert_synthetic_is_train_only` applies to drawn splits only; under a delivered split the synthetic twins of
held-out sources are evaluated, and the gate reports real documents apart (IMPL-08). The corpus manifest's
split record carries `delivered: true`, and the run manifest a `split_policy` summary (held-out carriers,
lines holding none out, frozen set, families moved to test, twin cap).

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line (commit f5668a4).
- §3: the modality target is the scope's `modality_mix` (default 50/20/30), recorded as `modality_mix_target`
  and checked by training; twins are capped before the draw; windowed policies draw one mode per epoch for
  all their windows.
- §5: assistant target split into flat-type JSON vs policy window target; user turn described as interleaved
  per page; row bookkeeping fields listed (`group_id`, `synthetic`, `carrier`, `twin_index`, `render_mode`,
  `is_scanned`, `val_half`, `epoch`, window fields); sorted output and scope views noted.
- §6: the manifest's `split_assignment` contents and `modality_mix_target` listed.
- Constraints and checklist: common-model targets checked against the model view (`_check_writable`); new
  checks for page-trimmed targets, unprinted values and the coverage home rule.
- Current implementation rewritten for Schema 2 windowing: `decl`/`arrays`/`lineblk` (and `dtd`) groups,
  the common-model profile, 5 leading declarations pages (was 1–3), `run_overlap_pages: 1`, window capacity
  from the group's reservation; target trimming, `additional_fields` placement, `text_sections` exclusion,
  structural ids and `form_refs` placement, renumbering and dangling references, the printed-identifier
  orphan rule with `_cited_only_here`, the `HOME_FIELDS` home rule ("away from home"), `_check_writable`,
  label verification, `with_inferred_pages`, unprinted-value windows left out, common-model expansion
  failures, refused `classify_rows`.
- Size estimate: answers counted by the base model's tokenizer when local, else estimate × 1.25 (was the
  plain estimate).
- Split: drawn-split held-out carriers, the twin cap, per-tenant frozen sets, the MinerU-version guard and
  the >25% set-aside refusal; delivered split extended to SPEC_21 twin bundles, seed documents, insured
  families for real-only deliveries, the per-line carrier hold-out (off by default) and `held_out_carrier`.

# IMPL-06_training

# SPEC 06 — Training (ms-swift LoRA: ONE Unified Adapter)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-05.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §3 (ViT escalation gate — **LoRA, never full FT**), §4.1 (**one unified adapter**), §4.2 (graduation gate for per-type adapters), §6.1 (per-epoch modality sampling), §8b (tenancy per Fideon SPEC_12; de-identification per Fideon SPEC_11 — blocked, see IMPL-05), **§9.2 (bf16 base vs 4-bit)**, §9.3 (memory settings), §9.4 / §9a (frozen ViT and mergers; LoRA targets), §10 (**locked three-layer trainer stack**), §11 (hyperparameters), §11.2 (checkpoint selection), §11a (**sweep methodology**), §12 (versioning + run registry).

## Goal

Fine-tune Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct with **LoRA on a bf16 base** via the locked trainer stack. **One adapter**, across every document type and every task, with the ViT *and* the mergers frozen, and every run — sweeps included — recorded in the registry.

Two reversals from v1, both forced by how vLLM serves:

* **One adapter, not a Foundation plus per-type stack.** vLLM applies one LoRA per request, so the two could never both be active — the v1 topology was unservable, not merely awkward. It also did not fit the data: at 25–30 documents per type a rank-16 adapter memorises its own training set.
* **The mergers are not a LoRA target** (`freeze_aligner: true`). Tower and connector LoRA in vLLM is experimental with known mixed-adapter batching risks, and image-vs-OCR arbitration is learned in the **decoder**, where image tokens and OCR text tokens attend to each other. The mergers only map visual features and never see OCR text at all.

## The locked stack (arch §10) — not a choice to re-make

```
Layer 3   ms-swift                    ← what you invoke (CLI / config)
              │ wraps and configures ↓
Layer 2   swift.trainers.Seq2SeqTrainer   ← the real loop, on the HF Trainer
              │ (optimizer, backprop, grad accumulation, checkpointing,
              │  eval hooks, early stopping, DeepSpeed/Accelerate)
              │ built on ↓
Layer 1   PyTorch + Transformers + PEFT + Accelerate (bitsandbytes only under load_in_4bit)
```

TRL's `SFTTrainer` is **not** in this stack: ms-swift builds its own `Seq2SeqTrainer` on the HF Trainer, and TRL is a dependency for its RLHF trainers only. `training/train.py` is a **thin wrapper that assembles the ms-swift config and launches it**, not a custom training loop.

**Fallback clause:** migrating to TRL `SFTTrainer` is permitted **only** if ms-swift lacks a specific Qwen3-VL capability needed at implementation time. It is a framework migration, not a layer swap — the template, collator and masking must be re-implemented against the §10.2 parity tests. Document it if invoked.

## Deliverables

### 1. `training/train.py` — the unified extractor run

Replaces v1's `train_foundation.py` and `train_adapter.py`. One entrypoint. The unified scope's run type is `unified` with run id `extractor-v{n}`; a narrower scope (`configs/scopes.yaml`) records run type `scoped` and run id `{lineage}-v{n}` — still one independent adapter on the base, never stacked.

- **Config:** `configs/training/unified.yaml` + `configs/base_model.yaml`. There are no per-type training configs. A scope names its training config (`training_config` in `configs/scopes.yaml`); `configs/training/personal_lines.yaml` declares `extends: unified` and adds only its `data_mix`, merged over unified by `common.config.training_config`, so the two cannot drift on LoRA, optimisation, batching or evaluation.
- **Base precision** from `training/base_precision.py`. Default is a **bf16 frozen base**: no quantization argument at all (ms-swift 3 has no `quantization_bit`), and every `bnb_4bit_*` argument suppressed so the run log never carries settings describing nothing the run did. Under `load_in_4bit` it renders `quant_method: bnb`, `quant_bits: 4` and the `bnb_4bit_*` set. `load_in_4bit: true` in `configs/base_model.yaml` switches to NF4 with double quantization and bf16 compute. The manifest's `technique` (`LoRA`/`QLoRA`) and `base_quantization` are derived from the same config, never defaulted.
- **LoRA:** rank 64 / alpha 128, dropout **0.10** at pilot scale (0.05 from ≥200 docs per type), `bias: none`. Targets are the attention and MLP projections of the **language-model decoder only** — `q/k/v/o_proj`, `gate/up/down_proj`. No `merger`.
- **Frozen:** `freeze_vit: true` (unless `--train-vit`, the §3 escalation — LoRA-on-ViT, never full fine-tune) and `freeze_aligner: true` always.
- **Optimisation (§11.1):** AdamW (`adamw_torch`), LR **1e-4**, cosine, **`warmup_steps: 10`** (steps, not a ratio — a ratio rounds to zero warmup at pilot volume), weight decay **0.0** (decay on a low-rank adapter is a shrinkage prior on the update, not regularisation), max grad norm 1.0, **effective batch 8** (`batch.effective_batch_size`) at per-device batch 1, the accumulation derived from the GPU count (`_accumulation_steps`: effective ÷ (per-device × GPUs), refused when it does not divide), gradient checkpointing, bf16. `dataset_num_proc` 8 for ms-swift's preprocessing, capped at the pod's CPU count.
- **Memory (§9.3):** `use_logits_to_keep`, `padding_free`. **`group_by_length` is false** (`memory.length_grouped_sampling: false`): with one row per device and padding-free batching there is no padding to save, and with it on ms-swift encoded every row before step 1 to learn its length (~1.5 h on the CGL run). Rows are visited in a plain random order: same data, same steps. `max_length` is the largest cap of **the scope's tasks**, including the `by_doc_type` overrides of the types it trains (`corpus_max_length(scope)`; 32,768 for any policy scope). If a cap does not fit one card, **sequence parallelism comes first**; DeepSpeed is off by default, because under LoRA ZeRO-2 shards ~1.5 GB of adapter optimizer state and is close to a no-op.
- **Datasets.** **Epochs are files, and ms-swift runs one pass over them** (§6.1). `dataset` is the first `num_train_epochs` (3) of the scope's epoch files — `corpus/{tenant}/v{n}/train/epoch_{1..4}.jsonl` for the unified scope, the view under `train/scope/{name}/` otherwise — and ms-swift is given **`num_train_epochs: 1`**. Each file already holds every train document once in that epoch's modality draw, so the concatenation *is* the N-epoch run; also telling ms-swift N looped the N files N times — nine passes for a "3 epoch" run. The manifest records the logical count. Fewer epoch files than epochs is refused. `val_dataset` is the scope's validation file (`corpus/{tenant}/v{n}/val/val.jsonl`, or `val/scope/{name}/val.jsonl`), staged as the fixed loss sample (see *Evaluation*) and passed separately (see below). The corpus is read for `--tenant`, never silently from the default tenant. (ms-swift shuffles across the concatenation, so epoch-2 rows can precede epoch-1 rows; each document is still seen exactly N times, each time in its drawn regime.)
- **Evaluation during training** is validation **loss** on a fixed stratified sample of **`evaluation.loss_sample_rows` (160)** validation rows (`train.loss_sample_rows`, falling back to `validation_sample_rows`; chosen by `evaluation.validation_sample.validation_sample`, stratified by line × reading mode, by a stable hash). It only draws the progress curve: **no early stopping is passed to ms-swift** and **`load_best_model_at_end: false`**. The run is divided into **`evaluations_per_run` (10)** checks (`_eval_interval`: ⌈optimizer steps ÷ 10⌉; without the setting, the configured `eval_steps` shrunk so a run gets at least 5). `_checkpoint_cadence` then saves a resume point at least every **`checkpoint_every_steps` (70)** steps — multiplied by the GPU count, since n GPUs run n× the steps in the same hour — evaluates on every k-th save (`eval_steps` = `save_steps` × k), and sets `save_total_limit` to the configured 4 × k + 1, so the limit counts evaluation checkpoints and resume points never push a candidate out. `metric_for_best_model: eval_loss`, `greater_is_better: false` let the trainer record the best-loss checkpoint, one selection candidate. **Loss never selects what ships** — see §3 below.
- **Staging:** the adapter and its `checkpoint-*` directories go to the RunPod staging volume under `staging/adapters/foundation/v{n}/` (unified; `staging/adapters/scope/{scope}/v{n}/` for a scoped run). Pushed to Blob by `package` (the checkpoint selection chose), or immediately with `finetune --push-adapters` (the best-loss checkpoint, else the last, before selection) — the adapter's weights and config only (`push_scoped_adapter`), never a checkpoint's resume state.
- **Manifest:** a `RunManifest` (IMPL-02) is written at status **`training`** before launch; `launch_and_record` flips it to `trained` when ms-swift returns, or **`failed`** when it raises, so the registry never claims weights a crashed run never wrote. It records the full config, data stats, LoB coverage, seed, git commit, corpus version, MinerU version, schema/prompt template versions, and the de-identification flag (recorded, not asserted, while de-identification is blocked — IMPL-05 §1). A model card is written beside it (`registry_utils/model_card.py`).
- **The YAML's `evaluation:` block** is read in three places. `build_training_config` passes `eval_strategy`, `save_strategy`, `metric_for_best_model`, `greater_is_better` and `load_best_model_at_end` directly, and derives `eval_steps`, `save_steps` and `save_total_limit` from `evaluations_per_run`, `checkpoint_every_steps` and `save_total_limit`. Staging reads `loss_sample_rows`. Checkpoint selection reads `validation_sample_rows` and the `selection_*` keys (§3). `early_stopping_patience` is still in the YAML and passed nowhere: ms-swift 3 accepts none.

Flags: `--corpus vN`, `--out-version vN`, `--tenant`, `--scope NAME` (defaults to unified), `--deepspeed zero2|zero3`, `--train-vit` (refused, see *Current implementation*), `--continue-from <checkpoint dir>`, `--dry-run`.

**Versioning rule (arch §12) — enforced in code:**
- **Major corpus expansion** → retrain **from the original HF base model** on the full accumulated corpus. Continued training on an existing LoRA compounds drift across cycles.
- **Minor incremental patch** → continuing from the current checkpoint is acceptable, **but promotion requires cross-type regression evidence** against the frozen golden eval set (IMPL-08). `--continue-from` sets `continued_from` on the manifest, which the gate reads.
- **`--continue-from` takes a checkpoint DIRECTORY, never a registry run-id.** It reaches ms-swift 3 as `adapters: [<dir>]` (it used to be `resume_from_checkpoint`, which restored the old run's optimizer, schedule and step), which reads a path; the directory must hold an `adapter_config.json`; given a run-id, ms-swift finds nothing, trains from base, and the manifest records a lineage that never happened. `assert_checkpoint_path` refuses any bare run-id of the shapes `common.run_ids` defines (`extractor-v2`, `extractor-v2.1`, scoped ids such as `policy-v2`, …), with or without a trailing slash or `./` prefix.

### 2. `training/merge.py`

`plan_merge` / `merge` fold **one** adapter — the checkpoint §11.2 selected, recorded on the plan — into the bf16 base with PEFT `merge_and_unload()`, on the staging volume. See IMPL-10 §1.

### 3. Checkpoint selection — `evaluation/checkpoint_eval.py` (arch §11.2)

Runs as the `checkpoint_eval` stage, between training and merge.

- **Candidates:** the last 3 checkpoints by step (`TRAILING_CANDIDATES`), plus the best-loss one. After a real run, `discover_checkpoints` reads them from ms-swift's versioned output directory (`…/v{n}/v0-<timestamp>/checkpoint-*`, the most recent run), keeping only checkpoints an evaluation fell on plus the last (resume points saved between evaluations are not candidates), and the best-loss checkpoint from `trainer_state.json`.
- **Scoring:** each candidate is applied as a **decoder LoRA** on the bf16 base in vLLM and generates over a fixed stratified sample of **`validation_sample_rows` (400)** rows of the scope's validation view, in all three reading modes, through `evaluation/validation_generation.py` — the row's own prompt with the golden answer removed, constrained as serving is, `validation_batch_rows` (96) rows per vLLM call — and is scored by the gate's own `build_report`. The winner has the best `field_normalized_match`; ties go to the later step. A candidate whose scoring raises is skipped and recorded, never scored zero.
- **Whole documents decide among the finalists** (`choose_on_documents`): the `selection_document_finalists` (2) best are scored on every validation row of `selection_document_modes` (`[ocr_plus_image]`; filtered by mode so every document stays whole) and judged on whole documents, windows merged as serving merges them. The best `document_field_normalized_match` wins among finalists whose `document_hallucination_rate` and `document_false_null_rate` are within `selection_guard_margin` (0.02) of the best finalist's. With fewer than 2 finalists configured, a near-tie under `selection_tie_break_margin` (0.015) is re-decided on the full split instead (`break_tie`). A mode filter matching no row is refused before the engine loads.
- **On a multi-GPU pod** the candidates are scored at once, one worker and vLLM engine per GPU (`parallel_vllm_scorer`), up to the run's training GPU count.
- **Why not loss:** averaged over every token, it is dominated by schema keys and JSON punctuation the model gets right in the first hundred steps. A checkpoint can improve on loss while getting worse at the values.

### 4. Per-type adapters — only through the §4.2 graduation gate

There is **no per-type training entrypoint** in the default topology. A per-type adapter returns only when a type has **≥500 labeled documents AND a measured >2 pp win outside the paired-bootstrap 95% CI**. Even then it trains on the **merged** foundation, decoder-only, and is applied at serving time as the request's one LoRA — never stacked, never merged. When the unified run moves to a new major version, every graduated adapter must be re-validated; `query_registry.adapters_depending_on()` (IMPL-02) produces the work list. **ACORD** stays one type with per-form schemas (arch §4b); per-form adapters are not considered until a form has 1000+ examples.

### 5. `training/data_collator.py`

**Override hook only.** ms-swift provides multimodal collation and `-100` label masking (system, image and OCR tokens masked; **loss only on the assistant JSON**). This file:
- documents that the framework default is in use;
- contains `assert_masking_correct` / `verify_batch`, which verify masking on a sample batch — the highest-value correctness check in the system, because broken masking trains the model to reproduce its own prompt and is invisible in loss curves;
- implements custom masking only if a genuine need arises (`custom_collator` raises until then).

### 6. `training/callbacks/early_stopping.py`

**Not in the launch path.** `swift_early_stopping_args` (which emitted `early_stopping_patience`) is no longer called: ms-swift 3 accepts no patience argument, and stopping early would only remove candidates from checkpoint selection, which chooses by generated field accuracy over the evaluation checkpoints. `EarlyStoppingState` (a field-F1 tracker) remains in the module, exercised by tests only; neither the launch path nor `checkpoint_eval` imports it.

### 7. `training/vit_gate.py` (decision helper — arch §3)

Given an eval report (IMPL-08) for a frozen-ViT run, decide whether the ViT escalation fires:

```
1. Train with the ViT frozen.
2. Evaluate on (a) the image-only eval subset and (b) the scanned-PDF eval subset.
3. IF image-only OR scanned accuracy is below target
   AND the errors are perception errors (misread characters, missed checkboxes)
   rather than schema/reasoning errors:
      → apply LoRA to the ViT and retrain.
4. ELSE keep it frozen.
```

- The **error-type distinction is the load-bearing part**: a character read correctly but placed in the wrong field is a decoder problem, and touching the ViT won't help. `classify_error` separates perception from schema/reasoning errors, and the gate reads that — not the accuracy number alone.
- **When the ViT is trained, LoRA is applied — never full fine-tuning** (arch §3). `--train-vit` configures a ViT LoRA; no code path enables full ViT fine-tuning.
- Emits a recommendation and rationale. **Does not auto-train.**

### 8. `training/sweep.py` — built; **execution waits for production volume**

The protocol is implemented and tested against a stub trainer. What waits is *running* it: a 9–12 run sweep against 25–30 documents per type mostly measures noise. `assert_enough_data` refuses below **200 documents per type**. It is deliberately **not a pipeline stage** — nine to twelve training runs is an operator decision.

| Phase | Sweeps (the unified run — `foundation` grid key) | Held fixed | Metric | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Learning rate** (first, highest impact) | `{5e-5, 1e-4, 2e-4}`, 1 epoch each | everything else | validation loss | 3 runs |
| **2 — Epochs** | `{2, 3, 4}` document passes, patience 2 | Phase 1 winner | validation field F1 | 3 runs |
| **3 — LoRA rank** (**only if F1 plateaus**; off by default) | `{32, 64, 128}`, rsLoRA on | Phases 1–2 winners | validation field F1 | 3 runs |

- The four materialized epoch files exist so the epoch phase compares the same data draws rather than regenerating them.
- The `per_type` grids in `configs/sweeps/` are dormant until a type graduates (§4 above).
- **Every sweep run writes a full `run_manifest.json`** with `is_sweep_run: true`. A candidate that produced no score is excluded from ranking, not defaulted.

## Constraints
- ms-swift is the entrypoint (locked). TRL `SFTTrainer` is a documented migration fallback only.
- **One adapter.** No code path trains a per-type adapter outside the §4.2 graduation gate, and none stacks two LoRAs.
- **ViT and mergers frozen** unless `--train-vit` — and then LoRA-on-ViT, never full fine-tune. The mergers are never a LoRA target.
- Every run writes a manifest — **no silent training, sweeps included**.
- Runs record (not yet assert) de-identification status; every run asserts its corpus is single-tenant.
- Vision budgets and sequence caps come from `configs/shared/` and must match data prep and serving (the §10.2 parity tests assert this).
- Seed is fixed, logged, and recorded in the manifest.

## Datasets, and the split that must stay split

The trainer receives **`--dataset` (the epoch files) and `--val_dataset` (val) as separate arguments**, with `split_dataset_ratio: 0.0`. Passing both to `--dataset` makes ms-swift treat the validation set as training data and carve its own eval split out of the union, so the validation loss — and the best-loss candidate — reads documents the model memorised, and nothing downstream can detect it.

**Argument rendering is part of this contract.** Two rendering rules are load-bearing:

- A `False` value renders as `--flag false`, never as an omitted flag. Dropping it silently disabled every option whose correct value is `False` — `freeze_vit=False` meant `--train-vit` never reached the trainer while the manifest recorded `vit_trainable: true`.
- A list renders as one argv element **per item**. Joining them gives `HfArgumentParser`'s `nargs="+"` one token containing every value, so the epoch files become one nonexistent filename and `lora_target_modules` matches no module.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] The unified run trains end-to-end on a tiny sample corpus and produces a loadable LoRA adapter (GPU milestone).
- [ ] `dataset` is the first `num_train_epochs` epoch files, ms-swift gets `num_train_epochs: 1`, and fewer files than epochs is refused.
- [ ] Every file training reads exists after a real dataset build (`tests/test_orchestration.py`).
- [ ] Train and val are passed as **separate** datasets; no path puts the validation split into `--dataset`.
- [ ] Under the default config the rendered CLI carries no quantization and no `bnb_4bit_*` argument; `load_in_4bit: true` switches to NF4 (`quant_method bnb`, `quant_bits 4`), and the manifest's `technique` follows.
- [ ] Target modules contain no `merger`; `freeze_aligner` is always true; `--train-vit` renders `--freeze_vit false` and records a ViT **LoRA**.
- [ ] `max_length` covers the largest cap of the scope's tasks, including the `by_doc_type` overrides of the types it trains.
- [ ] `group_by_length` renders `false`; a run gets `evaluations_per_run` evaluations, resume points no further apart than `checkpoint_every_steps` × GPUs, and a `save_total_limit` that keeps the last evaluation checkpoints; `load_best_model_at_end` is `false`.
- [ ] The trainer's validation file is the `loss_sample_rows` sample; checkpoint selection ranks on its own `validation_sample_rows` sample and decides among finalists on whole documents.
- [ ] The assistant span **includes** the end-of-turn token, so EOS is supervised.
- [ ] **Label masking verified**: loss only on assistant tokens; a mutated masking implementation fails the test.
- [ ] Every flag in the rendered command is one ms-swift 3's own parser accepts (Phase 0 spike `check_ms_swift` parses the exact command with `HfArgumentParser(TrainArguments)`).
- [ ] `--continue-from` refuses a run-id and marks the manifest so the gate demands cross-type regression evidence.
- [ ] Checkpoints are discovered from the versioned output directory after training, and selection scores them with the gate's metric, not loss.
- [ ] Adapter lands on the staging volume; the RunManifest lands in **Blob** with `artifacts.status: "staged"`, LoB coverage, and seed recorded.
- [ ] `vit_gate` fires only when errors are perception errors, never on schema/reasoning errors alone.
- [ ] `sweep.py` refuses below production volume, writes one manifest per candidate with `is_sweep_run: true`, conditions each phase on the previous winner, excludes unscored candidates, and leaves phase 3 off by default.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

**ms-swift 3 command** (`build_training_config`; `pyproject.toml` pins `ms-swift>=3.9,<4`): `model` (the
local base directory — see below — else the Hub id, with `model_revision` only for a Hub id), `use_hf`,
`dataset` (the first N epoch files), `val_dataset`, `train_type: lora`, `lora_rank`/`lora_alpha`/
`lora_dropout` (64 / 128 / 0.10), `target_modules` (`q/k/v/o_proj`, `gate/up/down_proj`), `use_rslora`,
`attn_impl: flash_attn`, `torch_dtype: bfloat16`, `num_train_epochs: 1`, `gradient_accumulation_steps` for
the GPU count, `dataset_num_proc`, `use_logits_to_keep`, `padding_free`, `group_by_length: false`,
`freeze_vit: true`, `freeze_aligner: true`, `split_dataset_ratio: 0.0` (validation is ours, never carved
from train), `eval_strategy`/`save_strategy: steps` with the derived `eval_steps`/`save_steps`/
`save_total_limit`, `load_best_model_at_end: false`, `metric_for_best_model: eval_loss`, `report_to: none`,
`adapters` for `--continue-from`, `truncation_strategy: delete` with `strict: true` (a row over `max_length`
is an error, never silently shortened, and never silently swapped for another row). No `model_type`
(ms-swift infers it). `--train-vit` is **refused**: the LoRA targets are decoder projection names and
ms-swift applies `freeze_vit=false` only when expanding `all-linear`, so it trained the same decoder-only
adapter while recording `vit_trainable`.

**Several GPUs** (`distributed.gpus: auto` = every visible GPU, or a number; `training_gpus`): data-parallel,
each GPU taking whole rows. The launch env gets `NPROC_PER_NODE=n` (ms-swift starts one process per GPU) and
the command `ddp_find_unused_parameters: false`; accumulation shrinks so the effective batch, the step count
and the schedule are those of one GPU.

**Resume.** An unfinished run picks up where it stopped. `resume_point` finds the latest complete
`checkpoint-*` (trainer state, adapter and optimizer state) under the staging directory of a run without
`training_complete.json` — the marker `launch_and_record` writes only when ms-swift returns successfully, and
what "trained" means to the pipeline (ms-swift creates the directory when training starts).
`assert_resumable` refuses the resume when `run_fingerprint.json` differs: corpus version and manifest hash,
scope, LoRA rank/alpha, learning rate and schedule, epochs, effective batch, target modules, and a hash of the
training config (minus `distributed` and the per-device batch) and the shared vision and sequence configs.
Resumed, ms-swift gets `resume_from_checkpoint`, the old run directory as `output_dir` and
`add_version: false` — the same run carried on, unlike `--continue-from`, which starts a new run from the
weights.

**Staging** (`training/stage_data.py`): the corpus rows are rewritten into ms-swift's own format —
string content with `<image>` placeholders and an `images` list — as local JSONL under
`staging/train-data/{scope}/v{n}/`, and their page images are fetched once into a per-corpus-version
cache (`staging/train-images/{tenant}/{corpus}/`, atomic unique `.part` downloads). The validation rows are
narrowed to the loss sample before conversion (`val_filter`, which needs the line and mode the conversion
drops); every row's output reservation is kept beside the files for the length check (`output_caps`); a row
whose task has another pixel budget than the run's is refused before any download. Older corpus versions'
caches are pruned (the current one and the most recent other are kept). A run is refused if its run id
already exists in any state but `training`/`failed`, and a real run is refused when its scope has no
validation rows.

**Before launch, on the pod**:
1. **Length** (`_assert_rows_fit`): every staged row is measured with the real tokenizer and the real resize
   rule (smart_resize, factor 32) against `max_length` **and** against its task's reserved output tokens
   (`training/length_check.py`); any over is refused with the rows named.
2. **Masking** (`_assert_masking`): the first staged rows are encoded with ms-swift's own template
   (`_masking_encoder`, ms-swift imported in-process) and `data_collator.verify_staged_rows` asserts exactly
   the assistant content plus `<|im_end|>` is supervised. ms-swift's import replaces the root log handlers,
   so `common.logging_setup.reassert()` follows it; without it every later stage in the process — checkpoint
   selection included — logged nothing.
3. **Where**: `assert_on_pod` refuses a real launch without CUDA (`common/gpu.py`), without the staging
   volume, or when `model.local_dir` is configured and holds no model. `FIDEON_ALLOW_OFF_POD=1` opts a
   non-RunPod GPU host in.

Earlier, before anything is staged: `training.data_mix.assert_corpus_mix` refuses a scope whose input-mode
mix differs from the corpus's `modality_mix_target`, and the training stage refuses a scope that would train
one layout family on two output shapes (`common.scopes.assert_one_output_shape`, read against the corpus's
`policy_line_counts`: a family part-way onto the common model, such as casualty_fleet with gl moved).

**Pixel budget**: one budget per run from `common.config.pixel_budget(scope.tasks)`, passed to ms-swift as
both `MAX_PIXELS`/`MIN_PIXELS` and `IMAGE_MAX_TOKEN_NUM`/`IMAGE_MIN_TOKEN_NUM` (whole 32×32 tokens), the
same function the vLLM engine takes its budget from.

**Base model**: loaded from `model.local_dir` in `configs/base_model.yaml` (default `/workspace/models`;
the directory itself, a folder named after the model, or a Hugging Face cache snapshot), overridable with
`FIDEON_BASE_MODEL_DIR`. The manifest still records `model_id@revision` as the identity.

**Environment**: the ms-swift subprocess gets the pod environment minus credentials it has no use for
(Azure, RunPod, anything named `*SECRET*`, `*PASSWORD*`, `*API_KEY*`, …); `HF_TOKEN` is kept.

**Manifest**: `data_stats.train_examples` is the actual row count of the epochs used; `tenant_id` is
recorded. A scoped run records its view's `val_examples` and `test_examples`, `examples_by_line`, and
`data_mix` (the shares configured and reached per line, documents rested, the render rotation applied);
the model card shows the same.

**On the pod** `training.train` and `training.sweep` run detached in tmux like every long job.

**Scopes by line of business** (`training/corpus_view.py`, `materialize`): a scope with `lines` filters the
one corpus by line as well as type — a row is kept only when all of its lines are in scope
(`Scope.covers_lob`: a personal + commercial package is not a personal-lines document, nor is a package of
common-model lines; a row with no line is outside the scope). The view is written under
`train/scope/{name}/` and `{val,test}/scope/{name}/`; train, val (hence checkpoint selection and
calibration) and the test count all follow. On the train rows, from the first epoch's documents and the same
for every epoch, in this order:

1. **Data mix** (`training/data_mix.py`, `data_mix:` in the scope's config — `personal_lines.yaml`): per
   line, `synthetic_fraction` and `scanned_share` of its train documents. Every real document trains;
   synthetic ones are taken one twin of every seed before a second of any (seeded); when a scanned or digital
   pool runs short, fewer synthetic documents are taken rather than the scanned share missed. A per-line
   `null` takes the scope default; a scope default of `null` leaves the share unsteered. All values are `null`
   today, so every document trains. A `scanned_share` on rows without `is_scanned` is refused.
2. **Render rotation** (`render_rotation: true`, `plan_render_rotation`): a twin delivered digital and
   scanned with one gold trains in one render per epoch and the other the next; half of a seed's twins start
   in each render. Real documents and single-render twins train every epoch; a line that rotation would take
   under the line-balance target keeps both renders.
3. **Line balance** (`line_balance: {min_documents: 100, max_repeat: 3}`, `line_repeats`): a line with fewer
   documents per epoch than the target (`min_documents`, capped at the largest line) has its rows repeated
   ⌈target ÷ documents⌉ times, at most 3, in every epoch file.

Validation and test are never sampled, rotated or repeated. None of the three applies to the unified scope,
which reads the corpus files as they are and warns when they are configured. **casualty_fleet** (`lines:
[gl]`, `training_config: unified`) is the CGL adapter, trained in its own tenant on the CGL bundles
(`--tenant`): one line, real documents only, so the three change nothing there. A scope without `lines`
(`policy`, `lossrun`) filters by type alone.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line (commit f5668a4).
- Run type and id: scoped runs (`scoped`, `{lineage}-v{n}`) beside unified `extractor-v{n}`;
  `assert_checkpoint_path` refuses every `common.run_ids` shape, scoped ids included.
- Config: scopes name their training config; `personal_lines.yaml` extends unified with its `data_mix`.
- Optimisation and memory: accumulation derived from the GPU count; `dataset_num_proc`; `group_by_length` is
  now false (`length_grouped_sampling: false`), with the reason; `max_length` scoped to the scope's tasks.
- Datasets: scoped epoch and validation files; the staged validation file is the loss sample.
- Evaluation: replaced "≥5 evaluations, `eval_steps` = `save_steps`, `load_best_model_at_end`" with
  `evaluations_per_run` 10, `_checkpoint_cadence` (`checkpoint_every_steps` 70 × GPUs, `save_total_limit`
  counted in evaluation checkpoints), `load_best_model_at_end: false`, no early stopping, and
  `loss_sample_rows` 160 for the trainer's validation file; corrected the "every evaluation key reaches
  ms-swift" bullet (`early_stopping_patience` is unused).
- `--push-adapters` publishes adapter files only; model card noted; `--scope` flag listed.
- §3 checkpoint selection: candidates exclude resume points; ranking on the 400-row sample; whole-document
  finalists (2, `ocr_plus_image`) with hallucination/false-null guards; near-tie fallback; parallel scoring.
- §6: `EarlyStoppingState` is not used by `checkpoint_eval` (tests only).
- Current implementation: full ms-swift argument list, multi-GPU launch, resume with fingerprint and
  completion marker, staging details, `_assert_rows_fit`/`_assert_masking` with `logging_setup.reassert()`,
  `assert_corpus_mix` and `assert_one_output_shape`, scoped manifest stats, and the scope view's data mix,
  render rotation and line balance; casualty_fleet added.
- Checklist: scoped `max_length`, evaluation cadence and the loss/selection samples.

---

# IMPL-07_inference_core

# SPEC 07 — Inference Core (Shared Model-Runner Primitive)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02. **This module exists to break the cycle between evaluation, calibration, serving, and testing** — they all need one shared way to run a model version over a document. Build it here, once; everything downstream imports it.
>
> **Naming hazard (master §0.1):** in this folder `IMPL-07` is the inference core. The architecture's "**Fideon SPEC_07 Stage 3**" is the external production **audit gate** — a different document. They are unrelated.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §5 (logprobs → field spans), §6 (modality modes), §7 (prompt template identity, multi-page inputs), §11 (resolution cap).

## Why this module exists

Evaluation (IMPL-08), calibration (IMPL-09), serving (IMPL-11), and testing (IMPL-12) must all run the model **identically** — same prompt assembly, same generation config, same logprob capture — or their numbers won't agree and "test == prod" breaks. Divergence between training-time and inference-time prompts is the single most common cause of post-fine-tuning degradation, and it is invisible in training metrics because it only manifests at serving time (arch §7). That shared primitive lives here, with **no dependency on calibration, classification, or routing** — those are higher-level concerns layered on later.

## Goal

A clean, low-level inference engine: given a resolved model version + a prepared document input + a doc_type, produce raw generated JSON text and per-token logprobs. No confidence calibration, no classification, no routing here — just correct, reproducible generation.

## Deliverables

### 1. `inference_core/model_runner.py`
- `load_model(tag, client, *, doc_type=None, backend=None, quant_format=None, config=None, backend_impl=None, adapter=None, label=None)` → `LoadedModel` — resolves the tag (`base` is first-class, then `v1`, `v2`, …) to concrete artifacts via IMPL-02 `resolve_model_version` and builds the configured backend. The vLLM backend serves the **merged** model or the base; a bare adapter stack has to be merged first (IMPL-10). Backend-swappable: vLLM for serving and every pipeline generation pass, HF for local eval, GGUF for the offline/edge path only (a stub that raises until llama.cpp support for Qwen3-VL with an `mmproj` file is confirmed).
  - **Base + adapter, not merged.** `adapter=` (accepted only with `tag="base"`) names a LoRA directory — a training checkpoint or a run's adapter folder, local or a Blob prefix copied once to `ADAPTER_CACHE`. `prepare_adapter` refuses one with no `adapter_config.json`, a rank above `max_lora_rank`, or an engine configured with `enable_lora: false` (the bare base would otherwise answer under the adapter's name). It becomes `LoadedModel.default_adapter`: applied **per request** to every request that names no adapter of its own, never folded into the weights. Results are filed under `label`, or `adapter_label()` (`base+<run>-<checkpoint>`). `without_adapter(model)` answers from the same engine with the base alone, and `NO_ADAPTER` (`""`) does the same for one request.
- `generate(model, messages, *, adapter=None, want_logprobs=True, json_schema=None, max_new_tokens=None)` → `Generation` (`text`, `tokens`, `token_logprobs`, `finish_reason`, `generation_fingerprint`, `latency_ms`).
  - **Logprobs are mandatory-capable** — vLLM supports them natively and HF via `generate(output_scores=True)`. Calibration (IMPL-09) is built entirely on them, so a backend that cannot return them is not a valid backend for anything but smoke testing. `load_model` refuses a backend that cannot return them; `Generation.assert_logprobs()` refuses an answer that came back without them.
  - `json_schema` constrains this one request (per request, because it is per document type and per window); `max_new_tokens` is this request's answer cap, never above the configured `max_new_tokens`. A generation that stops at the limit is logged (`Generation.truncated()`).
- `generate_batch(model, requests, *, adapter=None, want_logprobs=True)` — `requests` is `[(messages, json_schema)]` or `[(messages, json_schema, max_new_tokens)]`. The vLLM backend runs them in **one `engine.chat` call** (continuous batching), each with its own sampling parameters. One result per request, in order: a failed request is a `ModelRunnerError` in its slot, never raised, and a call that fails as a whole puts its exception in every slot. Backends that cannot batch run the requests one at a time.
- Deterministic given seed + greedy/temperature settings; every result carries `RunnerConfig.fingerprint()` (backend, temperature, top_p, `max_new_tokens`, seed, resolution cap, `structured_outputs`, `logprobs_mode`, and `repeatable` when on) so an output can be reproduced.
- **Structured outputs.** The vLLM engine is built with `structured_outputs_config=STRUCTURED_OUTPUTS_ENGINE` — `{"backend": "xgrammar", "disable_any_whitespace": True}`: no free whitespace between JSON tokens, so a drifting model cannot write newlines until the limit, and the separators are `json.dumps`' own (`", "`, `": "`), as the training answers are written. Each request carries its schema as `guided_decoding=GuidedDecodingParams(json=schema)`. `logprobs_mode` (`raw_logprobs`) is an engine argument, asserted where the engine is built (`RunnerConfig.assert_logprobs_are_the_models_own`), so confidence reads the model's own distribution, not the post-mask one. The sampled token is looked up by its token id (under masking it need not be rank 1), the stopping `<|im_end|>` token is dropped, and `assert_tokens_reconstruct` refuses tokens that do not concatenate back to the text — the invariant span mapping rests on.
- **Adapters.** vLLM applies **one LoRA per request** (`LoRARequest`), hot-swapped without reloading the base; `max_loras` caps how many stay resident, not how many are active. Loading with no adapter must also work — it is the classifier's low-confidence fallback path (IMPL-11) and the base-model reading. The HF backend binds adapters at load (Foundation, then the per-type adapter, in training order) and refuses a request naming a different adapter.
- **Repeatable mode** (`generation.repeatable`, off by default): sets `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1` before vLLM is imported (batch-invariant kernels where the installed vLLM has them, a warning where it has none) and turns prefix caching off, so a request's answer does not depend on what shares its batch.
- `to_vllm_messages` rewrites the ms-swift `{"type": "image"}` parts corpus rows and requests carry into `image_pil` / `image_url` parts `LLM.chat` accepts.
- `inference_core/vllm_patches.patch_qwen3_vl_lora` runs before vLLM is imported: on vLLM 0.11.0 it points Qwen3-VL's LoRA exclusion at the engine's own module names, so LoRA stays off the vision tower (otherwise the first request with an image fails in `_lora_shrink`), and it keeps the V1 engine core in this process (`VLLM_ENABLE_V1_MULTIPROCESSING=0`) so the patch reaches it.

### 2. `inference_core/input_builder.py`
- `build_messages(doc_type, image_paths, ocr_pages, modality_mode, *, acord_form=None, lob=None, sections=None, assistant_content=None, check_resolution_parity=True, page_numbers=None, total_pages=None)` → `BuiltMessages`: the exact chat-format `messages` (master §9), rendered through **`common.prompts`**. `lob` selects a policy's per-line schema, `sections` the slice of it one window asks for (`configs/schema_sections.yaml`), and `assistant_content` the golden JSON when `build_training_row` builds a corpus row. This is the single place prompt + image + OCR get assembled, so dataset build, evaluation, serving, and testing are guaranteed identical.
  - **`ocr_pages` is one markdown string per image, not one joined blob.** A mismatched length is refused: a short or long list shifts every page's text onto the wrong image, and every value after the shift is then attributed to the wrong page.
  - The user turn is **interleaved** — `[img_1][<page 1 of M> + md_1][img_2][<page 2 of M> + md_2]…` — so which text belongs to which image is positional rather than something the model infers. Joining destroyed that, and (because a blank line ends a Markdown table) split every table crossing a page boundary in two.
  - `page_numbers` / `total_pages` let a routed request label pages truthfully (`<page 9 of 20>`). The gap in the numbering is what tells the model it holds a fragment, so fields living on unshown pages are absent rather than missing.
  - `image_only` carries the markers and no markdown. The guarantee is *no OCR text*, not *no text at all*.
- Handles `image_only` (omit the OCR block, use the image-only prompt with its explicit "no OCR provided" declaration) vs `ocr_plus_image` (arch §6). `image_only` with OCR text, or another mode without it, is refused.
- **Multi-page inputs:** accepts an ordered list of page images and emits them as ordered `image` blocks, each followed by its own page's text. **Page order is positionally meaningful** under Interleaved-MRoPE (arch §3) — ordering must be explicit and stable, never set-like.
- `shown_pages(messages)` and `page_total(messages)` read a prompt's page numbers and the document's page count back from its `<page i of N>` markers — what validation generation and serving bound a window's `page_ref` with (§4).
- Applies the **resolution cap from config** to any image passed in — identical to the cap used at corpus build (arch §11). Assert the cap matches `configs/base_model.yaml` (`common.config.assert_resolution_parity`); a silent mismatch between training prep and inference is a correctness bug, not a tuning knob. The vLLM engine additionally receives the **pixel budget** as `mm_processor_kwargs` from `common.config.pixel_budget()`, the function training takes its budget from.
- A page whose OCR text is blank gets `EMPTY_PAGE_TEXT` — the same placeholder in training rows and serving requests.
- Exposes `prompt_template_version`, `schema_version` and `prompt_fingerprint` on the built message set so callers can verify they match the corpus the model was trained on; `BuiltMessages.assert_matches_corpus(manifest)` compares them with the corpus manifest's per-selector `schema_versions`.

### 3. `inference_core/span_map.py`
- `map_field_spans(generated_text, tokens, token_logprobs, *, strict=False)` → for each JSON field path — **including nested fields and individual list rows** (e.g. `claims[0].amount`) — the token span and its logprobs.
- This is the raw material calibration (IMPL-09) turns into confidence. Pure and testable; contains **no calibration logic itself**.
- Must handle: values spanning multiple tokens, `null` values, numeric vs string values, and rows inside arrays. A field whose span cannot be mapped is reported explicitly (`FieldSpan.mapped`, `unmapped_fields`) rather than silently dropped — an unmapped field would otherwise get no confidence and look like a clean extraction.
- **A string's span is its own characters, not its quotes** (an empty string keeps them): a quote shares its token with the JSON around it (`": "`, `",`), whose near-certain logprob says the structure was predictable, not that the value was read.
- Canonical envelopes are re-keyed by `common.canonical.collapse_spans`: `policy.effective_date.raw` / `.parsed` / `.page_ref[i]` become `policy.effective_date`, carrying **`raw`'s span** — the reading of the page, written first — with `parsed`'s standing in only when nothing was read; `page_ref` tokens are dropped.
- `list_field_paths` and `row_count` give the row-completeness signal (IMPL-09) the rows that were generated.

### 4. `inference_core/runner_config.py`
- Central generation + backend config (`RunnerConfig`: backend, temperature, top_p, `max_new_tokens`, seed, logprobs / `top_logprobs`, `logprobs_mode`, `structured_outputs`, `repeatable`, `max_seq_len`, GPU memory share, LoRA settings), loaded from `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml` by `load_runner_config()`. One source of truth so eval, serving, and testing cannot drift on generation parameters either. Logprobs off is refused; a non-greedy temperature warns.
- Current values: greedy (`temperature: 0.0`), `max_new_tokens: 12288` (at least every task's reserved answer), `max_seq_len: 32768`, `structured_outputs: true`, `logprobs_mode: raw_logprobs`, `repeatable: false`, `enable_lora: true`, `max_loras: 4`, `max_lora_rank: 64`. The same file's `generation.validation_batch_rows: 96` is read by evaluation's whole-split generation (IMPL-08), not by `RunnerConfig`.
- **Answer caps per task.** Callers pass each request's cap from `common.config.answer_cap(task, doc_type)` — the task's reserved `max_output_tokens` in `configs/shared/sequence.yaml`, which training targets are refused above, so a longer generation is a loop, not an answer: `policy_schedule` 12,288, `lossrun_rows` 8,192, `policy_declarations` 4,096, policy `extract` 3,072, `policy_endorsements` 3,072, the default 2,048.
- **Page bounds on the decoding schema.** Callers constrain with `common.schemas.with_page_bounds(schema, page_count, pages=shown_pages(messages))`: every `page_ref` is held to the pages the request shows — a range when they are consecutive, an `enum` when scattered (up to `MAX_PAGE_ENUM` = 32) — with at most that many entries, so a window cites only its own pages, as every training target does, and cannot count past the last page.
