For decoding only; the schema rendered into the prompt stays the one training showed.

## Constraints
- **No imports from** calibration, serving, evaluation, or testing (they import this, not vice-versa).
- Backend-agnostic interface so the same calls work in every context.
- Resolution cap and prompt rendering come from config/`common.prompts` — never inline.
- No PII in logs (never log generated field values or OCR text).

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] `load_model("v2")` resolves + loads a version's merged weights; `load_model("base", adapter=...)` serves base + a LoRA applied per request, not merged; base-only loading also works.
- [ ] `build_messages(...)` yields **byte-identical** structure for eval, serving, and testing given the same inputs.
- [ ] Each page image is immediately followed by that page's own text, and every text block carries its `<page N of M>` marker.
- [ ] A **routed subset is a subsequence of the full document** — the same blocks, fewer pairs — so a long policy is served in the shape it trained on. Comparing only *system* prompts does not catch this; the user turn must be compared too.
- [ ] A mismatch between the page-image count and the page-text count is refused.
- [ ] Two pages' markdown is never concatenated, so a table crossing a page boundary is not split.
- [ ] `build_messages(...)` rendering matches what IMPL-05 wrote into the corpus for the same doc_type + modality_mode (round-trip test against a corpus row).
- [ ] Multi-page input produces ordered image blocks; reversing the input order changes the output message order.
- [ ] Resolution cap mismatch between `base_model.yaml` and `vllm_serving.yaml` raises.
- [ ] `generate(...)` returns text + aligned token logprobs; `generate_batch(...)` returns one result per request in order, with a failure in its own slot.
- [ ] `map_field_spans(...)` correctly maps a scalar field, a `null` field, and a `claims[i].amount` list-row field on a sample generation; an unmappable field is reported, not dropped.
- [ ] Module imports without pulling in calibration/serving/eval/testing.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

- **vLLM backend**: refuses to build an engine without CUDA (`common.gpu.require_cuda`); passes
  `mm_processor_kwargs={"min_pixels", "max_pixels"}` from `pixel_budget()`; `close()` releases the engine
  (tears down vLLM's parallel state, collects, empties the CUDA cache) and `release_model(model)` calls it.
  The pipeline releases each engine before the next stage loads one — calibration per format and the golden
  eval each load a model on one card; checkpoint selection loads the base once per process, with one worker
  process and engine per GPU when training used several (IMPL-08).
- **HF backend**: `device_map="cuda"`, never `"auto"` (which silently offloads layers to the CPU when VRAM
  runs short); refuses without CUDA.
- **Base weights** resolve to the pod's local copy (`common.config.base_model_source()`); the registry used
  to hand vLLM the identity string `model_id@revision`, which no loader accepts.
- **Base + adapter callers**: checkpoint selection applies each candidate checkpoint as the request's LoRA on
  one base engine (IMPL-08); `testing/compare_models.py` loads `load_model("base", adapter=...)` and reads
  the base through `without_adapter`.
- **Dates**: generations are post-processed to `MM/DD/YYYY` (`common.canonical.with_output_dates`); a field is
  a date by a whole word of its name (`date`, `dates`, `dated`, `dob`), list elements included.
- **Pinned**: vLLM `==0.11.0` on both the training and the serving pod — calibration is fitted on the logprobs
  one build produces, so serving runs the same build. The engine already takes `structured_outputs_config`;
  the per-request constraint is still 0.11's `GuidedDecodingParams`, which has to move to vLLM's
  structured-outputs request parameters before the pin is raised, and the Qwen3-VL LoRA patch is only needed
  (and only applied) on 0.11.0.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- `load_model` signature corrected; added base + adapter loading (`adapter=`, `prepare_adapter`, `default_adapter`, `adapter_label`, `without_adapter`, `NO_ADAPTER`); "Foundation + per-type LoRA stacking" replaced by one LoRA per request (vLLM) and load-time binding (HF).
- `generate` signature and `Generation` fields corrected; added per-request `json_schema` / `max_new_tokens` and `generate_batch` (one `engine.chat` call per batch, failures per slot).
- Added structured outputs (xgrammar, `disable_any_whitespace`), `raw_logprobs` asserted at engine build, token-id lookup, end-of-turn token drop and token reconstruction check.
- Added repeatable mode, `to_vllm_messages`, and the vLLM 0.11.0 Qwen3-VL LoRA patch (`vllm_patches`).
- `build_messages` signature updated (`lob`, `sections`, `assistant_content`); removed the stale "concatenated OCR text" wording; added `shown_pages` / `page_total` and `assert_matches_corpus`.
- Span mapping: string spans exclude quotes; `collapse_spans` takes `raw`'s span.
- `runner_config`: listed the real fields and current values (`max_new_tokens` 12,288, `max_seq_len` 32,768); added per-task answer caps (`answer_cap`, policy schedule 12,288) and decoding page bounds (`with_page_bounds`).
- Current implementation: checkpoint selection's per-GPU engines, base + adapter callers, and the pin note updated.

---

# IMPL-08_evaluation

# SPEC 08 — Evaluation (Metrics + Promotion Gating)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-06, IMPL-07. Uses the shared inference core (IMPL-07) for all model runs — no forward dependency on serving or testing.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §15 (**metric definitions + LoB metric**), §0b (LoB gating), §3 (ViT gate inputs), §5 (list-field recall), §13 step 6 (the gate is a hard stop), §12 (gate result recorded in the manifest).

## Goal

Evaluate any model/adapter version against the **frozen golden eval set** and gate promotion so no regression ships.

## The gate is a hard stop (arch §13)

A candidate is promoted only if it passes **every** gating metric for the relevant document type(s): the absolute floors, and paired-bootstrap non-inferiority against the current production version (arch v2.1 §15). **There is no `--force` flag.** The one exception path is a **recorded written override** (arch v2.1 §15.5): a named approver, a written reason and the gates it waives, stored in the gate decision and the release bundle. `package` reads the recorded decision and refuses a version whose gate did not pass.

## Deliverables

### 1. `common/normalize.py` (shared normalizer — **built in IMPL-01**, consumed here)
> **Moved to IMPL-01.** `common/normalize.py` is now built in IMPL-01 alongside the other `common/` helpers, because **IMPL-04 `derive_aliases` needs it too** — and IMPL-04 builds before IMPL-08. It is a pure, dependency-free string utility, so it belongs with the rest of `common/`. This spec **consumes** it; it no longer creates it.

Field-type normalizers make comparison reflect correctness rather than formatting (arch §15). Insurance fields specifically need this or you under-count correct extractions:
- **Dates**: `01/01/2026` == `2026-01-01`
- **Currency**: `$1,200.00` == `1200.0`
- **Entity names**: `Acme Mfg LLC` ≈ `ACME MANUFACTURING LLC`
- Policy/claim numbers: whitespace and separator normalization; a number read as `1.0` is the identifier `1`.
- Two numbers compare as numbers whatever the field is called (`957` == `957.0`).

**Typed comparison.** Every scoring call site compares through `evaluation.metrics.field_accuracy.values_agree(expected, got, field_path)`: `common.normalize.values_match` with the type the schema declares for that field (`scoring_kind` — a common-model `MoneyValue` as money, a `YearValue` or count as a number, a date, an identifier), falling back to the field's name only where nothing is declared. Exact match never exceeds the normalised match.

One definition of "matches", applied **consistently in the promotion gate, checkpoint selection, calibration fitting, the testing routine (IMPL-12 — `testing.comparison.field_report`, written into test outputs as `all_fields`, grades every schema field with `values_agree`), and alias derivation (IMPL-04)** — divergence silently changes what "correct" means between them.

**Grounding** — whether a value is printed, and on which pages — has one definition too, `common/grounding.py`, shared by the data audit, the OCR-agreement calibration feature (IMPL-09) and the hallucination metric: case, punctuation and line breaks ignored; a value is on a page as a phrase on word boundaries, as every one of its words (across lines and table columns), or — an amount or a count — as the same figure in another format. `ground(raw, cited, pages)` returns `on_cited_page`, `on_other_page`, `not_printed`, or `unchecked` (shorter than `MIN_CHECKABLE_CHARS` = 3, no page text, or a cited page with no text).

### 2. `evaluation/metrics/` — one module per metric, each pure and testable

| Where (`evaluation/…`) | Metric | What it catches (arch §15) |
|---|---|---|
| `metrics/field_accuracy.score_fields` | Exact + normalized match, per-field and aggregate, by the schema's declared type (`values_agree`) | Core extraction accuracy |
| `metrics/field_accuracy.score_list_field` | Precision/recall/F1 for list fields (claims, schedule rows) | Precision/recall on repeating structures |
| `metrics/field_accuracy.score_list_field` (recall) | **Row completeness** — extracted row count vs ground truth; missed-row rate | Whole rows silently missed — a dropped Loss Run claim is invisible to per-value confidence (arch §5) |
| `metrics/coverage_metrics.score_schema_validity` | Parse + validate against the doc_type schema | Structural reliability; mirrors the Fideon SPEC_07 Stage 3 audit gate |
| `metrics/coverage_metrics.expected_calibration_error` | Expected Calibration Error on confidence vs correctness | Whether the confidence numbers are trustworthy, not just the extractions |
| `run_eval` — the `noisy_ocr` subset | Scored on the **deliberately-noisy-OCR subset** | Did the model correctly override bad OCR using the image? |
| `run_eval` — `image_only` / `scanned` subsets; `training/vit_gate.classify_error` on every error record | Separate accuracy for the `image_only` and `scanned` subsets | Confirms the second production pathway works; **feeds the ViT escalation gate** (IMPL-06) |
| `doc_type_classifier_accuracy` — not applicable while the corpus builds no classify rows | Doc-type + ACORD-form classification accuracy | Whether the right adapter/prompt/schema is even selected — **a classifier at 92% caps the whole system at 92%** (arch §4a) |
| `metrics/coverage_metrics.score_lob` | **`line_of_business` detection accuracy, overall and per LoB value** | The VLM is the fallback LoB detector when L1/L2 miss (arch §0b) |
| `metrics/confusable.score_alias_accuracy` (not yet called by `score_subset`); `metrics/unseen_labels.label_split` | Field accuracy **sliced by the observed surface label**; on common-model lines, `seen_label_field_match` / `unseen_label_field_match` (labels training showed vs never showed) | Whether the canonical mapping generalises across phrasings, or only works on the dominant one (master §1.4) |
| `metrics/confusable.score_misattribution` | Rate at which a **confusable entity's value is returned as the canonical field** | The failure that produces confident, well-formed, wrong extractions — a certificate holder returned as `insured_name` |
| `release_measurements` (golden eval) | P95 latency per adapter of each generation round, peak GPU memory | Production feasibility, not just accuracy — recorded in the eval report and the release bundle, not gated |
| `metrics/extraction_faults` — `score_false_nulls`, `score_hallucinations`, `score_page_refs`, `score_page_selection` | False-null rate, hallucination rate (schema fields; `additional_fields` apart), page_ref exact rate / precision / recall, page-selection recall | A value on the page emitted as null; a value printed on no page sent (looked for on the pages it cites); a right value citing the wrong pages; pages with fields that routing dropped |
| `metrics/common_model.CommonModelTally` | `reference_accuracy` (links), `coverage_code_accuracy`, `coverage_code_recall`, `additional_fields_recall` / `_precision` | Common-model lines: which unit a coverage applies to, whether it got the right code, the overflow scored apart from the core fields |
| `metrics/lossrun_table.table_f1` | Claims matched by claim number (or, unnumbered, by loss date) with total incurred within a cent, micro-averaged | Loss Run table reading as a whole |
| `metrics/auto_accept.score_auto_accept` | `auto_accept_error_rate`: of the values delivered without a review flag, the share that were wrong | What the release's review thresholds promise, measured on documents they never saw |
| `run_eval.score_subset` | `field_precision` / `field_recall` / `field_f1`, `field_accuracy_by_lob`, `weakest_fields`, `worst_line_field_match`, `error_summary` | Writing little but well vs everything half wrong; where accuracy is won and lost; one line hiding behind the others' volume |

**Alias accuracy specifics (master §1.4).** Joins predictions to each eval document's `field_provenance` (IMPL-04) and reports accuracy per canonical field × surface label. This turns an unhelpful aggregate into an actionable one: *0.94 on "Named Insured", 0.61 on "Applicant"* tells you the mapping is not generalising and names the documents to go collect. **Reported, not gating** — rare aliases have too little support for a stable gate, and gating on them would block promotion on noise. The report currently carries the seen/unseen-label split (computed when the eval metadata carries the training set's seen labels and the page text); `score_alias_accuracy` exists but `score_subset` does not call it.

**`confusable.py` specifics.** For each canonical field, check whether the returned value matches the document's value for one of that field's registered **confusables** instead. Requires the eval golden labels to carry the confusable entities' values, so the frozen eval set must include the confusable co-occurrence documents from IMPL-05. **This is a gating metric.** Ordinary field accuracy already penalises a wrong value — but misattribution is worth isolating because it is systematic rather than random: it means the model has collapsed two distinct entities, it will keep doing so, and the output is fluent and confident enough to pass every structural check.

**LoB accuracy specifics (arch §15):** LoB accuracy is reported as **its own metric, measured per LoB value**, and is **never averaged into overall field accuracy** — a class that is rare in the corpus must not hide inside a healthy-looking aggregate. It is a gating metric. It scores the extracted `line_of_business` field, which a canonical policy label does not carry, so it is not applicable to a policy-only scope. Reading a policy's line when no caller supplied one is a different number, measured apart (*Current implementation*).

**Mode accuracy specifics:** must also classify errors as **perception** (misread characters, missed checkboxes) vs **schema/reasoning** (right value, wrong field), because IMPL-06's `vit_gate` needs that distinction, not just the accuracy number. `classify_error` also names **omission** (nothing produced) and **invented** (a value where the label has none and no other field holds it); invented errors are counted in the report's error totals and left out of the ViT error mix.

### 3. `evaluation/run_eval.py`
- Given `--model vN` (resolved via IMPL-02) + the frozen golden eval set, run inference **through the serving pipeline** (`golden_eval.evaluate_version` → `serving.pipeline.extract`, built on the IMPL-07 inference core — so windows, merge, date formatting and calibrated confidence are what is measured) across all eval docs and all three modes, compute every metric, and write the report where the gate reads it (`paths.eval_report(version, scope=...)` — `eval-reports/{version}/summary.json`, or `…/scope/{name}/summary.json` for a scoped run). Broken down **per doc_type and per subset** inside it (`by_doc_type`).
- Runs the eval subsets explicitly: `image_only`, `scanned`, `noisy_ocr`, `long_policy`, plus the full set — and `held_out_carrier` when the set holds documents of a carrier held out of train and val (reported as `held_out_carrier_match`, never gated).
- Records per-document error records (field, expected, got, error class, mode, scanned, line) so `vit_gate` and failure-mode analysis have real material rather than aggregates, and totals them per subset (`error_summary`: by class, by line, the 15 fields that fail most).
- **The golden eval set is frozen and versioned separately, one per tenant, human-double-verified, and held constant across corpus versions** so model versions compare apples-to-apples over time (arch §8). Never train on it — assert no eval `source_id` appears in the tenant's corpus train or val split (`assert_eval_set_disjoint`).
- **Every gating metric must be emitted by `score_subset`.** `gating.GATING_METRICS` is checked with `require_all_measured=True`, so a metric the scorer never computes blocks *every* candidate, permanently, and the gate has no override by design. This is not hypothetical: `ece_confidence` and `confusable_misattribution_rate` were both gating metrics that `run_eval` never produced — `score_misattribution` had zero production callers and ECE had none at all — which made the gate unpassable under any input. The names must match exactly too: `list_field_recall` and `lob_detection_accuracy` are the gate's spellings, and emitting `list_recall` or `lob_accuracy` instead means the metric silently never arrives. **A test asserts the two sets agree** (`tests/test_module_seams.py`), because a hand-written metrics dict in a unit test proves nothing about what the scorer emits.
- ECE needs per-field confidences: `score_subset` reads them from each document's `metadata["field_confidence"]` and skips fields that carry none, rather than assuming a value. An unmeasured field is not evidence in either direction.
- A metric with nothing to measure is **absent, not zero** (`None` values are dropped from the subset report).
- Note: classifier accuracy uses the classifier from IMPL-11 once it exists; until then `run_eval` scores extraction with the true doc_type supplied, and classifier metrics fill in on a later pass. This keeps eval runnable before serving is built.

### 4. `evaluation/gating.py`
- `promotion_gate(candidate_metrics, current_metrics, *, per_document=None, floors=None, continued_from=None, cross_type_evidence=None, require_all_measured=True, fixes_defect=None, override=None, scope=None)` → `GateResult` (per-metric verdicts, failed gates, blocking reasons, tier). Three conditions, all of which must hold (arch v2.1 §15.5):
  1. **Absolute floors** (`PILOT_FLOORS`, with the scope's own overrides via `floors_for(scope)`) — applied to the first release too: `field_exact_match` / `field_normalized_match` 0.85, `list_field_recall` 0.75, `schema_validity_rate` 0.98, `doc_type_classifier_accuracy` 0.95, `lob_detection_accuracy` 0.85, `confusable_misattribution_rate` ≤ 0.05, `lossrun_totals_reconciliation_rate` 0.80, `table_f1` 0.88, `auto_accept_error_rate` ≤ 0.02, `worst_line_field_match` 0.85.
  2. **Non-inferiority** against the current release: the 95% CI lower bound of (candidate − current) from a paired bootstrap over documents (`per_document`) above −δ, with a per-metric δ (`NON_INFERIORITY_DELTA`, default 0.015); a point comparison against δ, recorded as weaker evidence, when no per-document scores are supplied.
  3. **Improvement**: at least one primary metric (`field_normalized_match`, `list_field_recall`) improves with its CI lower bound above zero, or the release names the defect it fixes (`fixes_defect`). The first release is exempt; without per-document scores the rule cannot be evaluated and is not enforced.
- A passing release is tiered by `release_tier`: **production** when `field_normalized_match` ≥ `PRODUCTION_FIELD_MATCH` (0.92), **interim** below that; a blocked release has no tier.

**Gating metric set** (`GATING_METRICS`): field exact/normalized match, list-field F1 (`field_f1_list_fields`), **list-field recall**, schema validity rate, ECE, OCR-arbitration accuracy, image-only accuracy, scanned accuracy, doc-type classifier accuracy, **LoB detection accuracy**, **confusable misattribution rate**, false-null rate, Loss Run totals reconciliation rate, `table_f1`, `auto_accept_error_rate`, `worst_line_field_match`.

- **Not applicable vs not measured.** `not_applicable_for(scope)` excuses what the scope's shape cannot produce (`common.scopes.structural_not_applicable`: no Loss Runs → no reconciliation or `table_f1`; no classify task → no classifier metric; policy-only → no LoB metric; no list fields → no list metrics) plus `CONDITIONAL_METRICS` (`lossrun_totals_reconciliation_rate`, `table_f1`, `page_selection_recall`, `auto_accept_error_rate`, `worst_line_field_match`, `scanned_accuracy`), which only exist when the eval set holds the relevant documents. A conditional metric the baseline measured is required. Anything else unmeasured blocks.
- `PENDING_GATING_METRICS` — `list_field_precision`, `page_selection_recall`, `hallucination_rate` — are reported, and not enforced until their producers are complete. A metric moves into `GATING_METRICS` in the same commit as the code that computes it.

`alias_accuracy` and `held_out_carrier_match` are **reported alongside but not gated** — see the note above.

A frozen document is an unseen carrier's when the split held its carrier out or the delivery marked it
(`label_metadata.json` `held_out_carrier`; `evaluation.freeze_eval_set.held_out_carrier`); the manifest lists
them as `held_out_source_ids`. `mark_held_out` (`freeze-eval-set --mark-held-out`) marks a set frozen before the
importer carried the flag, changing only that flag.

- **Additional gate for `--continue-from` Foundation runs (arch §12):** a minor incremental patch that continued from the previous Foundation checkpoint must additionally show **no regression on the *other* document types**, not just the type it was patching. The gate reads the manifest flag IMPL-06 sets and demands that cross-type evidence before passing; an empty entry for a type blocks.
- Writes the decision, tier, per-metric verdicts, metrics gated and `gated_against` into the candidate's `RunManifest` (IMPL-02, `apply_to_manifest`); a run recorded as failed is not marked evaluated.
- **No override flag.** A failed gate is a failed gate unless a written, attributed override is recorded with it (`GateOverrideRecord`: a named person, not a system identity; a reason of at least 20 characters; the gates it waives). It lifts only the named gates that actually failed, and they are recorded as waived, never as passed.

## Constraints
- Golden eval set is frozen + versioned separately; never train on it — assert it.
- Metrics deterministic + unit-testable on synthetic inputs.
- All model runs go through IMPL-07 — no bespoke inference here.
- `common/normalize.py` (through `values_agree`) is the single definition of "correct" shared with IMPL-12, and `common/grounding.py` the single definition of "printed".
- No PII in eval reports beyond what access control permits (master §8) — scrub or restrict.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Each metric returns correct values on hand-constructed synthetic cases.
- [ ] Normalized match handles dates, currency, and entity names via `common.normalize`, and numbers by the schema's declared type.
- [ ] `list_field_recall` flags a deliberately dropped claim row.
- [ ] `lob_detection_accuracy` / `lob_accuracy_by_value` report per-LoB-value accuracy and do not fold LoB into the aggregate field score.
- [ ] `alias_accuracy` slices field accuracy by observed surface label from `field_provenance`, and a field scoring well on its dominant alias but poorly on a rare one is visible in the report.
- [ ] `confusable_misattribution` scores a prediction that returns the certificate holder's value as `insured_name` as a failure, distinctly from an ordinary wrong value.
- [ ] A regression on confusable misattribution **alone** blocks promotion.
- [ ] `classify_error` separates perception errors from schema/reasoning errors on seeded inputs.
- [ ] `run_eval` produces a per-doc-type report with mode breakdowns (`image_only`, `scanned`, `noisy_ocr`, `long_policy`), using IMPL-07.
- [ ] An eval `source_id` appearing in a corpus train or val split causes a loud failure.
- [ ] `gating` blocks a candidate that fails **any single** gate metric's floor or non-inferiority test — including LoB accuracy alone.
- [ ] A `--continue-from` candidate without cross-type regression evidence is blocked.
- [ ] No code path waives a failed gate except a validated, attributed `GateOverrideRecord`, recorded as a waiver.
- [ ] Promotion decision + per-metric verdicts written to the RunManifest.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

**The frozen golden eval set** (`evaluation/freeze_eval_set.py`,
`python -m orchestration.run freeze-eval-set --corpus vN [--allow-small]`):
- copies one corpus build's **test split** — golden label, metadata, every page image and OCR page — into
  `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/{source_id}/`, with `golden-eval-set/{tenant}/manifest.json` (source corpus, commit,
  documents per type and per line of business, held-out carriers overall and per line). Each document's metadata
  records its carrier and whether that carrier was held out of train and val. `golden.json` is written last per
  document, so an interrupted freeze leaves nothing half-copied;
- **one set per tenant**: each tenant's releases are gated on its own documents, and source ids are numbered per
  tenant. A set still at the root (`golden-eval-set/`, frozen before sets were per tenant) is refused, never
  ignored (`refuse_unscoped_set`);
- **refused a second time** (the set is the yardstick every version is compared on), and **refused when
  any type would freeze fewer than 150 documents** (`MIN_FROZEN_DOCS_PER_TYPE`, arch §15.4) unless
  `--allow-small`: the set cannot grow once frozen, and at 150 documents a rate near 80% is known to about ±3
  points;
- after freezing, every corpus build excludes the frozen documents and their families and splits new
  documents into train/val only (IMPL-05).

**The golden eval** (`evaluation/golden_eval.py::evaluate_version`): refuses a partial set and asserts the set
is disjoint from the corpus; loads the tenant's frozen documents in the scope's doc types and lines
(`scope.covers_lob`), each label passed through label verification (a rule-added value no page prints is not
expected of the model, as in its training targets) and classic auto read as personal auto; runs every document
in **all three modes** (`GOLDEN_MODES`; `noisy_ocr_image` with the corpus build's own seeded corruption)
through `serving.pipeline.extract` with the release's bf16 calibrators; scores failures as `{}` with the error
recorded; and writes the report where the gate reads it. A common-model policy carries serving's own schema
verdict (`schema_valid`), which `schema_validity_rate` reads. The report adds `documents_failed`, `calibrated`
(a report made without calibrators is not reused once the release has them — `pipeline_dag.eval_report_metrics`),
`release_measurements` (P95 latency per adapter, peak GPU memory sampled through the eval; recorded, not gated)
and `windowing_ceiling` (what the oracle merge of the gold loses before any model runs; reported, never gated).
The gate stage produces the report when none exists.

**Validation-based scoring** (checkpoint selection, calibration): `evaluation/validation_generation.py`.
- `generate_validation(rows, model, *, adapter, constrain, batch_rows, stats)` generates every row once with the
  row's own prompt (assistant turn removed), constrained as serving is (`model.config.structured_outputs`) to
  the row's schema slice with `page_ref` bounded to the pages it shows (`with_page_bounds`), and capped at the
  row's task answer budget (`common.config.answer_cap`). Rows go `configured_batch_rows()` per `generate_batch`
  call — `generation.validation_batch_rows` (96) in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml`, else `BATCH_ROWS`
  (32) — **longest expected answer first within a call**, so the long tail runs beside the short answers. A
  call that fails together (one unreadable page image refuses the batch) is **retried in halves** until the bad
  row is alone. `stats` is filled with rows, rows generated, rows per call, calls, seconds and the slowest call.
- Answers are parsed as serving parses them (`collapse_spans(map_field_spans(...))`, `with_output_dates`). A
  row with nothing usable back is a `setup` failure; an answer that is not a JSON object is an `output` failure,
  scored as a wrong answer, with how generation ended recorded.
- `assert_generations_usable` refuses a pass in which more than **10%** of rows had a setup failure
  (`MAX_GENERATION_FAILURE_RATE`), or more than **50%** came back as unusable JSON (`MAX_UNUSABLE_OUTPUT_RATE`) —
  scoring a broken setup turned it into a checkpoint "chosen" at 0.0 and a calibrator fitted on nothing, while a
  checkpoint that loops on some documents has to lose, not be dropped.
- `score_generations` is the gate's `build_report` over the rows, window by window, plus the same metrics over
  **whole documents** (`document_*`): each policy's windows merged per reading mode by the serving merge
  (`merged_windows` → `serving.policy_merge.merge_policy_windows`), its window targets into the gold and its
  generations into the answer, so windowing loss is out of both and the score reads the model alone.
- Images are localised to the pod cache first (vLLM opens paths, the rows hold Blob keys).

**Checkpoint selection** (`evaluation/checkpoint_eval.py`, run by `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_checkpoint_eval`;
settings in the training config's `evaluation` block):
- **Candidates**: `discover_checkpoints` finds the latest run's `checkpoint-*` directories and the best-loss one
  (`trainer_state.json`'s `best_model_checkpoint`); when `eval_steps` exceeds `save_steps` only the checkpoints an
  evaluation fell on, and the last, are kept. `select_candidates` takes the last `TRAILING_CANDIDATES` (3) by
  step plus the best-loss one.
- **Ranking on the validation sample**: each candidate is applied as a LoRA per request on one base engine
  (`vllm_scorer`, `load_model("base")`) and scored on `validation_sample_rows` (400) rows of the scope's
  validation split — `evaluation/validation_sample.py`, stratified by line of business and reading mode (at
  least one row per group), deterministic by a hash of each row's identity, the whole split when it is no
  larger. `select_best` picks the best `field_normalized_match`, ties to the later step; a candidate whose
  scoring raises is skipped and recorded, not scored 0; loss/F1 disagreement and a margin under 0.005 are
  logged.
- **Whole documents decide** (`choose_on_documents`): the best `selection_document_finalists` (2) are scored on
  the full split's `.full` pass and judged on `document_field_normalized_match`, among those whose
  `document_hallucination_rate` and `document_false_null_rate` are within `selection_guard_margin` (0.02) of the
  best finalist's; ties go to the later step. `selection_document_modes` (`[ocr_plus_image]`) narrows that pass
  to those reading modes — **by mode, never by row** (`rows_in_modes`), so every document stays whole; the
  sample still ranks in all three modes, and the golden gate scores all three. A mode filter matching no row is
  refused before the engine loads (an unknown mode name by `_selection_settings`), as a `PipelineError` that is
  not retried. `.full` exists whenever the sample is not the whole split; without it the finalists are judged on
  the sample's own scores. `document_choice` records the finalists' metrics, the guard margin, the modes read
  (or `"sample (all modes)"` when no whole-document pass ran), those excluded by a guard, and whether the choice
  changed.
- **Near-tie**: with fewer than two finalists configured, `break_tie` re-scores the top two on `.full` when they
  are within `selection_tie_break_margin` (0.015) on the sample.
- **Several GPUs**: with more than one of the GPUs training used, `ParallelScorer` (`parallel_vllm_scorer`) deals
  the candidates to one worker process and vLLM engine per GPU (`python -m evaluation.checkpoint_score_worker`,
  `--full` and `--full-mode` for the whole-document pass), each building the same in-process scorer; the parent
  fetches the page images once, a failed worker fails only its candidates (with the end of its log), and each
  worker sends back what each generation pass cost.
- **Cost**: `generation_cost(scorer)` totals the passes (sample and `.full`: rows, calls, seconds) into the stage
  result; the selection report is written to `paths.checkpoint_selection(version, scope=...)`.

**Scoring corrections** (`evaluation/metrics/`):
- a value the golden label does not state is **scored wrong** (canonical targets are sparse, so a loop over
  golden paths alone never saw an invented field); empty extra values cost nothing;
- fields compare by their **declared type** (`values_agree`), so `957` and `957.0` agree and an identifier read
  as `1.0` is `1`;
- **tables are found at any depth** (`auto.vehicles`) and rows compared on **values**, not envelopes; rows are
  **paired by identity**, never by position (`rows_aligned_to`, and for common-model lines `aligned_for_scoring`:
  passes from the strictest identity down — a coverage by its printed name and what it applies to before its
  code — then shared values), so one omitted or reordered row does not make every later row wrong;
- **common-model lines** (`metrics/common_model.py`): both sides go through `comparable_view`, so structural ids
  are never scored and links are compared by what they name; values inside rows count; links, codes and
  `additional_fields` are scored apart; schema validity is asked of the answer as it was produced (or serving's
  verdict), not of the scored copy;
- **hallucination** looks for the value as printed (`raw`) on the pages it cites (`common.grounding`); printed
  only on another page is a wrong page, not an invention; a value too short to look for is left out; a right
  value is never a hallucination; only rows that were sent text count;
- **false nulls** pair an answer row nothing paired with the next unpaired label row (`fill_unpaired`), so a row
  the model wrote but misread is wrong values, never nulls; codes and links are neither false nulls nor
  hallucinations;
- **misattribution** is checked on flattened nested paths;
- **LoB** is not scored for a label that carries none (canonical policies), and the LoB metric is not
  applicable to a policy-only scope;
- field accuracy is **pooled per document reading** (source × mode), then averaged over documents — a
  60-window policy counts once, not 60 times; a window with nothing to score adds nothing;
- `worst_line_field_match` counts a common-model line only once it has `WORST_LINE_MIN_VALUES` (200) values
  scored;
- the classifier metric is not applicable while the corpus builds no classify rows (`common.tasks.CORPUS_TASKS`).

**Line-scoped gate**: for a scope narrowed by line of business (`personal_lines`), `evaluate_version` scores
only the frozen documents whose line is in scope. Double annotation of the frozen documents (arch §15.4)
remains a manual step before the first production gate.

**Freeze guard covers every corpus type**: a document type the corpus holds but the test split drew none
of counts as 0 and blocks the freeze (it used to pass unchecked and be missing from the frozen set for good).

**Leakage check reads train and val, not test** (`run_eval.corpus_source_ids`): the frozen set is compared
with the splits a model learns from and is selected on. The test split is what it is frozen FROM, so the
sequence *build v1 → freeze v1's test → gate the model trained on v1* is valid; counting test made it fail as
leakage for every frozen document. The family split keeps a frozen document's relatives out of train and val.

**Frozen = the manifest exists.** An interrupted freeze (documents copied, no manifest) is not frozen: it can be
resumed from the same corpus, a partial set from another corpus is refused with instructions, and the golden
eval refuses to gate on a partial set.

**Line detection is measured apart** (`evaluation/lob_detection_eval.py`, `scripts/measure_lob_detection.py`,
`metrics/lob_detection.py`): each frozen policy with one known line is read as serving would read it with no
line, and resolved by serving's own `_resolve_lob` at its thresholds; reported per line against
`DETECTION_FLOOR` (0.85, per line from `PER_LINE_MIN_SUPPORT` = 20 documents), with the confidently-wrong rate,
scanned/native and held-out/seen carriers apart, and a threshold sweep. Not a release gate (the detector is the
base model, which a release cannot move); `routing.detect_lob` stays off until it has been run.

### Real documents scored apart (2026-09-29)

The frozen set records each document's `synthetic` flag (`freeze_eval_set`), the golden eval carries it into
every scored document's metadata, and when the set mixes real and synthetic documents the eval report adds
`composition` (real and synthetic document counts) and `real_only.gate_metrics` — the same report over the real
documents alone (`golden_eval.real_only_section`). Reported beside the gate, never instead of it: the synthetic
twins of held-out sources are unseen layouts and a fair test, but their labels came from a generator, and the
gap between the two scores is how much it flatters the model. A set with no synthetic documents reports nothing
extra.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- §1: added typed comparison (`values_agree`, `scoring_kind`, numbers as numbers, identifiers) and the shared grounding matcher (`common/grounding.py`).
- §2 table: latency row now points at `release_measurements`; alias row corrected (`score_alias_accuracy` uncalled, seen/unseen split reported); `score_by_mode` replaced by `classify_error`; added page-ref, common-model, `table_f1`, auto-accept and precision/recall/error-summary rows; error classes include omission and invented.
- §3: report path is the scoped `summary.json` written by the golden eval; added the `held_out_carrier` subset, error totals, per-tenant frozen set, train/val-only leakage check.
- §4: replaced the stale "match or beat on every metric" gate with floors + paired-bootstrap non-inferiority + improvement, release tiers (0.92), the current gating/conditional/pending metric sets and the attributed override; checklist items corrected to match.
- Current implementation: frozen set is per tenant with carrier fields; golden eval details (label verification, served schema verdict, `calibrated`, release measurements, windowing ceiling); validation generation (96 rows per call, longest first, halves retry, stats, page bounds, answer caps, two failure rates, document metrics); new checkpoint-selection section (candidates, 400-row stratified sample, two finalists on whole documents with guards, `selection_document_modes`, `.full`, `document_choice`, `break_tie`, `ParallelScorer`, `generation_cost`); scoring corrections for typed comparison, identity pairing, common-model scoring, page-aware hallucinations, false nulls; line-detection measurement.
- Frozen held-out documents: the delivery's mark, `held_out_source_ids`, `mark_held_out` (commit f5668a4).

---

# IMPL-09_calibration

# SPEC 09 — Confidence Calibration

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-07, IMPL-08. Uses the inference core (IMPL-07) for logprobs/spans and the evaluation normalizer (IMPL-08) for the correctness signal. No forward dependency on serving/testing.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §5 (**confidence flow, calibration, list-field second signal**), §0b (LoB carries confidence too), §15 (ECE as a gating metric).

## Goal

Turn raw token logprobs into trustworthy per-field confidence via post-hoc calibration, plus the list-field completeness signal that logprobs are structurally blind to.

## The end-to-end flow being implemented (arch §5)

```
Generation ──> token logprobs ──> map to field spans ──> aggregate per field
                                                              │
                                                        raw confidence
                                                              │
                                              post-hoc calibration transform
                                              (temperature / isotonic, per type)
                                                              │
                                                     calibrated confidence
                                                       │              │
                                              scalar fields      list fields
                                                       │              │
                                              threshold check   + row-completeness
                                                       │              │
                                                       └──> review routing <──┘
```

(This is v1's flow. Under arch v2.1 the "aggregate per field" and "calibration transform" steps are a feature vector per field and a per-field-type calibrator, and the threshold is fitted per field type — see *Current implementation*.)

**Why calibration is not optional:** fine-tuned generative models — especially after LoRA fine-tuning on a narrow task — become **overconfident**. Correct and incorrect outputs carry similarly high token probabilities, because the model has learned to be fluent in the target format even when it is wrong about the content. Raw logprobs alone are not a usable confidence signal.

**Why not a separate verifier model:** another model to train, version, evaluate, and keep in sync with every Foundation/adapter version, plus doubled inference latency. It stays a documented escalation path **if** calibrated logprobs prove insufficient in evaluation (IMPL-08 ECE) — start with the cheap, well-understood approach.

## Deliverables

### 1. `calibration/logprob_confidence.py`
- Consumes field spans + logprobs from IMPL-07 `span_map` and aggregates to a raw per-field confidence.
- **Superseded as the confidence itself (arch v2.1 §5.1).** The minimum token probability is length-biased — the minimum of *n* draws falls as *n* grows, so a long correct policy number scores below a short wrong year. It is now **one feature among several** handed to a per-field-type calibrator; see *Current implementation* below. Serving reads this raw aggregate only when it holds no calibrator set, and then flags every field.
- Handles nested fields and list fields (per-value confidence within each row).
- **`line_of_business` gets confidence like any other field** (arch §0b) and is emitted in the same `{value, confidence}` shape.
- Costs nothing extra at training or inference time — the logprobs are already there.

### 2. `calibration/fit_calibration.py`
- On a **held-out labeled validation set — never training data** — compare raw confidence against actual field-level correctness (exact / normalized match via `common.normalize` from IMPL-08) and fit a transform:
  - **`temperature` scaling** (default: simple, one parameter, works well here)
  - **`isotonic` regression** (more flexible; handles non-monotonic miscalibration)
- Fit **per doc_type**, optionally per field-type. Fit for a specific model version — calibration is not transferable across versions.
- Persist to `calibration/{version}/{doc_type}.json` in Blob (`paths.calibration_params`, IMPL-02). This is a small lookup/transform applied at serving time **after** the model call, before the JSON is returned.
- Report the ECE before and after fitting, so a fit that fails to improve calibration is visible rather than assumed.

### 3. `calibration/apply_calibration.py`
- Load the fitted transform for a model version + doc_type; map raw → calibrated confidence at inference time. Used by serving (IMPL-11) and testing (IMPL-12).
- **Fails loudly when no calibration exists for a version/doc_type** rather than silently passing raw (overconfident) values through as if calibrated. A missing calibration is an operational error, not a fallback.
- Applies the review threshold (default 0.7, `DEFAULT_REVIEW_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` — arch §5) and emits `review_flags` for sub-threshold fields, which is the practical payoff of doing calibration properly. On serving's v1 fallback path no field is accepted on it (no measured error rate stands behind 0.7).

### 4. `calibration/list_completeness.py`
The second confidence signal that per-token logprobs structurally cannot provide (arch §5).

**The failure mode:** if the model extracts 6 of 8 claims, the 2 missing claims have **no generated tokens**, so there is no low probability to flag. Per-field confidence on the 6 extracted rows can all be high while the extraction is silently incomplete. This is a recall failure, and token confidence is blind to it.

Implement **two independent cross-checks**, either of which flags the list:
1. **Document-stated count** — a `total claims: N` style field extracted from the document.
2. **Structure-derived count** — the number of table rows MinerU detected on the relevant pages (recorded in `ocr_meta.json` by IMPL-03).

When the model's row count disagrees with either, **flag the whole list for review regardless of per-value confidence**.

**The flag format is a contract, so it is a constant.** Flags are `f"{field}{ROW_MISMATCH_SUFFIX}"` — `"claims:row_count_mismatch"` — and `is_row_completeness_flag()` is the only supported way to recognise one. Both are exported from this module and imported by `data_pipeline.labeling.active_learning`, which has to recognise them to honour the "row completeness outranks every confidence score" rule. The consumer previously matched hand-written prefixes (`list:`, `rows:`, `completeness:`) that this module has never emitted, so the override was dead code and an incomplete Loss Run with high per-value confidence was routed to a spot check.

**A stated count is read however the model spelled it.** `8`, `8.0` and `"8"` are the same assertion; accepting only `int` turned the whole stated-count cross-check off whenever the model emitted a JSON string.

**A flagged list is never fully confident.** `row_completeness_confidence` is scored against whichever signal disagrees *most*, not the first one available — preferring the stated count meant a list flagged solely by the structural check (document says 6, model returns 6, OCR saw 9) shipped confidence `1.0` on a list this function had just flagged. Optionally calibrate a separate `row_completeness_confidence` against ground-truth row counts on the validation set.

This matters most for **Loss Runs**, where a missed claim row is both easy to make and expensive to miss.

## Constraints
- Calibration fit ONLY on held-out data — assert the validation `source_id`s are disjoint from training.
- Params versioned per model version + doc_type (v2.1: per release + serving format); never reused across versions.
- Correctness signal reuses IMPL-08's definition of "correct" — `common.normalize` through `evaluation.metrics.field_accuracy.values_agree` (by the field's declared type) — the same definition as the promotion gate.
- Raw confidence is never returned to callers as if it were calibrated.
- No PII in logs (confidence records reference field paths, not field values).

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Raw per-field confidence extracted from a sample generation + logprobs (via IMPL-07 spans), including a `claims[i].amount` row field and `line_of_business`.
- [ ] Aggregation strategy is configurable and the choice is recorded.
- [ ] Temperature and isotonic both reduce ECE on a synthetic miscalibrated set; before/after ECE is reported.
- [ ] Fitted params persist/reload and apply deterministically.
- [ ] `apply_calibration` **raises** when no calibration exists for a version/doc_type.
- [ ] `list_completeness` flags a list when extracted rows < the document-stated count, and separately when < the MinerU-detected row count.
- [ ] A flagged list is routed to review even when every per-value confidence is high.
- [ ] Calibrated confidence is available with **no ground truth** — the inference-time case that makes production review routing possible.
- [ ] Fitting on data overlapping the training split fails the assertion.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

This spec's §1–§3 describe v1's single-number calibration. What runs now (arch v2.1 §5):

1. **Features per field** (`calibration/features.py::build_document_features`): min / mean / first-token
   logprob, token count, **OCR agreement**, **rule checks**, null flag, cross-mode agreement, field type. A
   feature that was not computed encodes as a neutral 0.5 plus a presence flag, never as 0.
   - *The logprob features read the printed value's own tokens*: a string's characters without its quotes
     (`inference_core.span_map`), and for a canonical envelope the span of `raw` — the reading of the page —
     with `parsed`'s standing in only when nothing was read (`common.canonical.collapse_spans`). `parsed` is
     the same value reformatted, written after `raw` and near-certain whether the reading was right or not.
   - *OCR agreement* compares the page text against the value **as printed** (a canonical envelope's
     `raw`, via `printed_view`), not the normalised `parsed` — a date rewritten to `MM/DD/YYYY` is not on the
     page.
   - *Grounded on the cited page*: given each page's text (`page_texts`, page number → text), agreement is
     `page_agreement` — `common.grounding.ground` against the pages the value's envelope cites: **1.0** printed
     on a cited page, **0.5** printed only on other pages (a wrong page, or another row's figure), **0.0**
     printed on no page, `None` when a cited page has no text (a scan OCR could not read), the value is too
     short to look for (under 3 characters), or there is no text (image_only). Serving and calibration
     fitting both pass each page's text. Without `page_texts`, `ocr_agreement` searches the whole shown text
     with the same matching — word boundaries, every word across line breaks, figures in any format — so "1"
     no longer agrees with every page holding a 1.
   - *Page text* has ONE definition for fitting and serving (`shown_ocr_text`): the OCR text of the pages
     the model was shown, page markers and the blank-page placeholder removed, `None` for `image_only`.
   - *Rule checks* read siblings beside the field (`policy.effective_date` vs `policy.expiration_date`),
     so they fire on canonical policies; totals against their rows; identifier shape.
   - *Field type* (`infer_field_type`) — one of `identifier`, `money`, `date`, `number`, `entity`, `address`,
     `enum`, `free_text`: the reviewed table `configs/field_types.yaml` first; then, **for a common-model
     document only**, the type its schema declares (`common_model_field_types` over `VALUE_TYPE_FIELD_TYPES`:
     `MoneyValue` money, `DateValue` date, year/number/percent number, NAIC and postal code identifier,
     state and the enum-like values enum) and an `additional_fields` value by what was parsed; then the name
     (`common.normalize.infer_field_kind`; an address by any path segment, `carrier.address.city`). The
     self-contained lines and ACORD keep the name-based types for the paths they share with the common model.
   - Features are built on the **value view** of the extraction (envelopes collapsed), keyed like serving.
     A field whose span could not be located is kept with `mapped=False` (no confidence, reviewed), never
     dropped.
2. **Per-field-type calibrators** (`calibration/feature_calibrator.py`): logistic regression per field type
   over those features (pure Python, L2-regularised), fitted on the **calibration half** of validation. A type
   with fewer than `MIN_INSTANCES` (300) labelled instances, or with only one outcome, gets no enforced
   calibrator, and every field of it routes to review. Isotonic above `ISOTONIC_THRESHOLD` (2,000) is
   recorded, not implemented.
3. **Risk-controlled thresholds** (`calibration/thresholds.py`): per field type, the lowest threshold on the
   0.50–0.99 grid whose auto-accepted error rate, as the **upper bound of a 95% binomial interval**, meets the
   target with at least `MIN_ACCEPTED_SAMPLE` (30) accepted fields, chosen on the **threshold half**.
   `DEFAULT_ERROR_TARGETS`: identifier, money, date, number 1%; enum 2%; entity, address 5%; free text has no
   target and is never auto-accepted. A type with no calibrator, or no threshold meeting the target, routes
   to review rather than carrying an unmeasured number.
4. **Per serving format**: each format gets its own calibrators from its own generations (quantization
   moves the logprob distribution), stored at
   `releases/{tenant}/{release}/calibration/{format}/calibrators.json` (`paths.release_calibrators`; the
   calibrators and the thresholds together).
5. **Loss Run reconciliation** (`calibration/reconciliation.py`) per policy period, alongside row
   completeness.

**Where the samples come from.** `orchestration.pipeline_dag.collect_calibration_samples` reads the scope's
validation split, localises its page images, and for each serving format in turn (`bf16` alone with
`--skip-quantize`) loads the staged merged or quantized model, generates **every validation row** with
`evaluation.validation_generation.generate_validation` (constrained as serving is, `page_ref` bounded to each
row's pages, the row's task answer cap, `validation_batch_rows` rows per call — IMPL-08), releases the engine
before the next format loads, and logs what the pass cost: rows, seconds, calls, rows per call and the slowest
call. A pass with too many failed rows is refused (`assert_generations_usable`); a format with no evidence gets
no entry. A resumed `package` finds the calibrators in Blob.

**How a sample is labelled** (`validation_generation.calibration_samples`), by the `val_half` the dataset build
stamped on each row (a row without one is left out, with a warning):
- a policy read in windows is fitted **as it is served**: its windows and their spans merged
  (`merged_windows`, the serving merge), held to the label conventions (`serving.conventions.conform`), then
  featured against each page's text and scored against the merged window targets — not one window at a time;
- every other row is fitted as generated; a row that failed to generate has no features;
- correctness is `values_agree` (IMPL-08's typed comparison), with the label's rows **paired by identity** to
  the answer's (`aligned_for_scoring` on a common-model line, `rows_aligned_to` otherwise), so one omitted or
  reordered row does not make every later row's correct values "wrong";
- a common-model line is featured without its ids, codes and links (`without_bare_values`) and typed as its
  schema declares — what serving calibrates (`serving.pipeline._feature_calibrated`).

**The calibrate stage** (`stage_calibrate`) fits calibrators on the calibration half and thresholds on the
threshold half per format, writes them to the release, and reports per format the unenforced types, the
guarantees, `auto_accept_error_rate`, and the **expected calibration error per field type and overall**
(`expected_calibration_error`, keyed `all`) — both measured on the threshold half, which the calibrators were
not fitted on.

**Thresholds** load back for serving (`ThresholdSet.from_dict`). An explicit target table that leaves a
field type out now means *no promise* — every field of that type is reviewed — instead of silently falling
back to the default target.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Noted that the flow diagram is v1's and that serving's v1 path (no calibrator set) flags every field.
- Corrected the v1 persistence path to `calibration/{version}/{doc_type}.json` (`paths.calibration_params`).
- Constraints: correctness is `values_agree` (typed comparison), and v2.1 params are per release and serving format.
- Features: logprob features read the value's own tokens (no quotes; `raw`'s span via `collapse_spans`); OCR agreement grounded on the cited pages (`page_agreement`: 1.0 / 0.5 / 0.0 / none) with the shared `common.grounding` matcher; field types include `number` and the common model's declared value types (common-model documents only); neutral encoding of missing features.
- Calibrators and thresholds: added `MIN_INSTANCES` 300 and the single-outcome rule, the threshold grid, binomial upper bound, `MIN_ACCEPTED_SAMPLE` 30 and the per-type targets; the calibrators file name.
- Samples: described `collect_calibration_samples` (every val row per format, merged model, engine released between formats, rows/seconds/calls logged) and `calibration_samples` (windowed policies merged and conformed as served, rows paired by identity, typed correctness, common-model fields as serving calibrates them).
- Calibrate stage: reports ECE per field type and overall on the threshold half.

# IMPL-10_postprocessing_merge_quantize

# SPEC 10 — Postprocessing (Merge + Serving-Format Quantization)

> Aligned with the code at commit f5668a4 (2026-10-13).

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-02, IMPL-06.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.3.docx` §13 steps 7–8, §13a (**vLLM-native serving formats**), §13b (quantization quality thresholds, absolute percentage points), §18 (artifact paths).

## Goal

Fold the trained adapter into the base model, and produce the quantized formats **the serving endpoint can actually load**. Quality thresholds are specified here and enforced before any quantized format is served.

## What changed from v1, and why

v1 produced `fp16` + `q5_k_m` GGUF files every cycle. GGUF is **llama.cpp's** format and the endpoint runs **vLLM**, so the pipeline spent conversion and eval compute on an artifact nothing could deploy — and there was no small format the server *could* open, leaving bf16 or nothing.

v1's own module docstring had the right of it: *"GGUF is the portable/edge path and vLLM on the merged model is the primary one."* It never followed through, and left `DEFAULT_FORMATS = ("fp16", "q5_k_m")` making a GGUF the per-cycle deliverable.

## 1. `training/merge.py`

- Loads the base in **bf16** and folds in **one** adapter via PEFT `merge_and_unload()` → one standalone bf16 model.
- **One adapter, one merge.** v1 applied a Foundation LoRA and then a per-type LoRA on top, producing a model per document type — and the order mattered, because reversing it produced a different model from the one that was evaluated. That whole ordering problem is gone with the topology that created it (arch §4.1).
- A graduated per-type adapter (§4.2) is **never merged**. It is applied at serving time on top of these merged weights, one LoRA per request — the only shape vLLM can serve.
- Folds in the **checkpoint the §11.2 selector chose**, recorded on the plan (`MergePlan.selected_checkpoint`). `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge` reads it through `selected_checkpoint(ctx)`: this process's `checkpoint_eval` result, else the choice recorded in Blob at `eval-reports/v{n}/[scope/{scope}/]checkpoint_selection.json`. The merged weights do not say which checkpoint they came from, and once the staging volume is reclaimed nothing else does either.
- `dtype` defaults to **bf16, not fp16**: the adapter trained in bf16 against a bf16 base, and FP8 is quantized from this artifact. Merging to fp16 would insert a precision change between training and every serving format for no reason.
- Writes to the **staging volume**, per scope (`plan_merge(scope=)`): `staging/merged-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/`. The merged model is ~16 GB and quantization also runs on RunPod; pushing it to Azure and pulling it back is a 32 GB round trip for nothing (master §12a).
- **Runs on the GPU** into the **pinned local base** (see *Current implementation*); written to `<output>.partial` and renamed into place only once `config.json` and the safetensors exist.

## 2. `postprocessing/quantize.py` — serving formats

| Format | Tool | Hardware | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **bf16 merged** | PEFT merge | Any | Reference, and cycle 1's serving format |
| **FP8 (W8A8)** | llm-compressor | Native FP8 on Ada/Hopper (L4, L40S, H100, H200); weight-only on A100 | Default serving format from cycle 2 |
| **AWQ INT4 (W4A16)** | llm-compressor | Modern NVIDIA | VRAM-constrained serving only, if it meets threshold |

```bash
# fp8 is refused until it is verified (below); --formats bf16 is the default
python -m orchestration.run package --version v2 --release-id release-2026.11.1 --formats bf16 fp8
```

**bf16 is never re-exported.** It *is* the merged model; a copy would be a second 16 GB artifact identical to the first.

**With `--formats bf16` — the default (`defaults.formats` in `orchestration/config/pipeline.yaml`) — quantize is a no-op.** `_is_quantized` reports the stage complete as soon as merge is, and `quantize()` returns the merged model as the bf16 entry without exporting anything.

**FP8 is refused until verified.** `plan_quantization` defaults to bf16 alone and `quantize()` raises on `fp8` unless `fp8_verified=True` (`StageContext.fp8_verified`), which is to be set once Phase 0 spike item 9 has verified the export; no CLI flag sets it. An unverified serving format is a deployment that fails at load — or worse, one that loads and reads badly.

**The vision path is never quantized.** `NEVER_QUANTIZED = visual.*, mergers, aligners, lm_head`, passed to llm-compressor as its ignore list (arch §13a). Compressing it produces a model that loads cleanly and reads pages badly — very hard to notice, because it still emits well-formed JSON.

**Every serving format gets its own calibrator fit and its own gate run.** Quantization degrades exactly what was fine-tuned in, so an FP8 release inherits nothing from bf16's result.

## 2a. GGUF — the edge export, on request only

`plan_gguf_export` / `export_gguf` produce a GGUF for an **offline or edge** deployment. Not per-cycle, not on the serving path, and **validated separately in llama.cpp** against the §13b AWQ INT4 column — the serving gate does not cover it, because the serving endpoint never loads one.

The v1 mmproj guard is carried over **unchanged**: Qwen3-VL is multimodal, so a GGUF needs the quantized LLM **plus a separate `mmproj`** file for the vision encoder and projector. Exporting without a verified one produces a model that loads and cannot see, which fails silently on every image-only document.

Serving and edge artifacts are stored under **different runtime prefixes** — `quantized-models/{scope}/v{n}/vllm/{format}/` and `.../gguf/{format}/`. They are loaded by different programs, and one prefix invites deploying the wrong one.

## 3. `postprocessing/quant_thresholds.py`

**Absolute percentage points against bf16, per field class** (arch §13b):

| Metric | FP8 max drop | AWQ INT4 max drop |
|---|---|---|
| Exact match — identifiers, money, dates | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Match — names, addresses | 1.0 pp | 2.0 pp |
| List-field row recall | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Confusable misattribution (increase) | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| ECE (increase) | 0.01 | 0.02 |
| Schema validity (constrained) | 100% | 100% |

`field_normalized_match` is judged on the **names, addresses** row and `field_exact_match` on the **identifiers, money, dates** row, each against its own allowance.

Three things about this table are deliberate:

- **Absolute, not relative.** v1 allowed a 2% *relative* field-F1 drop, which is 1.9 pp at F1 0.95 and 1.4 pp at 0.70 — so the rule got **stricter as the model got worse**, which is backwards. The same number should mean the same thing whatever the baseline.
- **Per field class.** A wrong policy number is a wrong extraction; a slightly-off entity name is usually still matchable. One allowance would let the forgiving class lend its slack to the unforgiving one.
- **Schema validity is a floor, not a margin.** Structured decoding guarantees it (§13), so anything below 100% means the guarantee is not working — an error, not a degraded result.

The reference is **bf16**. v1 measured every format against fp16, a GGUF format, so the reference itself was an artifact the endpoint could not load.

A format exceeding **any** threshold is not served. Asking the serving gate about a GGUF format raises and says where it *is* validated, rather than reading as "unsupported".

## 4. `postprocessing/validate_quant.py`

- Judges each produced format's metrics — from the extraction routine (IMPL-12) against the **frozen golden eval set** — against the table above. It scores nothing itself: the metrics are supplied (`StageContext.quant_metrics`), and with none supplied the quantize stage logs a warning instead of judging. No CLI path supplies them yet; a bf16-only cycle has nothing to compare, because bf16 *is* the reference.
- Also checks **row recall** and **confusable misattribution** on their own margins: a quantized model that keeps its field accuracy while dropping claim rows has lost exactly what a Loss Run is for, and an aggregate field score would not show it.
- Writes `quant_threshold_results` into the quantize stage result (`ValidationReport.manifest_entry()`) and **refuses promotion of an over-threshold format** (`assert_servable`) — no override flag.

## Constraints

- Merged models never overwrite adapters (separate Blob paths).
- Every **served** quantized format passes threshold validation first — no exceptions, no override.
- The bf16 reference is always produced: every §13b threshold is a margin against it, so a run producing only a quantized format has nothing to measure its drop from.
- Record all produced formats in the manifest.
- Never emit a GGUF whose multimodal projector support is unverified.

## Acceptance checklist

- [ ] `merge` produces one loadable bf16 model from one adapter, folding in the selected checkpoint.
- [ ] `plan_quantization` defaults to **bf16 alone**, and refuses a GGUF format with an error naming the runtime mismatch.
- [ ] `quantize` refuses FP8 until `fp8_verified`, and never re-exports bf16.
- [ ] With `--formats bf16`, the quantize stage is complete once merge is and exports nothing.
- [ ] A serving plan without the bf16 reference is refused outright.
- [ ] `NEVER_QUANTIZED` covers the vision tower, every merger and `lm_head`.
- [ ] `export_gguf` refuses an unverified mmproj, and is reachable only on request.
- [ ] Serving and GGUF artifacts land under different runtime prefixes.
- [ ] `quant_thresholds` returns pass/fail correctly **at each format's exact boundary** — a format landing precisely on its allowance passes.
- [ ] Margins behave identically at F1 0.95 and 0.70, which is what "absolute" means.
- [ ] `validate_quant` refuses an over-threshold format **with no override path**, and refuses validation without the bf16 reference.
- [ ] A format that was not measured does not pass.
- [ ] **`assert_servable` refuses a serving format that produced no verdict at all.** `validate_quant` only returns a result for formats it was given metrics for, so a format nobody scored appears in neither `servable_formats` nor `blocked_formats`. Checking `blocked_formats` alone let it sail through to push with zero measurements — the precise inverse of the rule this module exists to enforce.
- [ ] The threshold gate sits inside `package`, between quantize and calibrate.
- [ ] `package` publishes an adapter as its `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors` only, never a checkpoint's resume state.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-13)

**Merge** (`training/merge.py`, run by `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge`), implemented:
- refuses an adapter directory without `adapter_config.json` (a run's output root is not an adapter), and
  a base revision still `PIN_ME` when the base would come from the Hub;
- loads the base with `AutoModelForImageTextToText` in the merge dtype on **`device_map="cuda"`** — GPU
  only (`require_cuda`); every engine is released before merge runs — from the pod's local copy
  (`model.local_dir`) when present, applies the adapter with PEFT, `merge_and_unload()`, saves safetensors
  (5 GB shards) **and the processor** (vLLM reads the chat template and image-processor config from the
  model directory), then renames `.partial` into place;
- merges the checkpoint `checkpoint_eval` selected (`selected_checkpoint`), read from Blob on a resumed run;
  with nothing selected a real run refuses with a `PipelineError` rather than merging the output root. The
  same reader decides which adapter `package` publishes, so the adapter published is the one merged;
- `finetune` merges in bf16; `all` takes `--dtype` (default `bf16`, `defaults.dtype`).

**Quantization plan is scope-aware** (`plan_quantization(..., scope=)`): a scoped run quantizes its own
merged model into its own paths. With `--formats bf16` the stage does nothing. The llm-compressor export
(`_export`) refuses without CUDA and is not wired yet — it raises `NotImplementedError` naming the recipe and
the ignore list — and needs its **own environment** (`requirements-quantize.txt`; PyPI name `llmcompressor`):
it requires `datasets>=4` where ms-swift 3 needs `<4`, and a transformers range vLLM 0.11 excludes.

**Package uploads real weights** (`orchestration.pipeline_dag._push_weights`, through
`artifact_registry/transfer.py`), after the release bundle and its routing check are built (IMPL-13 §4):
- the selected adapter, by `push_scoped_adapter`, to `adapters/foundation/v{n}/` (unified) or
  `adapters/scope/{scope}/v{n}/` — **only** `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors`
  (`ADAPTER_FILES`), never the checkpoint's optimizer and scheduler state (~1.5 GB of a rank-64 checkpoint).
  `finetune --push-adapters` publishes the same two files;
- the merged model to `merged-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/`;
- every non-bf16 format to `quantized-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/vllm/{format}/`.

The adapter and merged-model pushes skip every file whose bytes Blob already holds, compared by the MD5
recorded with each upload (`BlobClient.upload_dir(..., skip_identical=True)`), so re-packaging a release
sends nothing; a blob that records no MD5 is sent again. A dry run still records where each would go. The run
manifest's `quantized_model` points at the first quantized format — never bf16, which is not an export.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- §1: the merge reads the selection through `selected_checkpoint` (process result, else `checkpoint_selection.json` in Blob); the staging output path is per scope.
- §2: `--formats bf16` (the default) makes quantize a no-op; `fp8_verified` has no CLI setter; the FP8 example notes it is refused until verified.
- §4: `validate_quant` judges supplied metrics only; no CLI supplies them yet, so the stage warns.
- *Current implementation*: the published adapter is `adapter_config.json` + `adapter_model.safetensors` only (also for `--push-adapters`); adapter and merged-model pushes skip identical files by MD5; the bundle and routing check come before any push; the llm-compressor export is not wired.
- Acceptance checklist: added the bf16 no-op and the two-file adapter publish.
